### §1.1 What the dialect is   [DLT-1.1]

````text
DLT-1.1-P1
  <1>1 [def] The dialect is the contract between **front ends** that emit LLVM IR containing calls to `__bennett_*` marker functions and the **plugin** that, at the final link of a whole program, recognises those calls, analyses and normalises the code they reach, and maps it onto the ISA (ISA §1.1).   deps: ISA-1.1
  <1>2 [req] It fixes:
DLT-1.1-P2
  <1>1 [unc] the marker ABI, operand encodings and C header (§3);   deps: DLT-3
DLT-1.1-P3
  <1>1 [unc] the processing order, execution tiers, polyvariant variants and function addresses (§4);   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (function address), DLT-4
DLT-1.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] the device analysis: points-to, value sets and every question answered from them (§5);   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-5
DLT-1.1-P5
  <1>1 [unc] the handle convention, provenance, comparison and collections of handles (§6);   deps: DLT-6
DLT-1.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] regions: kinds, candidate sets, dispatch, identity, well-formedness (§7);   deps: DLT-7
DLT-1.1-P7
  <1>1 [req] the oracle interface: the descriptor, parameter and device bindings, entry state, wide outputs and in-place updates (§8);   deps: DLT-8
DLT-1.1-P8
  <1>1 [unc] budgets (§9);   deps: DLT-9
DLT-1.1-P9
  <1>1 [unc] taint-driven lifting of classical code under control (§10);   deps: DLT-10
DLT-1.1-P10
  <1>1 [unc] effects and device models per tier (§11);   deps: DLT-11
DLT-1.1-P11
  <1>1 [req] the materialised normalisation and the residual-poison validator (§12), floating point (§13), calls and control transfer (§14), inline asm and target intrinsics (§15);   deps: DLT-12.2-P34.S1 (floating point), DLT-12, DLT-13, DLT-14, DLT-15
DLT-1.1-P12
  <1>1 [req] the lifting map from materialised LLVM to ISA objects (§16);   deps: DLT-16
DLT-1.1-P13
  <1>1 [unc] the device classical memory model, CC and R lowering rules and fault outcomes (§17);   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-17
DLT-1.1-P14
  <1>1 [unc] a reference semantics from marker programs to the ISA formal model (§18);   deps: DLT-18
DLT-1.1-P15
  <1>1 [unc] the driver's modes and plugin-invocation interface (§19);   deps: DLT-19
DLT-1.1-P16
  <1>1 [unc] the platform profile `riscv64-bennett-virt` (§20);   deps: DLT-20
DLT-1.1-P17
  <1>1 [unc] the legality corpus and conformance suites (§21); diagnostics (§22); versioning (§23); deferred mechanics (§24); blockers and ISA items (§25).   deps: DLT-21, DLT-22, DLT-23, DLT-24, DLT-25
DLT-1.1-P18
  <1>1 [req] It does not fix the plugin's optimisation pipeline after normalisation, the plugin's device objects beyond the constraints of §18.7, or any format the ISA owns.   deps: DLT-18.7
````

### §1.2 Conformance classes   [DLT-1.2]

````text
DLT-1.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] Producer | front ends (clang, rustc, Bennett.jl, importers) | emit modules that satisfy §3 and the outlining conventions of §7.6; never emit a reserved name of §3.1 other than a marker   deps: DLT-1.1-P1.S1 (front ends), DLT-3, DLT-7.6, DLT-3.1
DLT-1.2-P2
  <1>1 [req] Plugin | `bennett-plugin` and competitors | the processing order of §4.1; give every module the verdict the rules give it, with the diagnostics of §22; produce ISA objects whose execution equals the reference semantics (§18.7); suites DA–DX (§21.3)   deps: DLT-21.3-P1.S1 (DA), DLT-21.3-P14.S1 (DX), DLT-4.1, DLT-22, DLT-18.7, DLT-21.3
DLT-1.2-P3
  <1>1 [req] Driver | `bennett-driver` | §19; links `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` bitcode of the link's LLVM major (§19.1); builds the comparison link and the native comparison binary (§17.7); emits blocker reports (§25)   deps: DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (comparison link), DLT-17.7-P1.S1 (native comparison binary), DLT-19, DLT-19.1, DLT-17.7, DLT-25
DLT-1.2-P4
  <1>1 [req] Validator | the plugin's validator and `bennett-conformance` | the residual-poison validator (§12.4) and the reference recogniser (§3, §5, §6, §7), implemented independently; suites DA–DN   deps: DLT-21.3-P1.S1 (DA), DLT-21.3-P5.S1 (DN), DLT-12.4, DLT-3, DLT-5, DLT-6, DLT-7
DLT-1.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] Runtime | `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` | define every entry point that §11, §13, §14, §17 and §20 name (prefixes `__bennett_rt_`, `__bennett_dev_`, `__bennett_sf_`, §3.1) with the semantics stated there, as ordinary bitcode compiled by the same pipeline; suites DR and DF   deps: DLT-21.3-P11.S1 (DR), DLT-11, DLT-13, DLT-14, DLT-17, DLT-20, DLT-3.1
DLT-1.2-P6
  <1>1 [req] Platform | a platform profile of §20 | a board model plus shims satisfying §20; suite DP   deps: DLT-21.3-P13.S1 (DP), DLT-20
DLT-1.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] `scripts/dialect_check.py` in this repository is the seed of the pre-flight checker (§1.5).   deps: DLT-1.5
  <1>2 [req] It never bounds this document: an example it cannot yet execute fails the check (rule P-2, §1.5).   deps: DLT-1.5-P4.S1 (P-2), DLT-1.5
````

### §1.3 Relation to the ISA specification   [DLT-1.3]

````text
DLT-1.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] The ISA is settled; this document never redefines it.
  <1>2 [req] Every ISA notion used here (tiers, wires, `qreg` handles, oracles, actions, permutation actions, lprocs, F_B, budgets, tokens, fault codes, the reference interpreter, interface and closure hashes, layouts, the launch ABI) has exactly the meaning ISA §2–§17 give it.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-2, ISA-3, ISA-4, ISA-5, ISA-6, ISA-7, ISA-8, ISA-9, ISA-10, ISA-11, ISA-12, ISA-13, ISA-14, ISA-15, ISA-16, ISA-17
  <1>3 [def] Where the dialect needs a construct ISA 0.8 lacks, this document states (a) the full dialect mechanism, (b) its realisation on ISA 0.8 where one exists, which may be a longer construction (an **efficiency item**, §25.3), and (c) otherwise a **blocker** (§25.1) naming the exact ISA change.   deps: DLT-25.3, DLT-25.1
    <2>1 [def] (a) the full dialect mechanism
    <2>2 [req] (b) its realisation on ISA 0.8 where one exists, which may be a longer construction (an **efficiency item**, §25.3)   deps: DLT-25.3
    <2>3 [def] (c) otherwise a **blocker** (§25.1) naming the exact ISA change.   deps: DLT-25.1
  <1>4 [req] The dialect never shrinks to fit the ISA and never extends the ISA unilaterally.
````

### §1.4 Departures from the north star   [DLT-1.4]

````text
DLT-1.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] Only these remain.
  <1>2 [req] Every row of 0.1 §1.4 that narrowed scope (1.4-d, 1.4-e, 1.4-f) is deleted; its replacement is §10, §9 and §8.7 respectively.   deps: DLT-10, DLT-9, DLT-8.7
DLT-1.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] §3.1 handle row: a handle through an `inttoptr`/`ptrtoint` round trip is rejected | Provenance flows through bitwise copies (§6.2) | Spike F1; maintainer decision 2; north-star amendment A2 (drafted with A1, bd `-25h`)   deps: DLT-A-P3.S1 (A2), DLT-A-P2.S1 (A1), DLT-6.2-P1.S2 (bitwise copies), DLT-3.1, DLT-6.2
DLT-1.4-P3
  <1>1 [req] §3.1: regions entered as `__bennett_ctrl(q, @f, …)` with a constant `@f` | Region operands are classical data dispatched over a candidate set bounded by the program (§7.2) | Spike F3; maintainer decision 3   deps: DLT-2-P5.S2 (Region operand), DLT-3.1, DLT-7.2
DLT-1.4-P4
  <1>1 [req] Region named by symbol | Region identity is by content (§7.3) | Spike F2; maintainer decision 1   deps: DLT-7.3
DLT-1.4-P5
  <1>1 [req] §3.1 handle row is silent on comparison | `icmp eq/ne` of handles is identity (§6.3) | Maintainer decision 4 (2026-10-29; legality findings §3); a superset   deps: DLT-3.1, DLT-6.3
DLT-1.4-P6
  <1>1 [req] §3.1, §4.2: a `qstore` into a register not known to be clean is a compile-time error | `qstore` is the ISA's XOR store; the compile-time rule is an optional warning `BD-W-QSTORE-UNCLEAN` (§6.7) | A register-granularity rule forbids the uncomputing second store; a superset of the north star (audit 1.4-g, D7)   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-6.7-P7.S1 (`BD-W-QSTORE-UNCLEAN`), DLT-3.1, DLT-4.2, DLT-6.7
DLT-1.4-P7
  <1>1 [req] §3.2: normalisation of "the outlined region" | Normalisation applies to every device variant (§12.1) | Device CC code is lowered to ISA CC, whose semantics differs from LLVM's poison semantics (ISA §4.1); a superset (audit 1.4-h)   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P15.S1 (lowered), DLT-3.2, DLT-12.1, ISA-4.1
````

### §1.5 Notation and checked examples   [DLT-1.5]

````text
DLT-1.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] LLVM IR is LLVM's textual form with opaque pointers, as accepted by LLVM 18, 20 and 23.
  <1>2 [req] `P(a)` is the representation width of an integral pointer in address space a from DataLayout, falling back to the default pointer entry (64 with no entry).
  <1>3 [req] `pw=P(0)` is the ISA address width, not the width of every LLVM pointer.
  <1>4 [req] `I(a)` is its independently specified pointer-index width (default P(a)).
  <1>5 [req] Numbers are decimal unless written `0x…`.

DLT-1.5-P4
  <1>1 [def] **P-2.** An example that the checker cannot yet execute **fails** the check with `checker-unimplemented: <capability>`.
  <1>2 [req] It is never skipped, and this document never removes or weakens an example to make the checker pass.
  <1>3 [req] The freeze gate is an all-green check.
DLT-1.5-P5
  <1>1 [def] **P-3.** Every executed result comes from an independent executor: LLVM tools, natively built binaries, or the Rust ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`.
  <1>2 [req] Python computes only *expected* artefacts (recogniser verdicts, normaliser output, map output, TDL events), and is never both producer and judge of one example.
DLT-1.5-P6
  <1>1 [def] Every fenced block whose info string starts with `llvm`, `isa`, `c`, `rust`, `julia`, `json` or `text` followed by a tag below is a **checked example**.
  <1>2 [req] Its attributes are `key=value` pairs.
  <1>3 [req] The last column names the checker capability (Appendix B) and, where the ISA reference interpreter is the executor, the `bennett-isa-ref` command (`check`, `hash`, `run-oracle`, `step-check` exist; `run-cc`, `reverse`, `run-action`, `run-program` are requested, bd `b-isa-spec-xam`).
DLT-1.5-P7
  <1>1 [req] `llvm markers-ir` | the marker list of §3.2; compared with the C view | `parse`, `header`   deps: DLT-3.2
DLT-1.5-P8
  <1>1 [req] `c markers-c` | the prototypes of the C header; byte-equal to `spec/bennett_markers.h` between its BEGIN/END PROTOTYPES lines | `header`
DLT-1.5-P9
  <1>1 [req] `llvm example=ID verdict=V` | a module; it parses and verifies on LLVM 18, 20 and 23 and the reference recogniser gives verdict `V` (`accept`, `reject:<code>` or `blocked:<code>`, §1.6).   deps: DLT-1.6
  <1>2 [req] Optional attributes: `candidates=f,g` (the resolved candidate set of the first region operand, §7.2), `callees=f,g` (the candidate set of the first indirect call, §14.2), `shapes=N` (the number of compiled oracle shapes, §8.4), `mode=validate` (validator only, §12.4), `rmode=@f` (compiled in the whole-program R mode with entry f, §19.2) | `parse`, `recognise`   deps: DLT-7.2, DLT-14.2, DLT-8.4, DLT-12.4, DLT-19.2
DLT-1.5-P10
  <1>1 [req] `llvm normalise-in=ID` / `llvm normalise-out=ID` | the normaliser (§12) applied to the first gives the second, lines compared after collapsing runs of blanks; both verify on the three majors (a source-only `majors=23` tag limits a form introduced in LLVM 23); the second passes the validator | `parse`, `normalise`   deps: DLT-12
DLT-1.5-P11
  <1>1 [req] `text native=ID` | inputs and outputs of the native differential of a normalisation pair or a lifted example: both modules (or the C source and the module) built natively with the seeds of `spec/seeds/` (§21.4), run on the listed inputs, outputs equal to the listed values | `native`   deps: DLT-21.4
DLT-1.5-P12
  <1>1 [req] `llvm lift=ID` | a materialised module; attribute `region=@f` names the region function and `desc=` the binding shape: the parameters' bindings in order (`IN(n,zext)`, `IN(n,sext)`, `ARG(W)`, `MEM_IN(A)`, `OUT(size)`), then `;dev=` device bindings (`ENTROPY(A)`, …) and `;tgt=` n(tgt) | `parse`, `map`   deps: DLT-12.1-P1.S6 (materialised module)
DLT-1.5-P13
  <1>1 [req] `text linkmap=ID` | the comparison-link facts the map of `lift=ID` reads (§19.5): `null_size`, `heap_base`, `heap_limit_max`, the globals with their bases, the A(F) of address-taken functions, and the budget table's largest `heap` and `stack` | `map`   deps: DLT-9.2-P2.S1 (budget table), DLT-19.5
DLT-1.5-P14
  <1>1 [req] `isa object=ID` | the reference object (§16.2) that the map produces from `lift=ID`, as complete ISA text; byte-equal to the map output; `bennett-isa-ref check` accepts it | `map`, `check`   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), DLT-16.2
DLT-1.5-P15
  <1>1 [req] `text hash=ID` | the interface hashes of the object's symbols (`bennett-isa-ref hash`) | `hash`
DLT-1.5-P16
  <1>1 [req] `text run=ID` | lines `input=… [mem=name:hex] [arg=…] budget=s,h,st,d -> code=C payload=P steps=N`; each line is the output of `bennett-isa-ref run-oracle <object> ref` with those options | `run-oracle`
DLT-1.5-P17
  <1>1 [req] `text step-check=ID` | a prefix-reversal claim; the output of `bennett-isa-ref step-check <object> ref --exhaustive` with the stated options | `step-check`
DLT-1.5-P18
  <1>1 [req] `llvm semantics=ID corpus=NAME` | a marker program; byte-equal to `corpus/legality/cases/NAME.ll`; run through the trace runtime and the reference semantics (§18) | `trace`, `run-action`   deps: DLT-18
DLT-1.5-P19
  <1>1 [req] `text events=ID` / `text state=ID` | the event list (§18.2) of a marker program on an input, and the state vector before event `at` | `trace`, `run-action`   deps: DLT-18.2
DLT-1.5-P20
  <1>1 [req] `text tdl=ID` | the TDL partition (§10, TDL-3) and generated event list of an action region invocation | `tdl`, `run-action`   deps: DLT-10-P14.S1 (TDL-3), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), DLT-10
DLT-1.5-P21
  <1>1 [req] `text cc-run=ID` | a CC or R run: results, fault records, tape, and the state after `bennett_reverse` | `run-cc`, `reverse`   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
DLT-1.5-P22
  <1>1 [req] `text blocker=ID` | the blocker report (§25.1) the plugin emits for a `blocked:` example | `recognise`   deps: DLT-25.1
DLT-1.5-P23
  <1>1 [req] `json budget-table=ID` | a budget table (§9.2); parsed and resolved against the example it names | `driver`   deps: DLT-9.2-P2.S1 (budget table), DLT-9.2
DLT-1.5-P24
  <1>1 [req] `c example=ID`, `rust example=ID`, `julia example=ID` | a source file compiled by the toolchains of `corpus/legality/toolchains.toml` (`clang -O2 -flto` on 18, 20, 23, with `-I spec`; with `rmode=` against the `bennett-runtimes` musl sysroot, for which the checker's seeds stand in; `rustc --emit=llvm-bc`; Julia `code_llvm`) and then `lto<O3>`; the result is recognised with verdict `verdict=V` | `frontend`, `recognise`
DLT-1.5-P25
  <1>1 [req] A module example that calls a marker or a primitive without declaring it implicitly contains its declaration (§3.2, §3.1).   deps: DLT-3.2, DLT-3.1
  <1>2 [def] **Section references**: `§n` is a section of this document, `ISA §n` of the ISA specification, `NS §n` of `Bennett-NorthStar.md`; a reference that continues a list (`ISA §4.7, §5.3`) names the same document as the reference before it.   deps: ISA-4.7, ISA-5.3
  <1>3 [req] Fenced blocks without a tag are illustrations and are non-normative; an `llvm` or `isa` block without a tag is an error of this document.
````

### §1.6 Diagnostics and verdicts   [DLT-1.6]

````text
DLT-1.6-P1
  <1>1 [req] A module's **verdict** is one of:
DLT-1.6-P2
  <1>1 [req] `accept`: the plugin produces its objects;
DLT-1.6-P3
  <1>1 [unc] `reject:<code>`: a compile-time rejection of §22.1.   deps: DLT-22.1
  <1>2 [req] The plugin MUST fail the compilation (nonzero exit, no output object) and report at least the first diagnostic in the processing order of §4.1;   deps: DLT-4.1
DLT-1.6-P4
  <1>1 [req] `blocked:<code>`: the module is accepted by the dialect, but at least one program point needs a capability that ISA 0.8 cannot express (§25.1).   deps: DLT-25.1
  <1>2 [req] The plugin MUST produce its objects with every such point lowered to a **blocker trap** (§25.1: a run-time fault on exactly the executions that reach the point), MUST emit the blocker report of §25.1 and MUST exit with the distinct status 3.   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (lowered), DLT-25.1
  <1>3 [req] The code is the first blocker report in processing order.
DLT-1.6-P5
  <1>1 [req] The R-entry BLK-5 classification of §11.5 and §25.1 applies even when all supplied budgets allow a successful execution; §17.6 defines its mandatory typed fault point.   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5), DLT-11.5, DLT-25.1, DLT-17.6
DLT-1.6-P6
  <1>1 [req] A module that would be rejected and blocked is rejected.
  <1>2 [req] Warnings (`BD-W-*`, §22.4) never change a verdict.   deps: DLT-22.4
````

### §2 Terminology   [DLT-2]

````text
DLT-2-P1
  <1>1 [def] **Marker**: one of the 23 functions of §3.2.   deps: DLT-3.2
  <1>2 [def] **Marker call**: a `call` or `invoke` whose callee is a marker (§3.5).   deps: DLT-3.5
  <1>3 [def] **Effect marker**: one of the five `__bennett_effect_*` markers.
DLT-2-P2
  <1>1 [def] **Primitive**: a reserved function with prefix `__bennett_nz_` that the map lowers to an ISA instruction directly (§3.1, §16.4): `__bennett_nz_fault`, `__bennett_nz_alloc`, `__bennett_nz_free`.   deps: DLT-3.1, DLT-16.4
  <1>2 [def] **Runtime entry point**: a function with prefix `__bennett_rt_`, `__bennett_dev_` or `__bennett_sf_` that `bennett-runtimes` or `bennett-arith` defines in bitcode (§3.1).   deps: DLT-3.1
DLT-2-P3
  <1>1 [def] **Handle**: the `ptr` value a `__bennett_qalloc` or `__bennett_qborrow` call returns; it denotes a **register** of n qubits, the ISA's `qreg[n]` (ISA §3.3).   deps: ISA-3.3
  <1>2 [def] Qubit `i` of a handle is a **wire**.
  <1>3 [def] **Register tag**: the opaque value (nonzero, below 2^pw) that represents a handle in device memory and in integers; at the host–device boundary a handle is an ISA launch register id (§6.1).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-6.1
DLT-2-P4
  <1>1 [def] **Root**: an allocation of a register, identified by its allocation site and dynamic instance index (§6.5).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-5.1-P4.S1 (allocation site), DLT-6.5
  <1>2 [def] **Handle-derived**: a value whose provenance (§6.2) contains a root.   deps: DLT-6.2
DLT-2-P5
  <1>1 [def] **Region function**: a function a region-entry marker receives.
  <1>2 [def] **Region operand**: the marker operand that names it.
  <1>3 [def] **Candidate set**: the functions a region operand can denote (§7.2).   deps: DLT-7.2
DLT-2-P6
  <1>1 [def] **Action region**: a candidate of a `ctrl`, `adj` or `compute` operand; its interface is a quantum action (ISA §5.3).   deps: ISA-5.3
  <1>2 [def] **Oracle region**: a candidate of an `oracle` or `inplace` operand; its interface is a lifted oracle (ISA §5.2).   deps: ISA-5.2
  <1>3 [def] **Reversible region**: a candidate of a `reversible` operand, or the whole-program entry of §19.2; R-tier code (ISA §3.1).   deps: DLT-19.2, ISA-3.1
DLT-2-P7
  <1>1 [def] **Tier** of a function variant: **H** (host, native), **CC** (device classical control, ISA §3.1), **R** (reversible, ISA §3.1), **L** (lifted: an lproc or oracle body, ISA §3.7).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), ISA-3.1, ISA-3.7
  <1>2 [def] A **variant** is a (function, tier, coherence signature) triple (§4.3); host code keeps the original function, every device tier gets a clone.   deps: DLT-4.3
DLT-2-P8
  <1>1 [def] **Device program**: every variant of tier CC, R or L.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>2 [def] **Device entry**: a device variant called by an H function (a launch, ISA §10).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), ISA-10
DLT-2-P9
  <1>1 [def] **Function address** A(F): the address of F in the driver's comparison link (§4.4).   deps: DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (comparison link), DLT-4.4
DLT-2-P10
  <1>1 [def] **Abstract object**, **slot**, **value set**: §5. ⊤ is the unbounded value set.   deps: DLT-5
DLT-2-P11
  <1>1 [def] **Descriptor**: the in-memory oracle interface record of §8.1.   deps: DLT-8.1
  <1>2 [def] **Binding**: one parameter or device entry of a descriptor.
  <1>3 [def] **Shape**: a tuple of binding kinds, modes and widths (§8.4).   deps: DLT-8.4
DLT-2-P12
  <1>1 [def] **Budget record**: the in-memory record `bennett_budget` of §9.1.   deps: DLT-9.1
  <1>2 [def] **Budget table**: the driver input of §9.2.   deps: DLT-9.2
DLT-2-P13
  <1>1 [def] **Coherence state**: Cl (classical) or Co (coherent) of a value or object instance (§10, TDL-2).   deps: DLT-10-P3.S1 (TDL-2), DLT-10
  <1>2 [def] **Coherent slice**: an outlined maximal region of Co instructions (TDL-3).   deps: DLT-10-P14.S1 (TDL-3)
  <1>3 [def] **Arena**: the coherent memory of §10 TDL-4.   deps: DLT-10-P19.S1 (TDL-4), DLT-10
  <1>4 [def] **Generation**: the classical execution of an action region that records its events (§18.4).   deps: DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), DLT-18.4
DLT-2-P14
  <1>1 [def] **Device model**: one of `console`, `clock`, `entropy`, `block` (ISA §7.3), realised per tier by §11.   deps: ISA-7.3, DLT-11
DLT-2-P15
  <1>1 [def] **Coverage classes** (ISA §13): **lowered** (the construct has an ISA realisation), **shimmed** (it is replaced by a runtime or platform shim with a recorded `shim_hash`), **trapped** (it faults with code 8, `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`, on the executions that reach it, and has a coverage line).   deps: ISA-13
DLT-2-P16
  <1>1 [def] **Driver mode**: one of the invocation modes of §19.2–§19.7.   deps: DLT-19.2, DLT-19.3, DLT-19.4, DLT-19.5, DLT-19.6, DLT-19.7
  <1>2 [def] **Platform profile**: §20.   deps: DLT-20
DLT-2-P17
  <1>1 [def] **Materialised module**: the module after normalisation (§12).   deps: DLT-12
  <1>2 [def] **Reference object**: the ISA object the map produces for one oracle shape (§16.2); its oracle `@ref` defines F_B.   deps: DLT-16.2
DLT-2-P18
  <1>1 [def] **Blocker**: a capability with no ISA 0.8 realisation in a stated case (§25.1), BLK-1, BLK-2, BLK-4 and BLK-5 (BLK-3 retired, §25.1).   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1), DLT-25.1-P3.S1 (BLK-2), DLT-25.1-P5.S1 (BLK-4), DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5), DLT-25.1
````

### §3.1 Reserved names   [DLT-3.1]

````text
DLT-3.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] Every function or global name that starts with `__bennett_` is reserved.
  <1>2 [req] The reserved names other than the markers of §3.2 are:   deps: DLT-3.2
DLT-3.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] `__bennett_nz_` | nobody: **primitives**, lowered by the map (§16.4) and the CC/R lowering (§17.4) | the normaliser (§12) and runtimes bitcode   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (lowered), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-16.4, DLT-17.4, DLT-12
DLT-3.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] `__bennett_sf_` | `bennett-arith` bitcode (§13) | the normaliser and runtimes bitcode   deps: DLT-13
DLT-3.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] `__bennett_rt_` | `bennett-runtimes` bitcode (§11, §14, §17, §20); the driver's layout objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>` (§14.9) | the normaliser, the driver's synthesised code (§19) and runtimes bitcode   deps: DLT-11, DLT-14, DLT-17, DLT-20, DLT-14.9, DLT-19
DLT-3.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] `__bennett_dev_` | `bennett-runtimes` bitcode: device-model entry points and device state (§11.3) | the plugin's L lowering of effect markers and runtimes bitcode   deps: DLT-11.3-P1.S2 (device state), DLT-11.3
DLT-3.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] The primitives are exactly:
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 5 lines) declare void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64 %code) ; fault with code C (§12.3)   deps: DLT-12.3, DLT-17.1
DLT-3.1-P7
  <1>1 [def] A **producer input** is a bitcode file given to the driver other than `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` bitcode.
  <1>2 [unc] A producer input:   deps: DLT-3.1-P7.S1 (producer input)
DLT-3.1-P8
  <1>1 [req] MUST NOT define (`define`) a function whose name is a marker or a reserved name (`BD-E-MARKER-DEFINED`);   deps: DLT-22.1-P3.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-DEFINED)
DLT-3.1-P9
  <1>1 [req] MUST NOT declare, define or reference a reserved name that is not a marker (`BD-E-MARKER-UNKNOWN`);   deps: DLT-22.1-P4.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-UNKNOWN)
DLT-3.1-P10
  <1>1 [req] MUST NOT use a marker other than as the callee of a call or invoke (`BD-E-MARKER-ADDRESS`): no stored, compared, passed or cast marker address, so that no marker is ever called indirectly.   deps: DLT-22.1-P5.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-ADDRESS)
DLT-3.1-P11
  <1>1 [req] The driver checks rules 1–3 on every producer input before linking; the plugin checks rules 1 and 3 on the linked module, and, in closed-unit mode (§19.7), all three on its input.   deps: DLT-3.1-P7.S1 (producer input), DLT-19.7
  <1>2 [req] Checked examples are producer inputs.   deps: DLT-3.1-P7.S1 (producer input)
  <1>3 [req] `__bennett_ctrl_v`, the variadic probe of the spike ABI v0, is not a marker; it is rejected by rule 2. *(non-normative)* The legality corpus's trace runtime defines markers; it is a test harness, never a production link input (§19.9).   deps: DLT-19.9
````

### §3.2 The marker list   [DLT-3.2]

````text
DLT-3.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] The normative IR signatures (return type, name, parameter types; parameter names are documentation):
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 25 lines) declare ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 %cls, i64 %n)
DLT-3.2-P2
````

### §3.5 Recognition   [DLT-3.5]

````text
DLT-3.5-P1
  <1>1 [def] A call or invoke instruction is a **marker call** iff its callee operand is the function itself (not a loaded or cast value) and that function's name is exactly a marker name.
  <1>2 [req] Names are compared byte for byte; no prefix, suffix or demangled match is made.
  <1>3 [req] A marker call is accepted iff all of the following hold; otherwise `BD-E-MARKER-SIGNATURE`:   deps: DLT-22.1-P6.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-SIGNATURE), DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call)
DLT-3.5-P2
  <1>1 [req] the declaration's function type is exactly the marker's IR signature, **or** is the unprototyped form `R (...)` with R the marker's return type (a C declaration without a prototype, spike case `c-unprototyped-decl`);
DLT-3.5-P3
  <1>1 [req] the call instruction's function type is exactly the marker's IR signature, **or**, only when the declaration is unprototyped, the marker's parameter list followed by `...`;
DLT-3.5-P4
  <1>1 [req] the call passes exactly as many arguments as the signature has parameters;
DLT-3.5-P5
  <1>1 [req] the calling convention is the default C convention and the call has no operand bundles.
DLT-3.5-P6
  <1>1 [req] Attributes and metadata are not part of the comparison; they are governed by §3.6.   deps: DLT-3.6
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 12 lines) declare ptr @__bennett_qalloc(...)
DLT-3.5-P7
  <1>1 [req] The same program passing an `int` index without a cast gives an `i32` argument and is rejected:
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 12 lines) declare ptr @__bennett_qalloc(...)   deps: DLT-22.1-P6.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-SIGNATURE)
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 10 lines) declare i8 @__bennett_measure(ptr, i64)   deps: DLT-22.1-P6.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-SIGNATURE)
DLT-3.5-P8
  <1>1 [req] A 0.1-style `ctrl` with an `i64` budget no longer has the marker's signature:
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 14 lines) declare void @__bennett_ctrl(ptr, i64, ptr, i64, ptr)   deps: DLT-22.1-P6.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-SIGNATURE)
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 10 lines) declare void @__bennett_ctrl_v(ptr, i64, ptr, i64, ...)   deps: DLT-22.1-P4.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-UNKNOWN)
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) @table = global ptr @__bennett_gate   deps: DLT-22.1-P5.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-ADDRESS)
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 5 lines) define void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h) {   deps: DLT-22.1-P3.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-DEFINED)
DLT-3.5-P9
  <1>1 [req] A producer input may not reference a runtime entry point:   deps: DLT-3.1-P7.S1 (producer input)
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 8 lines) declare void @__bennett_rt_memmove(ptr, ptr, i64)   deps: DLT-22.1-P4.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-UNKNOWN)
````

### §3.6 The positive legality requirement and attributes   [DLT-3.6]

````text
DLT-3.6-P1
  <1>1 [req] A marker is an external declaration that may read and write any memory, may unwind and may not return: exactly an unknown external function to LLVM.
  <1>2 [req] Stock passes then preserve the dynamic sequence of marker calls on every executed path (spike findings §1: every standard pipeline of LLVM 18, 20 and 23, all 50 legal cases; the four negative controls are detected).   deps: DLT-10-P18.S1 (marker calls)
  <1>3 [req] No attribute is needed and none may be added that licenses elimination, commoning, speculation or motion.
DLT-3.6-P2
  <1>1 [req] Every attribute, keyword and calling convention on a marker declaration or marker call site is in exactly one of three lists:   deps: DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call)
DLT-3.6-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Forbidden** (`BD-E-MARKER-ATTRIBUTE`): attributes that license motion or elimination: `memory(...)` in any form, `readnone`, `readonly`, `writeonly`, `argmemonly`, `inaccessiblememonly`, `inaccessiblemem_or_argmemonly`, `willreturn`, `speculatable`, `noreturn`, `returned`, `nocapture`, `captures(...)`, `dereferenceable(...)`, `dereferenceable_or_null(...)`, `align`, `nofpclass(...)`, `byval`, `sret`, `writable`, `initializes(...)`, `dead_on_unwind`, `nosync`, `nofree`, `norecurse`, `mustprogress`, `musttail`, any non-default calling convention, `extern_weak` linkage. *(non-normative)* The first eight are the spike's L2 forbidden set; `noreturn` would delete the code after a marker; `returned` would replace a marker result by an argument; the parameter-effect attributes would let LLVM move stores to an environment across a region call; `dereferenceable` would let it speculate loads through a handle.   deps: DLT-22.1-P7.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-ATTRIBUTE), DLT-21.1-P3.S1 (L2)
DLT-3.6-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Benign** (ignored): function position `nounwind`, `nonlazybind`, `uwtable` (any form), `strictfp`, `cold`, `hot`, `nomerge`, `noinline`, `convergent`, `builtin`, `nobuiltin`, `optsize`, `minsize`, `optnone`, and every string attribute; return and parameter position `noundef`, `nonnull`, `range(...)`, `zeroext`, `signext`; declaration linkage and keywords external linkage, `dso_local`, `dso_preemptable`, `unnamed_addr`, `local_unnamed_addr`, any visibility; call-site keywords `tail`, `notail`; instruction metadata of any kind.
  <1>2 [req] The first group is the spike's L2 evidence (findings §1, §4: front ends add `nounwind`, `nonlazybind`, `uwtable`, `noundef`, `nonnull`, `zeroext` and string attributes; passes add `local_unnamed_addr`, `nonnull`, `noundef`, `range`, `tail`).   deps: DLT-21.1-P3.S1 (L2), DLT-1.1-P1.S1 (front ends)
  <1>3 [req] `strictfp` through `optnone` are added by build flags NS §0 allows (`-ffp-model=strict`, `-Os`, `-O0`) or restrict motion; none licenses elimination, commoning, speculation or motion of an unknown call.
  <1>4 [req] Before this version freezes, a corpus run confirms each of them on the three majors (§21.2, bd `.21`).   deps: DLT-21.2
DLT-3.6-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Unknown**: every other attribute is `BD-E-MARKER-ATTRIBUTE` until corpus evidence moves it to one of the lists above with a version change.   deps: DLT-22.1-P7.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-ATTRIBUTE)
  <1>2 [req] This is required by NS §3.1 (the positive legality requirement: an attribute the dialect has not classified may license motion).
DLT-3.6-P6
  <1>1 [req] Attribute groups (`#N`) are resolved before the check.
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 16 lines) declare ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 noundef, i64 noundef) unnamed_addr #0
DLT-3.6-P7
  <1>1 [req] Call sites under `-ffp-model=strict` carry `strictfp`; a `cold` declaration is benign too:
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 13 lines) declare void @__bennett_rot(i32, ptr, i64, double) #0
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 12 lines) declare void @__bennett_gate(i32, ptr, i64) #0   deps: DLT-22.1-P7.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-ATTRIBUTE)
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 10 lines) define void @g() {   deps: DLT-22.1-P7.S1 (BD-E-MARKER-ATTRIBUTE)
````

### §3.7 Unwinding of markers   [DLT-3.7]

````text
DLT-3.7-P1
  <1>1 [req] Markers never unwind.   deps: DLT-2-P1.S1 (Marker)
  <1>2 [req] Before any other analysis (§4.1 step S1) the plugin rewrites every `invoke` of a marker into a `call` with the same operands followed by `br label %normal`, removes the invoking block from the phi nodes of the unwind destination, and then deletes every block not reachable from the function's entry. *(non-normative)* This is Enzyme's practice (enzyme survey §1.1); C++ with `-fexceptions` and Rust `extern "C-unwind"` produce such invokes (emission summary item 3).   deps: DLT-4.1-P2.S1 (S1), DLT-4.1, DLT-1.1
  <1>3 [req] Front ends SHOULD declare markers non-unwinding (`noexcept`, Rust `extern "C"`).
  <1>4 [req] Unwinding through every other call of device code is the explicit-propagation transform of §14.4; 0.1's `BD-E-EH` is deleted.   deps: DLT-14.4
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 18 lines) declare i32 @__gxx_personality_v0(...)
````

### §3.8 Front-end emission routes   [DLT-3.8]

````text
DLT-3.8-P1
  <1>1 [req] **clang (C, C++)** and **rustc** emit markers as direct calls to the declaration (emission summary).
  <1>2 [req] Rust MUST declare each marker with one ABI per crate (`extern "C"`); mixed `"C"`/`"C-unwind"` declarations are merged by rustc into one declaration with `invoke` call sites, which §3.7 accepts.   deps: DLT-3.7
DLT-3.8-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Julia** MUST emit markers with `ccall("extern __bennett_<name>", llvmcall, …)` or with `Base.llvmcall((module_ir, "entry"), …)` whose module contains the `declare`.
  <1>2 [req] Both give a direct call to an attribute-free declaration; `ccall("extern __bennett_measure", llvmcall, Bool, …)` gives `declare i1 @__bennett_measure(ptr, i64)`, exactly the §3.2 signature (verified with Julia 1.13.3).   deps: DLT-3.2
  <1>3 [req] A plain `ccall(:__bennett_x, …)` or `@ccall` is an indirect call through a `jlplt` GOT global and is not a marker call.   deps: DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call)
  <1>4 [req] The plugin MUST reject a module containing a global variable whose name matches `jlplt___bennett_<name>_<digits>_got` or that carries the attribute `"julia.fname"="__bennett_<name>"` (`BD-E-JULIA-CCALL`), so that this mistake fails at compile time instead of at a lazy binding at run time; the `llvmcall` route keeps the full capability (audit B-13, L).   deps: DLT-22.1-P8.S1 (BD-E-JULIA-CCALL)
DLT-3.8-P3
  <1>1 [def] **Region functions** are passed as `ptr @f` or as any value whose value set §7.2 resolves.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-7.2
  <1>2 [req] Bennett.jl emits `ptr @f` in its own module (LLVM.jl), which keeps the full capability (audit B-14).
  <1>3 [req] A Julia `@cfunction` pointer is an `inttoptr` constant of an address of the Julia process; like every region pointer with value set ⊤ it is dispatched by value against the address-taken functions of the required type (§7.2), and an execution on which it equals no A(F) traps (`T-REGION`).   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-7.2
DLT-3.8-P4
  <1>1 [def] **Descriptors and budget records** are built by plain stores into an `alloca` or global (C: the header macros; Rust: `#[repr(C)]` structs; Julia: `Ref` of an `isbits` struct), which survive `default<O3>` as constant stores that §5.3 reads (corpus cases, §21.2).   deps: DLT-5.3, DLT-21.2
DLT-3.8-P5
  <1>1 [def] **Source effect lies** (spike F5). A device function definition carrying any of `memory(none)`, `memory(read)` (any `memory(...)` whose effects exclude a write to any location), `readnone`, `readonly`, `willreturn` or `speculatable`, from which a marker call is reachable through the call graph of §4.1 (the function itself included), is `BD-E-EFFECT-LIE`. *(non-normative)* LLVM cannot infer these attributes for a function that reaches an unknown external call, so they come from the source; the front end's own optimiser may already have deleted marker calls on their strength, which is why this is a diagnostic and not a guarantee (definedness assumption, NS §3.2).   deps: DLT-22.1-P9.S1 (BD-E-EFFECT-LIE), DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call), DLT-4.1-P12.S1 (call graph), DLT-10-P18.S1 (marker calls), DLT-4.1
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 11 lines) @jlplt___bennett_gate_528_got = external global ptr #0   deps: DLT-22.1-P8.S1 (BD-E-JULIA-CCALL)
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 15 lines) define internal void @kick(ptr %h) #0 {   deps: DLT-22.1-P9.S1 (BD-E-EFFECT-LIE)
````

### §4.1 Processing order   [DLT-4.1]

````text
DLT-4.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] A plugin processes the whole-program module (after the driver's link and post-link pipeline, §19.1) in this order; the first failing rule gives the verdict (§1.6):   deps: DLT-19.1, DLT-1.6
DLT-4.1-P2
  <1>1 [def] S1 | §3.1 reserved names; §3.5 recognition; §3.6 attributes; §3.8 Julia check; §3.7 marker invokes; §3.8 effect lies; §19.9 version checks   deps: DLT-3.1, DLT-3.5, DLT-3.6, DLT-3.8, DLT-3.7, DLT-19.9
DLT-4.1-P3
  <1>1 [def] S2 | the device analysis (§5): one fixpoint for points-to, value sets, region candidates (§7.2), indirect-call candidates (§14.2), handle provenance (§6.2) and descriptor kinds (§8.4)   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-5, DLT-7.2, DLT-14.2, DLT-6.2, DLT-8.4
DLT-4.1-P4
  <1>1 [def] S3 | tier assignment and variants (§4.2–§4.4); calls out of the device program (§4.5)   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-4.2, DLT-4.3, DLT-4.4, DLT-4.5
DLT-4.1-P5
  <1>1 [def] S4 | handle rules (§6)   deps: DLT-6
DLT-4.1-P6
  <1>1 [def] S5 | region well-formedness (§7.5)   deps: DLT-7.5
DLT-4.1-P7
  <1>1 [def] S6 | the TDL partition and the may-Co analysis (§10)   deps: DLT-10
DLT-4.1-P8
  <1>1 [def] S7 | normalisation of every device variant (§12) and the residual-poison validator (§12.4); dispatch domains (§3.4)   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-3.4-P20.S3 (dispatch domain), DLT-12, DLT-12.4, DLT-3.4
DLT-4.1-P9
  <1>1 [def] S8 | lifting (§16) of every oracle shape and coherent slice; in-place verification (§8.7)   deps: DLT-16, DLT-8.7
DLT-4.1-P10
  <1>1 [def] S9 | lowering to ISA objects (§17; plugin-defined, constrained by §18.7); blocker reports (§25.1)   deps: DLT-17, DLT-18.7, DLT-25.1
DLT-4.1-P11
  <1>1 [req] Within a step, rules apply in the order listed; within a rule, to instructions in module text order.
  <1>2 [req] Blocker reports are collected during S2–S8 and issued in S9 in that order.   deps: DLT-4.1-P3.S1 (S2), DLT-4.1-P4.S1 (S3), DLT-4.1-P5.S1 (S4), DLT-4.1-P6.S1 (S5), DLT-4.1-P7.S1 (S6), DLT-4.1-P8.S1 (S7), DLT-4.1-P9.S1 (S8), DLT-4.1-P10.S1 (S9), DLT-25.1-P8.S1 (Blocker report)
DLT-4.1-P12
  <1>1 [def] The **call graph** has an edge F → G when F contains a direct `call` or `invoke` of G (after resolving aliases), an edge F → R for every candidate R of every region operand of a marker call in F (a **region edge**), and an edge F → C for every candidate C of every indirect call in F (§14.2).   deps: DLT-14.2
  <1>2 [req] Only functions with a definition are nodes.
  <1>3 [req] The **closure** of a function is the set of functions reachable from it by direct and indirect-candidate edges (region edges start a new region and are not followed).   deps: DLT-4.1-P12.S1 (region edge)
````

### §4.2 Tier assignment   [DLT-4.2]

````text
DLT-4.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] Every defined function reachable from the program's roots gets a set of tiers.
  <1>2 [def] The **host roots** are the functions with non-local linkage and the functions whose address host code uses; the **device roots** are the region candidates and the whole-program entry of §19.2.   deps: DLT-19.2
DLT-4.2-P2
  <1>1 [req] **CC**: a function that contains a marker call, receives a handle (a parameter or a loaded value whose provenance contains a root, §6.2), is a candidate of an action region operand (its classical part is the generation, §18.4), is a device entry, is in the CC closure of one of these, or is promoted by §19.4.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), DLT-6.2, DLT-18.4, DLT-19.4
DLT-4.2-P3
  <1>1 [unc] **R**: a function in the closure of a `reversible` region operand, or every function under `--bennett-tier=reversible` (§19.2).   deps: DLT-19.2
DLT-4.2-P4
  <1>1 [unc] **L**: a function in the closure of an oracle or in-place region operand, or of a coherent slice (§10, TDL-3).   deps: DLT-10-P14.S1 (TDL-3), DLT-10
DLT-4.2-P5
  <1>1 [unc] **H**: every other function reachable from a host root, and every function reachable from a host root through host code only.
  <1>2 [req] A function in no device tier stays H only.
DLT-4.2-P6
  <1>1 [req] A function may get several tiers; it then has one variant per tier (§4.3). *(non-normative)* SQLite in the rung-4 program is H (the host builds and serialises the database) and L (the oracle runs the query), so it has two variants and one set of globals (SQLite §5.2(i)).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-4.3
````

### §4.3 Polyvariant cloning   [DLT-4.3]

````text
DLT-4.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] A **variant** is a (function, tier, coherence signature) triple, where the coherence signature is the tuple of coherence states of the function's parameters and of the may-Co objects it reads (§10, TDL-2) and is empty outside TDL.   deps: DLT-10-P3.S1 (TDL-2), DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-10
  <1>2 [req] The H variant is the original function; every device variant is a clone.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>3 [req] **Clones share every global: data is never cloned.** There is one classical memory (NS §3.2), shared by H, CC, R and the entry state of every L invocation (§8.5).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-8.5
  <1>4 [req] Calls inside a variant call the variant of the callee with the caller's tier and the coherence signature at the call.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>5 [req] 0.1's `BD-E-DEVICE-ADDRESS` is deleted: a host function may take the address of any function (audit B-16).
````

### §4.4 Function addresses   [DLT-4.4]

````text
DLT-4.4-P1
  <1>1 [def] Every function whose address is used has the **function address** A(F): its address in the driver's **comparison link** (§19.5: non-PIE, fixed section addresses, identical-code folding and constant merging off; NS §3.2, SQLite §5.2(ii)).   deps: DLT-19.5
  <1>2 [req] A(F) is the value of `ptr @F` in every tier:
DLT-4.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] in host code, by linking the production host image with the comparison layout whenever an address may cross the host–device boundary (§19.5);   deps: DLT-19.5
DLT-4.4-P3
  <1>1 [unc] in CC and R code, as `const` plus `inttoptr` (ISA §4.1);   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), ISA-4.1
DLT-4.4-P4
  <1>1 [unc] in lifted code, as `lift.const` (ISA §4.2);   deps: ISA-4.2
DLT-4.4-P5
  <1>1 [unc] in every data image: global initialisers, vtables, function tables (SQLite's `aBuiltinFunc`, VFS and pcache method tables).
DLT-4.4-P6
  <1>1 [unc] So `ptrtoint`, comparison, hashing and storage of function pointers equal native execution.
  <1>2 [req] Each tier maps A(F) to its own variant of F by the dispatch of §14.2.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-14.2
  <1>3 [req] Two functions never have equal A(F) (identical-code folding is off), so the map is well defined.
  <1>4 [req] A `blockaddress` constant is §14.9.   deps: DLT-14.9
````

### §4.5 Calls out of the device program   [DLT-4.5]

````text
DLT-4.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] A call in a device variant of a function with no definition resolves, in this order:   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
DLT-4.5-P2
  <1>1 [unc] a marker (§3.2) or a primitive (§3.1);   deps: DLT-3.2, DLT-3.1
DLT-4.5-P3
  <1>1 [def] a **runtime shim**: a definition of the symbol in `bennett-runtimes` or `bennett-arith` bitcode, which the driver links before S2 (§19.1): libc and libm (musl-derived), libpthread with one thread, the Julia shim runtime, Rust's `core::panicking::*` targets and the platform shims of §20.   deps: DLT-4.1-P3.S1 (S2), DLT-19.1, DLT-20
  <1>2 [req] The shim is an ordinary device function (coverage class `shimmed`, with its `shim_hash`, ISA §13);   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (shimmed), ISA-13
DLT-4.5-P4
  <1>1 [def] in a CC variant only, a **host call** through `hostcall` (§11.4);   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-11.4
DLT-4.5-P5
  <1>1 [req] otherwise **trapped**: the call is replaced by F(8) (§12.3) and the coverage report has a `trapped` line for it (NS §3.3: "Calls with no bitcode are coverage-report lines (*trapped*), never silent").   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (trapped), DLT-12.3
DLT-4.5-P6
  <1>1 [req] An R or L variant never makes a host call (ISA §4.1: `hostcall` is CC only; NS §4.2: coherent regions are closed). *(non-normative)* Item 4 is the only fault the dialect introduces for a call, and it occurs only on the executions that reach the call; it is the north star's own rule.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-4.5-P4.S1 (host call), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), ISA-4.1
````

### §5 The device analysis (one fixpoint)   [DLT-5]

````text
DLT-5-P1
  <1>1 [req] The device analysis is a whole-program, flow-insensitive, inclusion-based (Andersen-style), field-sensitive points-to and value-set analysis over every device variant and every host function that exchanges data with one (passes it a pointer, or shares a global it accesses).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>2 [req] It is specified exactly: **every verdict, candidate set and dispatch domain of this document is computed from the reference analysis below.**   deps: DLT-3.4-P20.S3 (dispatch domain)
  <1>3 [req] A plugin MAY use a more precise analysis to shrink dispatch domains or arena placement in its lowering, never to change a verdict (the run-time value is always a member of the reference set, so the results are unchanged).   deps: DLT-3.4-P20.S3 (dispatch domain)
````

### §5.1 Abstract objects and atoms   [DLT-5.1]

````text
DLT-5.1-P1
  <1>1 [def] The **abstract objects** are:
DLT-5.1-P2
  <1>1 [unc] each `alloca` instruction;
DLT-5.1-P3
  <1>1 [unc] each global variable;
DLT-5.1-P4
  <1>1 [def] each **allocation site**: a call of a function that carries `allockind("alloc")` or `allocsize(...)`, or whose name is one of `malloc`, `calloc`, `realloc`, `aligned_alloc`, `posix_memalign` (the object is the one written through its first argument), `memalign`, `strdup`, `strndup`, `_Znwm`, `_Znam`, `_ZnwmRKSt9nothrow_t`, `_ZnamRKSt9nothrow_t`, `_ZnwmSt11align_val_t`, `_ZnamSt11align_val_t`, `__rust_alloc`, `__rust_alloc_zeroed`, `__rust_realloc`, `julia.gc_alloc_obj`, `ijl_gc_alloc_typed`, `jl_gc_alloc`, `ijl_gc_small_alloc`, `ijl_gc_big_alloc`, or `__bennett_nz_alloc`;
DLT-5.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] the pseudo-object `EXT` (memory of unknown provenance: host memory reached through a pointer the analysis did not create).
DLT-5.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] Heap objects are context-insensitive: one object per allocation-site instruction of the variant in which it occurs.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>2 [req] The list above is the reference list; `bennett-runtimes` declares its allocator entry points with `allockind("alloc")` so that they are covered by the first rule.
DLT-5.1-P7
  <1>1 [def] An **offset** is an integer byte offset, a **residue class** (r mod s)[lo, hi) with s ≥ 2 (every offset o with o ≡ r (mod s) and lo ≤ o < hi), or `*` (any offset).
  <1>2 [req] An **atom** is one of:
DLT-5.1-P8
  <1>1 [unc] `(X, o)`: a pointer into object X at offset o;
DLT-5.1-P9
  <1>1 [unc] `FN(F)`: the address A(F) of a defined function F;
DLT-5.1-P10
  <1>1 [unc] `ROOT(r)`: a handle with root r (the allocation site of a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call, §6.5);   deps: DLT-5.1-P4.S1 (allocation site), DLT-6.5
DLT-5.1-P11
  <1>1 [unc] `INT(c)`: the integer constant c (of the value's width).
DLT-5.1-P12
  <1>1 [def] Every SSA value v (per lane for vectors, per index path for aggregates) has an **abstract value** AV(v) = (A, t): a finite set A of atoms and a flag t; t = ⊤ means "and any other integer or address". ⊤ never stands for a `ROOT` atom (handle provenance is always explicit); for a function-pointer operand it stands for "any function of the operand's type whose address is used" (§7.2, §14.2).   deps: DLT-7.2, DLT-14.2
  <1>2 [req] Every slot (X, o) of memory has a content AV(X, o) of the same form.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>3 [req] When A holds more than **K = 4096** `INT` atoms they are dropped and t is set (a normative constant: it decides dispatch and blocker verdicts, so §24 does not defer it).   deps: DLT-24
  <1>4 [req] "AV(v) = ⊤" below means t is set; unions and inclusions are taken component-wise.
````

### §5.2 Rules   [DLT-5.2]

````text
DLT-5.2-P1
  <1>1 [unc] Before the fixpoint, seed the initialiser atoms of every global, mutable or constant, into its slots.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>2 [req] For each alloca and allocation-site object, also represent every extent whose initial contents are zero under §§17.1/17.3 as a zero-byte initial image; represent a nonconstant extent symbolically over its possible allocated bytes.   deps: DLT-17.1, DLT-17.3
  <1>3 [req] Subsequent stores union additional possibilities and never erase these initial possibilities.
  <1>4 [req] This is an initial image, not a claim that the object remains immutable.
  <1>5 [req] The analysis is the least fixpoint of the following rules over AV.
  <1>6 [req] "Copies" means AV(result) ⊇ AV(operand) lane by lane; ⊤ absorbs.
  <1>7 [req] S2 models the value effects of the specified normalisation before verdicts and dispatch domains freeze; pre-normalisation syntax does not permit omitting a value produced by the materialised execution.   deps: DLT-4.1-P3.S1 (S2), DLT-3.4-P20.S3 (dispatch domain)
DLT-5.2-P2
  <1>1 [def] PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above
DLT-5.2-P3
  <1>1 [def] PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a).   deps: DLT-16.4
  <1>2 [req] Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p).
  <1>3 [req] For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions.
  <1>4 [req] Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute.
  <1>5 [req] If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative.   deps: DLT-5.2-P7.S1 (PT6)
  <1>6 [req] The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions.   deps: DLT-6.4
  <1>7 [req] ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit.   deps: DLT-6.2
  <1>8 [req] Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound
DLT-5.2-P4
  <1>1 [def] PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union).
  <1>2 [req] A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane.
  <1>3 [req] Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width.   deps: DLT-12.2-P23.S1 (N21), DLT-12.6
  <1>4 [req] Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane.   deps: DLT-5.2-P5.S1 (PT4), DLT-5.2-P6.S1 (PT5)
  <1>5 [req] Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar.
  <1>6 [req] Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers.
  <1>7 [req] Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT.   deps: DLT-6.2
  <1>8 [req] Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze.   deps: DLT-4.1-P3.S1 (S2), DLT-3.4-P20.S3 (dispatch domain)
  <1>9 [req] Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required
DLT-5.2-P5
  <1>1 [def] PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions.
  <1>2 [req] Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends.   deps: DLT-16.4
  <1>3 [req] Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance.   deps: DLT-5.2-P3.S1 (PT2)
  <1>4 [req] A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape.
  <1>5 [req] Apply also to constant expressions
DLT-5.2-P6
  <1>1 [def] PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition.
  <1>2 [req] Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a).
  <1>3 [req] Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance.   deps: DLT-5.2-P3.S1 (PT2)
  <1>4 [req] Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it.
  <1>5 [req] Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤.
  <1>6 [req] Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image.
  <1>7 [req] Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation.
  <1>8 [req] Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2).   deps: DLT-5.2-P3.S1 (PT2)
  <1>9 [req] The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain.   deps: DLT-12.2-P5.S1 (N3), DLT-12.2-P6.S1 (N4), DLT-3.4-P20.S3 (dispatch domain)
  <1>10 [req] Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders
DLT-5.2-P7
  <1>1 [def] PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation.
  <1>2 [req] A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers).
  <1>3 [req] For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values.
  <1>4 [req] X=EXT gives ⊤.
  <1>5 [req] A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>6 [req] ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply.   deps: DLT-6.2
  <1>7 [req] For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules.
  <1>8 [req] PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory.   deps: DLT-5.2-P9.S1 (PT8)
  <1>9 [req] Zero contributes no ROOT.
  <1>10 [req] Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros
DLT-5.2-P8
  <1>1 [def] PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback.   deps: DLT-5.2-P7.S1 (PT6)
  <1>2 [req] AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k
DLT-5.2-P9
  <1>1 [def] PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s).   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>2 [req] Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT.
  <1>3 [req] Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant)   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
DLT-5.2-P10
  <1>1 [def] PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-14.2
DLT-5.2-P11
  <1>1 [def] PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics.   deps: DLT-12.2-P17.S1 (N15)
  <1>2 [req] Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero.
  <1>3 [req] Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L).
  <1>4 [req] No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT.
  <1>5 [req] In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector.
  <1>6 [req] Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected.
  <1>7 [req] Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23)
DLT-5.2-P12
  <1>1 [def] PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6   deps: DLT-5.2-P10.S1 (PT9), DLT-6.6
DLT-5.2-P13

  <1>2 [req] An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>3 [req] `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg`: a load plus a store (PT6, PT7)   deps: DLT-5.2-P7.S1 (PT6), DLT-5.2-P8.S1 (PT7)
DLT-5.2-P14
  <1>1 [req] An `inttoptr` of a constant gives (EXT, `*`) and, for function-pointer purposes, ⊤: function addresses are symbolic `FN` atoms during the analysis, and their numeric values A(F) are fixed only by the comparison link (§19.5), after normalisation (S7) and before lifting (S8).   deps: DLT-4.1-P8.S1 (S7), DLT-4.1-P9.S1 (S8), DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (comparison link), DLT-19.5
DLT-5.2-P15

DLT-5.2-P19
  <1>1 [req] **Pointer-integer wrap witnesses.** Two i64 additions of −2^63 have net zero displacement modulo 2^64, so p=@tab and f=@body.
  <1>2 [req] Modular displacement alternatives are retained alongside unknown-address alternatives, never lost at an intermediate out-of-object value.
  <1>3 [req] A ptrtoint to i128 followed by +2^64 and inttoptr to a 64-bit pointer likewise returns @tab.
  <1>4 [req] The third witness computes the address of a different allocation: unknown-address loading must retain its possible function value.
  <1>5 [req] With adequate budgets dispatch succeeds exactly once.
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 17 lines) target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"
  <1>7 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"candidates":["body"],"calls":1,"trap":0}
  <1>8 [ex] (code block, 16 lines) target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"
  <1>9 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"candidates":["body"],"calls":1,"trap":0}
  <1>10 [ex] (code block, 19 lines) target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"
  <1>11 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"candidates":["body"],"calls":1,"trap":0}
DLT-5.2-P20
  <1>1 [req] **PT6 byte reconstruction witnesses.** Immutable bytes [1,1] decode as 1+256=257 under e-p:64:64, never the union {1}.   deps: DLT-5.2-P7.S1 (PT6)
  <1>2 [req] A two-byte subrange of a mutable four-byte store of 66049 (bytes 01 02 01 00) concretely gives 513, but has no exact whole-interval atom: its fixed abstract result is TOP, not {66049}.
  <1>3 [req] The fallback executes width 513 without claiming a finite reference dispatch set.
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 13 lines) target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"value_set":[257],"width":257,"wire":256,"live_after":0,"trap":0}
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 12 lines) target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"
  <1>7 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) BD-B-ISA4 %n: partial mutable load has TOP width; BLK-1 (ISA-4)   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1)
  <1>8 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"value_set":"TOP","width":513,"live_after":0,"trap":0}
DLT-5.2-P21
  <1>1 [req] A constant-byte fill carries its complete filled interval and byte value; a load contained in that interval decodes the repeated byte at the load's width.
  <1>2 [req] Other overlapping writes still participate in PT6 (a fill does not erase them).   deps: DLT-5.2-P7.S1 (PT6)
  <1>3 [req] PT8 copies preserve storage intervals/representation widths only for fully copied atoms.   deps: DLT-5.2-P9.S1 (PT8)
  <1>4 [req] Partial copies and fills add overlapping affected intervals; PT6 then returns ⊤ for any load whose whole bits are not represented exactly.   deps: DLT-5.2-P7.S1 (PT6)
  <1>5 [req] These are fixed reference rules, not optional precision improvements.
````
