### §5.3 Value sets   [DLT-5.3]

````text
DLT-5.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] The **value set** VS(v) of an integer or pointer operand is the set of its `INT` and `FN` atoms (pointer atoms denote addresses that are not compile-time constants and make VS(v) = ⊤ for dispatch purposes), or ⊤.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set)
  <1>2 [req] A value set is a precision tool only:   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set)
DLT-5.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] an operand with a finite VS is dispatched over it (§3.4, §14.2);   deps: DLT-3.4, DLT-14.2
DLT-5.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] an operand with VS = ⊤ is dispatched over its type's whole valid domain where that domain is finite: operation codes and classes (§3.4), binding kinds (§8.4), the function addresses of a type (§7.2, §14.2);   deps: DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (function address), DLT-3.4, DLT-8.4, DLT-7.2, DLT-14.2
DLT-5.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] otherwise it is a blocker case of §25.1: widths (BLK-1), budgets computed on the device (BLK-2), parameters of in-place pairs (§8.7).   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1), DLT-25.1-P3.S1 (BLK-2), DLT-25.1, DLT-8.7
````

### §5.4 Uses   [DLT-5.4]

````text
DLT-5.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] `FN` atoms of region operands | region candidate sets (§7.2)   deps: DLT-7.2
DLT-5.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] `FN` atoms of indirect callees | indirect-call candidate sets (§14.2)   deps: DLT-14.2
DLT-5.4-P3
  <1>1 [req] `ROOT` atoms | handle provenance (§6.2), handle containment (§6.4), static register files (§6.5)   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-6.2, DLT-6.4, DLT-6.5
DLT-5.4-P4
  <1>1 [req] `INT` atoms of descriptor and budget fields | descriptor shapes (§8.4), budget pre-minting (§9.5)   deps: DLT-8.4, DLT-9.5
DLT-5.4-P5
  <1>1 [req] slots stored under control | the may-Co analysis and arena placement (§10, TDL-2, TDL-4)   deps: DLT-10-P3.S1 (TDL-2), DLT-10-P19.S1 (TDL-4), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-10
DLT-5.4-P6
  <1>1 [req] objects loaded by L code | the oracle read set R and entry-state transfer (§8.5)   deps: DLT-8.5-P2.S14 (entry-state transfer), DLT-8.5
DLT-5.4-P7
  <1>1 [req] `INT` atoms of `op`, `cls`, `n` | dispatch domains (§3.4)   deps: DLT-3.4-P20.S3 (dispatch domain), DLT-3.4
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 20 lines) %struct.tab = type { [4 x ptr], i64 }
DLT-5.4-P8
  <1>1 [non] *(non-normative)* In `pt-variable-index`, `%pk` has the offset class (0 mod 8)[0, 32) in `%s` (the `[4 x ptr]` array), so the load `%hk` reads the slots 0, 8, 16, 24: AV(`%hk`) = {ROOT(`%h0`)}.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>2 [non] The count field is at the exact offset 32, outside the class, so AV(`%n`) holds INT(3) and, through the store of `%n2`, INT(4), INT(5), … until it exceeds K and becomes ⊤; it holds no `ROOT` atom, and the arithmetic on it is legal (audit B-27: 0.1 gave the load offset `*` and rejected this program).
DLT-5.4-P9
  <1>1 [req] **Finite-width and materialised-domain witnesses (round 6).** Under I=32, 2^32 truncates to zero, so the table load yields FN(body) and dispatch succeeds.
  <1>2 [req] For the ranged inputs, N3/N4 erase the annotations: `n & 3` gives {0,1,2,3} and `| 1` gives {1,3}.   deps: DLT-12.2-P5.S1 (N3), DLT-12.2-P6.S1 (N4)
  <1>3 [req] At n=2 width 3 admits both X gates on wire 2 and a clean free.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>4 [req] No new input precondition or fault is introduced.
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 14 lines) target datalayout = "e-p:64:64:64:32"
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"pointer_offset":0,"candidates":["body"],"calls":1,"trap":0}
  <1>7 [ex] (code block, 14 lines) declare ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64, i64)
  <1>8 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"value_set":[1,3],"input":2,"width":3,"live_after":0,"trap":0}
  <1>9 [ex] (code block, 16 lines) declare ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64, i64)
  <1>10 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"value_set":[1,3],"input":2,"width":3,"live_after":0,"trap":0}
````

### §6.1 Representation   [DLT-6.1]

````text
DLT-6.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] A handle is an ordinary `ptr` in address space 0.
  <1>2 [def] In device memory and in integers a handle is its **register tag**: an opaque value below 2^pw assigned to an allocation generation (§6.5), nonzero and distinct from every identity still observable through live or stale copies.   deps: DLT-6.5
  <1>3 [req] Physical slots are reusable storage, never allocation identity.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>4 [req] A tag MUST NOT be recycled while an observable stale copy can remain; integer wrap is not permission to alias generations.
  <1>5 [req] Copies continue to denote their original generation after free.
  <1>6 [req] ISA 0.8 has no instruction that reads a `qreg`'s launch id (BLK-1's `qreg.id` would add one), so tags are the plugin's.   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1)
  <1>7 [req] At the host–device boundary a handle is the ISA launch register id (`bennett_qreg`, ISA §10), which the launch binds to a `qreg` parameter or result (§6.6).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), ISA-10, DLT-6.6
  <1>8 [req] Neither value may influence a result other than through §6.3; the rules below make them unobservable.   deps: DLT-6.3
  <1>9 [req] A handle denotes a register of n wires, n the `qalloc` operand; wire i of handle h is written h.i.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
````

### §6.2 Provenance (maintainer decision 2)   [DLT-6.2]

````text
DLT-6.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] The **provenance** of a value is the set of `ROOT` atoms of its abstract value (§5).   deps: DLT-5.1-P12.S1 (abstract value), DLT-5
  <1>2 [def] Provenance flows through **bitwise copies** only: `ptrtoint`/`inttoptr` at width pw, integer loads and stores of width pw, vector lanes (including dynamic lanes, PT10: a dynamically indexed vector of handles is a handle table), aggregate fields, memory copies, `phi`, `select`, `freeze`, call arguments and returns (PT3–PT10).   deps: DLT-5.2-P11.S1 (PT10), DLT-5.2-P4.S1 (PT3), DLT-5.2-P5.S1 (PT4), DLT-5.2-P6.S1 (PT5), DLT-5.2-P7.S1 (PT6), DLT-5.2-P8.S1 (PT7), DLT-5.2-P9.S1 (PT8), DLT-5.2-P10.S1 (PT9)
  <1>3 [req] A handle-derived value (any lane or field) MAY be:
DLT-6.2-P2
  <1>1 [unc] a handle operand of a marker (§3.2);   deps: DLT-3.2
DLT-6.2-P3
  <1>1 [unc] copied by the rules above, including storage in any slot the analysis tracks (§6.4);   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-6.4
DLT-6.2-P4
  <1>1 [unc] an operand of `icmp eq` or `icmp ne` (§6.3);   deps: DLT-6.3
DLT-6.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] an argument of a direct or indirect call of a defined function, or a return value.
DLT-6.2-P6
  <1>1 [req] Every other use is `BD-E-HANDLE-USE` (maintainer decision 2: "any other use of a handle-derived integer is rejected fail-loud"; NS §3.1 "never dereferenced"): in particular arithmetic, bitwise operations, `icmp` with any other predicate, any cast other than the copies above (`trunc`, `zext`, `sext`, `bitcast` to a non-pointer-width type, `ptrtoint` to a width ≠ pw), `getelementptr` with a handle as base or index, `switch` on it, use as the pointer operand of a `load`, `store`, `atomicrmw`, `cmpxchg` or memory intrinsic (dereference), and a load of any type whose byte range overlaps a handle-derived slot without being exactly one pw-wide lane of it (a partial read).   deps: DLT-22.1-P11.S1 (BD-E-HANDLE-USE), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
DLT-6.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] A handle operand of a marker whose provenance is empty is `BD-E-HANDLE-UNTRACKED` (it cannot be a handle of this program). *(non-normative)* A tracked value that at run time is not a live handle (a stale copy, a freed register) traps at the marker (`T-DEAD`, §18.3).   deps: DLT-22.1-P13.S1 (BD-E-HANDLE-UNTRACKED), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-18.3
````

### §6.3 Comparison (maintainer decision 4)   [DLT-6.3]

````text
DLT-6.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] `icmp eq` and `icmp ne` of a handle with `null`, or with another handle-derived value, compare **identity**: a live handle is never null, and two handles are equal iff they denote the same allocation generation, including when comparing a freed identity with a later allocation in the same physical slot.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>2 [req] The result is not handle-derived.
  <1>3 [req] In CC it lowers to `icmp` on register tags.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-6.1-P1.S2 (register tag)
  <1>4 [req] This covers C `if (!q)` and Rust's `Option<Handle>` niche (audit D8). *(non-normative)* A comparison is a classical observation of identity, not of the register's content; ids are unique, so it equals the native comparison of the two handle values.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (Handle)
````

### §6.4 Collections and containment   [DLT-6.4]

````text
DLT-6.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] Handles may be stored in any slot the analysis tracks: tables, vectors, linked structures, globals (field-sensitively), heap objects, and objects passed to indirect calls or returned by address-taken functions (audit B-22, B-24, B-26, B-29).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (Handle), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>2 [req] A handle may **escape** only where code the analysis cannot see could read it:
DLT-6.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] `BD-E-HANDLE-ESCAPE`: a `ROOT` atom reaches a slot of `EXT`, an argument or a reachable slot of a call that resolves to a host call or is trapped (§4.5), or the return value of a function whose callers are not all in the link, other than a device entry's `QREG` result (§6.6).   deps: DLT-22.1-P12.S1 (BD-E-HANDLE-ESCAPE), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-4.5-P4.S1 (host call), DLT-2-P15.S1 (trapped), DLT-4.5, DLT-6.6
  <1>2 [req] NS §3.1: "any handle use that survives into host native code is rejected".
DLT-6.4-P3
  <1>1 [req] H functions in the link that receive launch register ids through §6.6 are analysed like device code: they may copy, store, compare and pass them back to device entries (§6.2 items 2–4); any other use is `BD-E-HANDLE-USE`.   deps: DLT-22.1-P11.S1 (BD-E-HANDLE-USE), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-6.6, DLT-6.2-P3, DLT-6.2-P4, DLT-6.2-P5
````

### §6.5 Realisation on ISA 0.8: static register files   [DLT-6.5]

````text
DLT-6.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] ISA 0.8 has no instruction that stores, loads or converts a `qreg` (ISA §3.2, §3.3, §4.1), and `qreg[n]` types have literal widths.   deps: ISA-3.2, ISA-3.3, ISA-4.1
  <1>2 [req] CC code holds `qreg` values in its classical environment (dynamic SSA instances, block parameters, call arguments and results; ISA §3.2, §7.4).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), ISA-3.2, ISA-7.4
  <1>3 [unc] The plugin realises each allocation site s (a `qalloc` or `qborrow` call) in one of two ways:   deps: DLT-5.1-P4.S1 (allocation site)
DLT-6.5-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Direct.** If no `ROOT(s)` atom reaches a memory slot (PT7, PT8), the handle is a CC `qreg` value threaded through SSA values, block parameters, call arguments and results ("register- environment passing"; §25.2 records that ISA §3.3's transition table should name CC `call`, `ret` and `br` operands of `qreg` type).   deps: DLT-5.2-P8.S1 (PT7), DLT-5.2-P9.S1 (PT8), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-25.2, ISA-3.3
  <1>2 [req] Every execution of s creates a new dynamic instance, so loops and recursion need no bound.
DLT-6.5-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Static register file.** Otherwise a physical **slot** is a pair (s, instance index k < N(s)); ROOT(s) is static provenance, not run-time identity.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>2 [req] For each slot the plugin keeps one CC `qreg` variable, threaded like a direct handle through every CC variant that may use it; `qalloc` at s picks a free instance index at run time and executes `qreg.new cls n` into that variable (one variable per member of the width dispatch domain, §3.4).   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-3.4-P20.S3 (dispatch domain), DLT-3.4
  <1>3 [req] The default CC table holds each slot's current generation tag and liveness; a fresh-tag allocator gives every allocation a new identity (§6.1), stored in memory.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-6.1
  <1>4 [req] A marker operand is dispatched by matching its full tag against current live candidate slots (compare tree, §14.2); a missing or retired generation traps T-DEAD before changing any register.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-14.2
  <1>5 [req] Free retires the identity even if the slot is immediately reused.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>6 [req] Recycle tags only after old copies are proven unobservable, never by slot reuse alone.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>7 [req] This table and allocator use ordinary classical operations, without qreg.id.
  <1>8 [unc] N(s) bounds simultaneous physical slots, not the number of historical identities.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>9 [req] A direct handle that is converted to an integer (`ptrtoint`, a store) gets a tag in the same way.
DLT-6.5-P4
  <1>1 [def] **Reference instance bound** of a site s in function F with a static register file:   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
DLT-6.5-P5
  <1>1 [req] Before rules 1–2, augment each function's CFG with N29 continuation edges: from every call whose closure may longjmp, add an edge to each setjmp continuation in every function that may be an ancestor of that call (including the same function).   deps: DLT-12.2-P31.S1 (N29)
  <1>2 [unc] Include the materialised __bennett_rt_setjmp continuations, candidate callees and interprocedural call edges.
  <1>3 [req] The reference default remains conservative: if a setjmp continuation in any may-ancestor A of F (A=F included) can reach s through these CFG and call edges, s is not self-disjoint and E(s)=∞.   deps: DLT-6.5-P8.S1 (self-disjoint)
  <1>4 [req] Apply this rule to §7.5(b) R hoisting as well.   deps: DLT-7.5-P3
  <1>5 [req] Such a site uses BLK-1 (R hoisting: BLK-4), with the bounded static-file fallback, never rejection.   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1), DLT-25.1-P5.S1 (BLK-4)
  <1>6 [req] The read-only ISA 0.9 draft's K1 supplies the exact missing dynamic qreg.width/id/from_id and run-time-width register capability; K2 supplies R allocation for BLK-4.   deps: DLT-25.1-P5.S1 (BLK-4), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>7 [unc] Neither witness claims a finite reference bound.
DLT-6.5-P6
  <1>1 [req] **Entry-boundary liveness (before rules 1–2).** If an instance of s may be live at the return of any device entry that may reach s — memory live at that return (a global, heap object, or memory reachable from a result or env) may hold ROOT(s), and not every path from s through the augmented CFGs and call edges to that return passes a qfree with provenance exactly {s} — then s is not self-disjoint and E(s)=∞.   deps: DLT-6.5-P8.S1 (self-disjoint)
  <1>2 [req] Use BLK-1, with the bounded static-file fallback, never rejection.   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1)
  <1>3 [req] The host may call entries any number of times: executions per entry call do not bound instances that survive return.
  <1>4 [req] Under §7.5(b), substitute reversible-invocation return and BLK-4; the same boundary test precedes hoisting.   deps: DLT-25.1-P5.S1 (BLK-4), DLT-7.5-P3
  <1>5 [req] An internal callee's return alone is not a device-entry return: a guaranteed free in its caller before the entry returns can discharge this condition.
DLT-6.5-P7
  <1>1 [req] **Parameter and result roots.** Apply the same boundary test to candidate handle parameter ROOT(E,i) (§6.6), and to a qalloc instance returned as an entry QREG result.   deps: DLT-6.6
  <1>2 [req] Persistent memory (a global, heap object, or memory reachable from result or env) or the entry's QREG result may retain it.
  <1>3 [unc] Include every reusable exit, including unwind/resume edges, in the augmented interprocedural graph; an exact-root qfree on every path from receipt/allocation discharges liveness.
  <1>4 [req] Otherwise E=∞: the host may supply or retain unboundedly many distinct live registers across calls.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>5 [req] Marker uses of such roots loaded from persistent memory are BLK-1 blocker points (`blocked:BD-B-ISA4`), with §25.1's bounded static-file fallback and blocker trap, never rejection.   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1), DLT-2-P1.S1 (Marker), DLT-6.5-P10.S1 (blocker point), DLT-1.6-P4.S2 (blocker trap), DLT-25.1
  <1>6 [req] A handle parameter used directly only within its receiving entry keeps the direct realisation, as does a returned QREG never stored in memory.   deps: DLT-6.6-P1.S2 (handle parameter)
  <1>7 [req] The infinite bound on a returned root is relevant to its static-file realisation when it reaches memory; it does not impose a finite bound on direct QREG transport.
  <1>8 [unc] Under §7.5(b), substitute reversible-invocation exit and BLK-4 (`blocked:BD-B-ISA12`) for receipt through env.   deps: DLT-25.1-P5.S1 (BLK-4), DLT-7.5-P3
DLT-6.5-P8
  <1>1 [def] s is **self-disjoint** iff the only memory its `ROOT(s)` atom reaches is allocas of F whose address does not escape F (it is not stored, returned, or passed to a call other than a marker call, which includes the `env` of a region-entry marker), and every CFG path of F from s to a `ret`, to s again, or to a call whose callee may reach F through the call graph passes through a `qfree` whose operand's provenance is exactly {s}.   deps: DLT-4.1-P12.S1 (call graph)
  <1>2 [unc] Then N(s) = 1.
DLT-6.5-P9
  <1>1 [req] Otherwise N(s) = E(s), the bound on executions of s per device-entry call: E(s) = TC(s) × Σ_c E(c) over the device call sites c of F (E = 1 for a device entry), where TC(s) is the product of the constant maximum trip counts of the loops of F's augmented CFG that contain s (LLVM's `ScalarEvolution::getSmallConstantMaxTripCount` on the materialised module of the major in use), and E(s) = ∞ when a containing loop has no constant maximum trip count or F is in a cycle of the call graph.   deps: DLT-12.1-P1.S6 (materialised module), DLT-4.1-P12.S1 (call graph)
  <1>2 [req] E(c), for a call site c in function C, uses the same recurrence: TC(c) in C's augmented CFG times the sum of E over C's device call sites (entry multiplicity 1).
  <1>3 [req] Any unbounded augmented loop, recursive cycle or conservative setjmp-continuation or entry-boundary-liveness case gives infinity; normal CFG loop facts cannot override it.
DLT-6.5-P10
  <1>1 [def] If N(s) = ∞, every execution of s is a **blocker point** of BLK-1 (`blocked:BD-B-ISA4`, §25.1): ISA 0.8 cannot name an unbounded number of registers reached through memory.   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-25.1
  <1>2 [req] The plugin compiles the program with a static file of `--bennett-qreg-instances` instances (default 64, §24) and a blocker trap (§25.1) on an execution that needs more.   deps: DLT-1.6-P4.S2 (blocker trap), DLT-24, DLT-25.1
DLT-6.5-P11
  <1>1 [req] **Cross-entry liveness witness.** The host calls put twice, then use.   deps: DLT-4.5-P4.S1 (host call)
  <1>2 [req] The first instance remains live in @slots when the second is allocated at the same site.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>3 [req] TC=1 per call does not bound lifetime across calls: E(s)=∞, BLK-1.   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1)
  <1>4 [req] The 64-slot fallback keeps two distinct registers live, flips each twice and frees both clean.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 33 lines) @n = internal global i64 0
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) BD-B-ISA4 @put qalloc: instance may outlive a device-entry return; BLK-1 (ISA-4)   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1)
  <1>7 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"verdict":"blocked:BD-B-ISA4","entry_calls":2,"instances":2,"distinct":true,"li…
DLT-6.5-P12
  <1>1 [req] **Ancestor setjmp witness.** P's continuation calls G again after deep longjmps; G contains no setjmp.
  <1>2 [req] The first handle is still live in slot[0] when the second is stored in slot[1].   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>3 [req] The reference E(s)=∞ gives BLK-1; the 64-instance fallback executes two distinct clean handles and frees both, max live 2, no trap.   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1)
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 35 lines) @jb = global [256 x i8] zeroinitializer
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) BD-B-ISA4 @G qalloc: ancestor setjmp continuation re-entry; BLK-1 (ISA-4); requi…   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1)
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"verdict":"blocked:BD-B-ISA4","instances":2,"distinct":true,"live_after":0,"tra…
DLT-6.5-P13
  <1>1 [req] **Reused slot, stale identity.** Both iterations allocate at the same site and free before re-entry/return, with ROOT memory only in nonescaping allocas: N(s)=1.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>2 [req] The saved first-generation handle is unequal to the second and traps T-DEAD/code 4 at its first gate, before changing the second register.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>3 [req] Two X gates cannot hide the invalid first use.
  <1>4 [req] Runtime fault cleanup then releases the clean second slot.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 29 lines) @generation_equal = global i32 0
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"physical_slots":1,"equal":false,"stale_code":4,"second_value":0,"live_after":0…
DLT-6.5-P14
  <1>1 [req] A width with VS = ⊤ is BLK-1 likewise (§3.4).   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1), DLT-3.4
  <1>2 [req] In R code, which cannot allocate registers on ISA 0.8, §7.5(b) applies.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-7.5-P3
DLT-6.5-P15
  <1>1 [req] **Setjmp re-entry witness.** The first h remains live across g's longjmp; the second execution of the same allocation site produces a distinct h.   deps: DLT-5.1-P4.S1 (allocation site)
  <1>2 [req] The simple reference default gives N=∞ and BLK-1, not N=1.   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1)
  <1>3 [req] The bounded file of 64 runs these two allocations without a trap; each register is flipped twice then freed clean.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>4 [req] Native setjmp execution checks the two distinct live instance ids and balanced release.

  <1>7 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"instances":2,"distinct":true,"live_after":0,"trap":0,"verdict":"blocked:BD-B-I…
````

### §6.6 Host–device boundary   [DLT-6.6]

````text
DLT-6.6-P1
  <1>1 [unc] A device entry's parameters and results of handle type bind to launch `QREG` arguments and results (ISA §10).   deps: ISA-10
  <1>2 [def] A `ptr` parameter of a device entry is a **handle parameter** iff its candidate atom `ROOT(E, i)` (added by PT11 to every pointer parameter of every device entry) reaches a marker handle operand; otherwise the candidate atom is removed before §6.2 is checked.   deps: DLT-5.2-P12.S1 (PT11), DLT-6.2
  <1>3 [req] A handle parameter binds to `QREG`; host code may copy, store and compare launch register ids and pass them back.   deps: DLT-6.6-P1.S2 (handle parameter), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>4 [req] Leak checking happens at program end and at `bennett_qreg_free`, not at each device-entry return, which gives the REPL's lexical lifetime (NS §5; audit B-30). *(non-normative)* A register returned to the host stays live across launches until the host frees it or a `measure` consumes it (ISA §10: "register ids created by the run remain valid unless measured or freed").   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), ISA-10
````

### §6.7 Copy, XOR and in-place rules   [DLT-6.7]

````text
DLT-6.7-P1
  <1>1 [unc] Copying a handle copies a reference.
  <1>2 [unc] Quantum data moves only by:
DLT-6.7-P2
  <1>1 [req] `qload(dst, mem, addr)`: XOR of cell `addr` of `mem` into `dst` (§18.3); a copy when `dst` is clean, the source preserved;   deps: DLT-18.3
DLT-6.7-P3
  <1>1 [req] `qstore(mem, addr, val)`: XOR of `val` into cell `addr` of `mem` (ISA `qstore`), whatever `mem` holds;
DLT-6.7-P4
  <1>1 [unc] `qprep(h, src, n)`: XOR of classical bytes into `h` (§3.2);   deps: DLT-3.2
DLT-6.7-P5
  <1>1 [req] `inplace(g, ginv, budget, desc)`: x ← g(x), admitted only under §8.7;   deps: DLT-8.7
DLT-6.7-P6
  <1>1 [unc] `oracle(f, budget, desc)`: targets ⊕= outputs, fault ⊕= code, inputs preserved (§8).   deps: DLT-8
DLT-6.7-P7
  <1>1 [def] The north star's compile-time rule "a store into a register not known to be clean is an error" (NS §3.1) is the optional warning **`BD-W-QSTORE-UNCLEAN`**: under `-bennett-warn-qstore`, the plugin warns at every `qstore` whose target is not provably clean (no event since its `qalloc` other than a `qstore` of the same `addr` and `val`).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>2 [req] The warning never changes the verdict, so the uncomputing second store stays legal (§1.4).   deps: DLT-1.4
````

### §6.8 Handle → wire obligations   [DLT-6.8]

````text
DLT-6.8-P1
  <1>1 [req] The plugin converts handles to ISA wires (ISA §3.3 linear values) when it lowers.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-3.3
  <1>2 [req] Its obligations, each a property of every ISA object it emits (ISA suite F), are: every gate's wires pairwise distinct; no wire used after its release; every `qalloc`ed bundle released by an explicit `qfree` (ISA §5.1); every control context disjoint from the operands it controls (ISA §3.3); the uncomputation-validity discipline of ISA §6 for every value it introduces.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-5.1, ISA-3.3, ISA-6
  <1>3 [req] Where an obligation depends on run-time data, the reference semantics checks it dynamically and the plugin MUST include the same check (§18.3 traps `T-ALIAS`, `T-DEAD`, `T-LEAK`, `T-CTRL`); `T-CLEAN` is checked as ISA `qreg.free` cleanliness is: by simulating executors, with hardware following ISA §17 item 14.   deps: DLT-18.3, ISA-17-P15
````

````text
DLT-7.1-P7
  <1>1 [req] The marker fixes the region's **interface** (TDL-1, §10).   deps: DLT-10-P2.S1 (TDL-1), DLT-10
  <1>2 [req] The body is partitioned by content (§10) and never rejected for containing classical code; the plugin outlines coherent slices of action bodies (TDL-3).   deps: DLT-10-P14.S1 (TDL-3), DLT-10
  <1>3 [def] An **oracle region** is a lifted classical oracle with output targets and a fault register (ISA §5.2); an **action region** is a quantum action on the wires its events name (ISA §5.3) with no fault outcome of its own: a fault of an oracle it calls lands in a fault register the action declares (§10, TDL-8).   deps: DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8), DLT-8.2-P7.S3 (fault register), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), ISA-5.2, ISA-5.3, DLT-10
````

### §7.2 Candidate sets and dispatch (maintainer decision 3)   [DLT-7.2]

````text
DLT-7.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] The candidate set RR(v) of a region operand v is computed from its abstract value (§5):   deps: DLT-5.1-P12.S1 (abstract value), DLT-5
DLT-7.2-P2
  <1>1 [unc] every `FN(F)` atom of AV(v), resolved by `resolve` below;
DLT-7.2-P3
  <1>1 [req] if AV(v) has the ⊤ flag: in addition every function of the required type of §7.1 (for `oracle`/`inplace`: every function) whose address is used anywhere in the linked program, resolved (decision 3 read literally: "every function whose address can reach a region marker"; audit B-33).   deps: DLT-7.1
  <1>2 [req] The set is therefore always bounded by the program.
DLT-7.2-P4
  <1>1 [def] `resolve(F)` follows aliases to their aliasee and **forwarding thunks** to their target: F is a forwarding thunk of G iff F's body is one block holding exactly a call of G (optionally `tail`/`musttail`/`notail`, default calling convention, no operand bundles) whose arguments are F's parameters in order and whose function type equals F's, followed by `ret void` or `ret` of that call's result; then `resolve(F) = resolve(G)`, except that a thunk on a cycle of thunks resolves to itself (it is an ordinary defined function whose execution recurses until a budget or the host stops it; 0.1's `BD-E-REGION-THUNK` is deleted).
DLT-7.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Dispatch.** The region operand is classical data.
  <1>2 [req] The plugin compiles a body for every candidate.
  <1>3 [req] At run time the operand's value is compared, by the compare tree of §14.2 over the sorted keys, with A(S) of every **source** S (every function or alias that resolves into the candidate set and whose address reaches v, plus every candidate itself); on equality the region `resolve(S)` is entered.   deps: DLT-14.2
  <1>4 [req] No match is the run-time trap `T-REGION` (fault code 4).
  <1>5 [req] A candidate with no definition (an external declaration, a function without bitcode) is entered as a trapped call: F(8) with a coverage line (§4.5 item 4).   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (trapped), DLT-4.5-P5
DLT-7.2-P6
  <1>1 [def] **Ill-formed candidates.** A candidate that is not well-formed for the marker's kind (a type other than §7.1's, or a violation of §7.5) is compiled as a **trap body** (`T-REGION-INVALID`, fault code 4) with a coverage line: it may never be dispatched, so it is not a compile-time error.   deps: DLT-7.1, DLT-7.5
  <1>2 [req] It is rejected (`BD-E-REGION-TYPE`, `BD-E-REGION-MEASURE`, `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE`) only when it is the **unique** candidate, because then every execution of the marker fails (outline E14).   deps: DLT-22.1-P14.S1 (BD-E-REGION-TYPE), DLT-22.1-P15.S1 (BD-E-REGION-MEASURE), DLT-22.1-P16.S1 (BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE)
DLT-7.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] 0.1's `BD-E-REGION-UNBOUNDED` and `BD-E-REGION-EXTERNAL` are deleted.
````

### §7.3 Region identity (maintainer decision 1)   [DLT-7.3]

````text
DLT-7.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] A region is identified by its content, never by its symbol.
  <1>2 [req] Candidates that resolve to the same function are one region.
  <1>3 [def] The semantic identity of an **oracle region** in a shape (§8.4) is the interface hash (ISA §9.2) of `@ref` in its reference object (§16.2): every lproc, data image and layout leaf of the closure is part of it, and `@ref`, `@L<n>`, `@G<n>` are fixed names, so two functions with equal materialised bodies have equal identity whatever their source names.   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), DLT-8.4, ISA-9.2, DLT-16.2
  <1>4 [req] Symbol names, linkage, aliases and thunks are non-normative: diagnostics and coverage entries MAY print them.
  <1>5 [req] Budget lookup uses the separate provisional `selection_identity` and the once-only staging of §9.2, never a fixed point in this final semantic identity.   deps: DLT-9.2-P3.S2 (selection_identity), DLT-9.2
DLT-7.3-P2
  <1>1 [def] The **region key** of any region is the SHA-256 of the text printed by the LLVM printer of the major in use for a module that contains the materialised region function and its closure, after `strip` (local names), with the functions renamed `f0, f1, …` in depth-first preorder from the region along direct calls in instruction order, and without datalayout, triple and module metadata.
  <1>2 [req] It keys the budget table (§9.2) and is recorded in the certificate and coverage entries.   deps: DLT-9.2-P2.S1 (budget table), DLT-9.2
  <1>3 [req] It depends on the LLVM major, which the certificate records (§24, D13).   deps: DLT-24-P3.S1 (D13), DLT-24
````

### §7.4 Kinds by content   [DLT-7.4]

````text
DLT-7.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] NS §4.2: "the plugin classifies each outlined region by its contents and the verifier checks the declared interface".
  <1>2 [unc] After the TDL partition (§10):   deps: DLT-10
DLT-7.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] an action region whose generated events are all permutation events (X-family gates, `qload`, `qstore`, `qexch`, oracle calls, permutation actions) is emitted as an ISA `perm_action` (ISA §5.3), which certificates can verify by T3; otherwise as an `action`;   deps: DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), ISA-5.3
DLT-7.4-P3
  <1>1 [req] an oracle region stays an oracle;
DLT-7.4-P4
  <1>1 [req] a `reversible` marker call reached from an action region's generation or from an L closure is not an R entry: its operand is treated as a direct call of the region function, partitioned by TDL inside actions and lifted inside oracles, with the call's budget record governing its slices (§9.3; audit B-37).   deps: DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), DLT-9.1-P1.S1 (budget record), DLT-9.3
  <1>2 [req] 0.1's `BD-E-REGION-TIER` and `T-TIER` are deleted.
````

### §7.5 Static well-formedness   [DLT-7.5]

````text
DLT-7.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] Only the rules the north star requires:
DLT-7.5-P2
  <1>1 [def] **(a) Measurement in a unitary region.** A `measure` call in the closure of an action region is `BD-E-REGION-MEASURE` (NS §3.1: "user measurement inside it is refused"), unless the module requests **deferred measurement** with the module flag `!{i32 1, !"bennett.measure_in_unitary", !"defer"}` (NS §3.1: "or, when the front end asks for it, deferred by the deferred-measurement principle, which the certificate states").   deps: DLT-22.1-P15.S1 (BD-E-REGION-MEASURE), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region)
  <1>2 [req] Then a `measure(h, i)` inside a generation becomes a `qcopy` of `h.i` onto a fresh wire w (ISA §4.5), the call's `i1` result is a Co value whose wire is w (§10, TDL-2 seed).   deps: DLT-10-P3.S1 (TDL-2), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-4.5, DLT-10
  <1>3 [req] Retain this intentional outcome record until the first permitted CC measurement boundary after the outermost action returns, and measure it there before the next source operation.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>4 [req] Earlier removal requires the complete instrument (outcomes and correlations included) to be unchanged; an open window alone never justifies quncopy.
  <1>5 [req] These channel outputs are separate from clean workspace (TDL-7).   deps: DLT-10-P35.S1 (TDL-7)
  <1>6 [req] Standalone adj uses the deterministic latest-before-reversed-consumer placement of §18.2, including source-wire lifetime extension.   deps: DLT-18.2
  <1>7 [req] The coverage report has a `lowered` entry with source `dialect-0.2.10:7.5a` for every such call, and the certificate lists the id `deferred_measurement` in the region's `definedness_assumptions` (ISA §12.3; §25.2 records the wording item).   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (lowered), ISA-12.3, DLT-25.2
DLT-7.5-P3
  <1>1 [req] **(b) Reversible regions.** The closure of a reversible region MAY contain gate and region markers on handles received through `env`: they lower to R-tier `action_call`/`oracle_call` (ISA §3.1, §4.7), recorded in the R history and reversed by re-application or adjoint (ISA §7.4).   deps: DLT-2-P6.S3 (Reversible region), ISA-3.1, ISA-4.7, ISA-7.4
  <1>2 [req] A `measure` call in the closure is `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE` (ISA §10: a run that measured is not reversible; ISA §3.3: a `measure` frame is never reversed).   deps: DLT-22.1-P16.S1 (BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE), ISA-10, ISA-3.3
  <1>3 [req] A `qalloc`, `qborrow` or `qfree` in the closure is **hoisted** (ISA 0.8 has `qreg.new` in CC only): when every such site s has a finite reference instance bound N(s) (§6.5 rules 1 and 2, applied to every site whether or not it reaches memory, including the entry-boundary liveness precondition with the reversible invocation in place of the device entry), the CC caller allocates N(s) clean registers per site before the R call, passes them as `qreg` arguments, and frees them after it returns; `qalloc` in R selects a free one, `qfree` checks that it is clean (`T-CLEAN`) and returns it to the free set.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-6.5-P8, DLT-6.5-P9
  <1>4 [req] Otherwise the site is a blocker point of BLK-4 (`blocked:BD-B-ISA12`, §25.1).   deps: DLT-25.1-P5.S1 (BLK-4), DLT-6.5-P10.S1 (blocker point), DLT-25.1
DLT-7.5-P4
  <1>1 [req] **(c) In-place pairs** are verified by §8.7.   deps: DLT-8.7
DLT-7.5-P5
  <1>1 [req] Deferred measurement is a channel on adjoint paths as well: the special §18.2 rule creates fresh retained records for standalone adj and retains E_f records through compute.   deps: DLT-18.2
  <1>2 [req] An open window never authorises quncopy of these records.
````

### §7.6 Outlining conventions   [DLT-7.6]

````text
DLT-7.6-P1
  <1>1 [req] Regions are functions on arrival: the front end outlines (a Julia macro, a clang attribute, a Rust closure trampoline); the dialect imposes no source syntax.
  <1>2 [req] The plugin additionally outlines coherent slices (TDL-3), which is not observable beyond §18.   deps: DLT-10-P14.S1 (TDL-3), DLT-18
DLT-7.6-P2
  <1>1 [req] Action and reversible regions have type `void (ptr env)`.
  <1>2 [req] `env` points to classical memory the front end owns (an `alloca` or heap object) that is live for the duration of the marker call; the region reads and writes it as classical memory (§18.4).   deps: DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call), DLT-18.4
  <1>3 [req] Handles reach a region through `env` (PT9 binds a region's parameter to the marker's env argument).   deps: DLT-5.2-P10.S1 (PT9), DLT-2-P3.S1 (Handle)
DLT-7.6-P3
  <1>1 [req] Oracle regions are arbitrary functions bound by a descriptor (§8); no packing wrapper is needed (audit D21).   deps: DLT-2-P6.S2 (Oracle region), DLT-8
DLT-7.6-P4
  <1>1 [req] A region body MUST be referenced by the region operand (directly or through §7.2); a body referenced only from a side-table global is not a region (enzyme survey implication 5).   deps: DLT-7.2
DLT-7.6-P5
  <1>1 [req] Region functions SHOULD have internal linkage, so that they are not device entries.   deps: DLT-3.8-P3.S1 (Region functions)
  <1>2 [req] C++ lambdas pass their static `__invoke` thunk and Rust closures an `extern "C"` trampoline; both are ordinary region functions whose closure contains the closure body.
````

### §8 The oracle interface (oracle-abi 0.2.4)   [DLT-8]

````text
DLT-8-P1
  <1>1 [req] `__bennett_oracle(f, budget, desc)` applies the lifted oracle of region function f, under the budget of §9, to the registers and classical values that the **descriptor** `desc` binds to f's parameters, result and device models.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-9
  <1>2 [req] `__bennett_inplace(g, ginv, budget, desc)` applies an in-place bijection (§8.7).   deps: DLT-8.7
  <1>3 [req] The descriptor is classical data in the caller's memory, built by plain stores (§3.8); its kinds are classical values dispatched over their finite domains (§8.4).   deps: DLT-3.8, DLT-8.4
````

### §8.1 Descriptor layout   [DLT-8.1]

````text
DLT-8.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] The descriptor is the LLVM struct `{ H, [nparam x Bind], [ndev x DevBind] }` laid out by the module's datalayout, where (C view in `bennett_markers.h`, §3.3):   deps: DLT-3.3
DLT-8.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] H | `{ i32, i32, i32, i32, ptr, ptr }` | `version` (= 2), `nparam`, `ndev`, `flags` (= 0), `tgt`, `fault`
DLT-8.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] Bind | `{ i32, i32, ptr, i64, i64 }` | `kind`, `mode`, `q`, `value`, `size`
DLT-8.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] DevBind | `{ i32, i32, ptr, i64 }` | `model`, `mode` (= 0), `q`, `value`
DLT-8.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] On x86-64, AArch64 and RISC-V64 (pw = 64) H is 32 bytes, Bind 32 bytes (`kind` at 0, `mode` 4, `q` 8, `value` 16, `size` 24) and DevBind 24 bytes, so `param[i]` is at 32 + 32i and `dev[j]` at 32 + 32·nparam + 24j.
  <1>2 [req] The plugin reads the descriptor at the marker call, in the caller's tier, with ordinary loads.   deps: DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call)
  <1>3 [req] `nparam` MUST equal the number of flattened scalar fields of the dispatched region function after N21/N22 and ABI lowering, using §8.2 carriers (one field for a scalar).   deps: DLT-12.2-P23.S1 (N21), DLT-12.2-P24.S1 (N22), DLT-8.2
  <1>4 [req] A descriptor whose `version`, `flags`, `nparam` or any kind, mode or model is not one this section defines, or whose binding does not fit the parameter's type (below), traps (`T-IFACE`, fault code 4) when the marker is called.
````

### §8.2 Parameter bindings   [DLT-8.2]

````text
DLT-8.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] Let the dispatched region function have IR parameters p_0 … p_{m−1} and return type R (`void` or a first-class type of at most 128 payload bits after N25, §12.2).   deps: DLT-12.2-P27.S1 (N25), DLT-12.2
  <1>2 [unc] Before binding, represent each floating-point scalar by its §13.1 integer carrier (half/bfloat i16, float i32, double i64, x86_fp80 i80, fp128/ppc_fp128 i128).   deps: DLT-13.1
  <1>3 [unc] IN and ARG bind carrier bits.
  <1>4 [req] Flatten aggregate/vector parameters depth-first in field/lane order, one Bind per scalar field; apply N21/N22 first.   deps: DLT-12.2-P23.S1 (N21), DLT-12.2-P24.S1 (N22)
  <1>5 [unc] `param[i]` binds flattened field i.
  <1>6 [req] Results concatenate fields in the same order, field 0 in the lowest bits, no padding; width is the sum of scalar carrier widths.
  <1>7 [unc] N25 demotes results whose packed width exceeds 128 bits.   deps: DLT-12.2-P27.S1 (N25)
  <1>8 [req] Memory accesses retain datalayout padding; this padding is never part of the packed return payload:
DLT-8.2-P2
  <1>1 [req] `IN` | 1 | `iW` or `ptr addrspace(a)` (W = P(a)) | q with n(q) ≤ W | the parameter's value is the basis value of q, zero-extended (`mode` = ZEXT 0) or sign-extended (`mode` = SEXT 1) to W; a `ptr` parameter may be `IN` (a superposed pointer) | an `ins` bundle `x<k>:wire[n(q)]` (a pseudo-location, ISA §3.7), extended by `lift.zext`/`lift.sext` in the wrapper   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-3.7
DLT-8.2-P3
  <1>1 [req] `ARG` | 2 | `iW` (W ≤ 128) or `ptr` | none (`q` ignored) | a classical value: bits 0–63 from `value`, bits 64–127 from `size`; a `ptr` ARG is an address in the caller's classical memory, which the oracle reads through its entry state (§8.5) | an `args` entry `a<k>:i<W>` (W = P(a) for `ptr addrspace(a)`; ISA §4.7, §5.1)   deps: DLT-8.5, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.1
DLT-8.2-P4
  <1>1 [req] `MEM_IN` | 3 | `ptr` | q with n(q) = 8·2^A, `size` = 2^A | the parameter is the base address of an interface region of 2^A bytes whose cell i is byte i of q (bits [8i, 8i + 8)); its contents may be superposed; role `in` (restored on every basis configuration, ISA §5.1).   deps: ISA-5.1
  <1>2 [req] This is the quantum-addressed memory of NS §8 rung 4 | a `mem` region `m<k>:region[A,8] in`; the argument is `lift.addr @m<k>`
DLT-8.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] `OUT` | 4 | `ptr` | q with n(q) = 8·`size` | the parameter points to a fresh zero-filled buffer of `size` bytes in the oracle's memory; at exit its final bytes are XORed into q, byte k into wires [8k, 8k + 8) (a declared output, NS §4.2) | `lift.alloc size` in the wrapper; the bytes are part of the payload (§8.6)   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-8.6
DLT-8.2-P6
  <1>1 [req] `INOUT` | 5 | `iW` | q with n(q) = W | `inplace` only (§8.7): the in-place register x | `qinplace … x`   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-8.7
DLT-8.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] The **result**: a non-null `tgt` register receives the return value: payload bits = the return value zero-extended to n(tgt) if n(tgt) ≥ R's width; if n(tgt) is narrower, the dropped bits are checked and an execution on which they are nonzero faults with code 4 (`FAULT_UB_TRAP`); the retained bits are the low n(tgt).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>2 [req] A null `tgt` discards the return value.
  <1>3 [req] For `oracle`, the **fault register** `fault` MUST be a register of exactly 4 wires (ISA §5.4: F = 4) and MUST NOT be null (`T-IFACE`).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-5.4
  <1>4 [req] Null `tgt` remains valid for a `void` function or a discarded non-void return.
  <1>5 [req] If there is no `OUT` or non-null `CONSOLE` output either, the shape has zero payload outputs; it still executes once and delivers its fault through §8.6's synthetic variant.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-8.6
  <1>6 [req] Every register named by the descriptor MUST be live and pairwise distinct (`T-DEAD`, `T-ALIAS`).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>7 [req] A width mismatch in the table above is `T-SIZE`.
````

### §8.3 Device bindings   [DLT-8.3]

````text
DLT-8.3-P1
  <1>1 [unc] `dev[j]` binds a device model for the lifted (coherent) tier (§11.3):   deps: DLT-11.3
DLT-8.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] `ENTROPY` | 1 | q with n(q) = 8·2^A | an `in` region `e0:region[A,8]` holding the entropy tape, possibly superposed (NS §8 rung 8: "the entropy device is replaced by a superposed register")   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
DLT-8.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] `BLOCK` | 2 | q with n(q) = 8·512·2^A′ | an `in` region `k0` holding the disk image of 2^A′ sectors; writes go to a lifted copy-on-write overlay in the oracle's heap
DLT-8.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] `CLOCK` | 3 | none | the clock model's initial value is `value`
DLT-8.3-P5
  <1>1 [req] `CONSOLE` | 4 | q with n(q) = 8·L, or null | the first L bytes the oracle writes to the console are XORed into q as an output (payload, §8.6); with a null q console bytes are discarded with the oracle's other writes   deps: DLT-8.6
DLT-8.3-P6
  <1>1 [unc] At most one binding per model.
  <1>2 [req] A model without a binding is **unbound**: an effect on it in L code faults with code 8 on the executions that reach it (§11.3; NS §4.2).   deps: DLT-11.3
````

### §8.4 Shapes and dispatch   [DLT-8.4]

````text
DLT-8.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] A **shape** is the tuple (region candidate F; for each parameter its kind, mode, register width n(q) and `size`; n(tgt) or "none"; for each device binding its model and n(q)).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>2 [req] The kind, mode, model, `size` and `value` fields have value sets (§5.3); register widths come from the register file (§6.5), whose roots have known widths.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-5.3, DLT-6.5
  <1>3 [req] The plugin compiles one reference object (§16.2) per shape that the value sets allow, taking every unbounded kind or mode field over its finite domain (5 kinds, 2 modes, 4 models), so the number of shapes is always bounded.   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-16.2
  <1>4 [req] At run time the caller's tier reads the descriptor and selects the compiled shape with the compare tree of §14.2; a descriptor that matches no compiled shape traps (`T-IFACE`), which happens only for a field outside its table.   deps: DLT-14.2
  <1>5 [req] `value` and `size` of `ARG` bindings are the oracle's classical arguments and are not part of the shape. *(non-normative)* With one valid value per shape field there is one shape per candidate.
  <1>6 [req] Initial zero possibilities still participate in §5; kind 0 is outside the five-kind table and takes T-IFACE rather than creating an additional valid shape.   deps: DLT-5
DLT-8.4-P2
  <1>1 [def] The marker then issues, in the caller's tier, one **oracle invocation** (§8.6) whose operands are the static registers the descriptor's handles denote (§6.5), the budget token (§9.5) and the classical arguments.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-8.6, DLT-6.5, DLT-9.5
````

### §8.5 Entry state   [DLT-8.5]

````text
DLT-8.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] **Reference semantics.** The region function runs on a **private copy of the caller's whole classical memory at the call**, with the bound inputs; its writes are discarded except through `OUT`, `tgt` and the bound device outputs (NS §4.2: "every other write being restored workspace").
  <1>2 [req] Constant globals, and mutable globals that only the oracle's own L closure writes, equal their initialisers at every call.
DLT-8.5-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Realisation on ISA 0.8.** Let R be the abstract objects the oracle closure may load (§5.4), minus constant globals and mutable globals written by no function outside the L closure.   deps: DLT-5.1-P1.S1 (abstract objects), DLT-5.4
  <1>2 [req] Translate source read-set objects after normalisation to runtime backing allocations.
  <1>3 [req] If R includes a caller alloca/frame, include its live shadow-stack backing storage and metadata, bytes and addresses.
  <1>4 [req] If reachability is uncertain, include all live caller heap/stack backing allocations.
  <1>5 [req] Whenever the L closure uses the shadow stack, add its state globals to G and its block to Hb with current base, size and stack pointer, even without a source heap object.
  <1>6 [req] Include the logical frame/allocation table of §17.3, dynamic extents, liveness and save/restore state in G whenever stack access or stack operations can execute.   deps: DLT-17.3
  <1>7 [req] Replay these bytes before private allocations/accesses and let ss_init adopt them; a live backing entry alone does not recreate live program frames.
  <1>8 [req] All such metadata mutations invalidate snapshot reuse independently of the byte read set.
  <1>9 [req] Allocator metadata is an independent entry-state dependency: allocation, deallocation, allocation-validity checks and observation of allocation-derived addresses read it even without an LLVM load.
  <1>10 [req] Replay the complete caller allocation history (sizes and live bits) whenever any can execute, including when the byte read set R is empty.
  <1>11 [req] Bytes are included only for byte dependencies; the sound default is the complete table, with a smaller proven dependency optional.
  <1>12 [req] Only when both byte and allocator-state dependencies are absent may transfer be omitted and globals start at their images (ISA §3.7), as in rung 4 route A (SQLite §5.2(i)).   deps: ISA-3.7
  <1>13 [req] After replay heap_used, atop and stack-state values equal the caller's call-entry values.
  <1>14 [def] Otherwise the plugin emits an **entry-state transfer**:
DLT-8.5-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Snapshot.** At the call the caller's tier forms the byte string S = k ‖ (size_i ‖ live_i)_{i<k} ‖ G ‖ Hb, where k is an 8-byte little-endian count of heap allocations in the caller's allocation table (in allocation order, ISA §7.1), `size_i` and `live_i` are 8-byte little-endian fields (`live_i` is 0 or 1), G is the bytes of the mutable globals in R in `layout.globals` order, and Hb is the bytes of every live heap allocation in allocation order if R contains a heap object or shadow-stack backing allocation (else empty); each live Hb segment has exactly size_i bytes, even for size_i=0.   deps: ISA-7.1
  <1>2 [req] S is zero-padded to 2^A_s bytes, where A_s is the smallest exponent with 2^A_s ≥ 8 + 16·N_alloc + Σ|G| + `heap_limit_max` (N_alloc the allocation-table capacity of the layout, §19.5); A_s is static.   deps: DLT-19.5
  <1>3 [req] The snapshot bytes S are assembled in a plugin-added unnamed CC global of 2^A_s bytes, zero-filled before every build; thus all 16-byte leaf loads, including padding, are valid.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
DLT-8.5-P4
  <1>1 [def] **Register.** The CC tier builds a register of 8·2^A_s wires holding S with a **concat tree** of A_s − 3 static-width CC functions `B_j` (j = 4, …, A_s): `B_4(p)` executes `qreg.new any 128`, loads the 16 bytes at p as two `i64` values and makes the `oracle_call` of the runtime oracle `@xor128` (§16.5) that XORs them into the register (with a token minted at launch for budget (6, 0, 0, 0) and a fresh 4-wire fault register, which stays clean and is freed); `B_j(p)` (j > 4) returns `qreg.concat B_{j−1}(p) B_{j−1}(p + 2^{j−1})`.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-8.2-P7.S3 (fault register), DLT-16.5
  <1>2 [req] Every type is static and the cost is 2^(A_s−4) region calls.
  <1>3 [req] The register is bound as an extra `in` region `s0:region[A_s,8]`.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
DLT-8.5-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Replay.** The wrapper prologue (§16.2) calls the runtimes function `__bennett_rt_entry_replay(s0)`, which for i < k executes `__bennett_nz_alloc(size_i)` (ISA `lift.alloc`) and, if `live_i` = 0, `__bennett_nz_free` of the result.   deps: DLT-16.2
  <1>2 [req] ISA §4.2 `lift.alloc` and ISA §7.1 `alloc` share the rule "bump + max(n, 1) from `heap_base`", so the replay reproduces the caller's heap addresses exactly (audit O-07).   deps: ISA-4.2, ISA-7.1
  <1>3 [unc] It then copies G into the globals (by the driver-generated table `__bennett_rt_entry_table` of their addresses and sizes) and Hb into the replayed live allocations.
DLT-8.5-P6
  <1>1 [req] **Release.** The snapshot register holds the basis state |S⟩ and is unentangled after every oracle call (an `in` region is unchanged on every basis configuration, ISA §5.2).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), ISA-5.2
  <1>2 [req] The caller keeps it for later calls only while its dependent bytes and allocator metadata are unchanged.
  <1>3 [req] Relevant alloc/free and shadow-stack push/pop/restore invalidate it as well as stores to R, and releases it by unpreparation: retain the classical S for the register's lifetime, take successive slice views of at most 128 bits, invoke the same @xor128 preparation oracle with the original two i64 arguments on each 128-bit chunk (one view per call), then qreg.free the now-clean whole register.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>4 [req] A_s ≥ 4, so every leaf is exactly 128 bits.
  <1>5 [req] No whole-register measurement, view measurement or un-concat operation is needed.
DLT-8.5-P7
  <1>1 [req] The replay's rows, allocations and calls are part of the reference body and count against the budget (§9.4).   deps: DLT-9.4
  <1>2 [req] Entry-state transfer from R-tier code needs registers allocated in R (BLK-4, §25.1); no rung uses it.   deps: DLT-25.1-P5.S1 (BLK-4), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-25.1
  <1>3 [def] **Build-time snapshots** (rung 7, §19.6) rewrite the initialisers of the mutable globals to snapshot values at link time, so byte transfer may be empty; allocator dependencies still require replay.   deps: DLT-19.6
  <1>4 [unc] Efficiency: G-12 (§25.3).   deps: DLT-25.3
````

### §8.6 Oracle invocations and wide outputs   [DLT-8.6]

````text
DLT-8.6-P1
  <1>1 [req] The **outputs** of a shape are, in order: `tgt` (if non-null), each `OUT` parameter in parameter order, and the `CONSOLE` register (if bound and non-null).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>2 [req] Each output register is split into **slices** of at most 128 bits from its low end (ISA §3.3: payloads are `iN` with N ≤ 128); the slices of all outputs are numbered 0, 1, …, k − 1 in that order.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), ISA-3.3
  <1>3 [req] Every slice j has an oracle **variant** W_j: the same reference body (§16.2) whose `lift.retval` delivers slice j of the payload.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-16.2
  <1>4 [req] Every W_j executes an identical ordered computation and validation of every projection, with identical row charges and fault conditions; only its last lift.retval operand differs.
  <1>5 [req] Thus all variants compute the same fault code, even when budget expires in delivery, and all copy-outs are zero on any common failure.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>6 [req] For a single slice, retain its existing rows and return directly without an extra projection move.
DLT-8.6-P2
  <1>1 [def] **k = 0** (no payload outputs): there is one synthetic variant **W_empty**, with T = 1.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>2 [req] It executes the same prologue, region body and unwind check as other variants; on success its terminal rows are `p0 = lift.const.i1 0` and `lift.retval p0` (§16.2), charged normally.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-16.2
  <1>3 [req] The caller allocates one clean hidden target bit, invokes `oracle_call @W_empty tk (ins..) hidden fault (mem..) (args..)` exactly once using the caller's required four-wire fault register, then frees the hidden target.   deps: DLT-8.6-P2.S1 (W_empty), DLT-8.2-P7.S3 (fault register)
  <1>4 [req] The hidden bit remains zero on success and fault; it is workspace, not a payload output.
  <1>5 [req] Under invocation controls, predicate the call on all of them; allocation and free remain unconditional.
  <1>6 [req] Do not issue a second call to clean the hidden target: that would XOR away the delivered fault code.
DLT-8.6-P3
  <1>1 [req] **k = 1** (every output fits 128 bits in one register): the invocation is `oracle_call @W_0 tk (ins..) tgt fault (mem..) (args..)`.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
DLT-8.6-P4
  <1>1 [unc] **k > 1** (the `wide` event, §18.2): the caller's tier issues, for j = k − 1, …, 1, with slice j at bits [lo_j, lo_j + w_j) of output register o_j:   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-18.2
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 8 lines) t_j = qreg.new any w_j ; f_j = qreg.new any 4
DLT-8.6-P5
  <1>1 [unc] followed by `s_0 = qreg.slice o_0 lo_0 w_0` and `oracle_call @W_0 tk (ins..) s_0 fault (mem..) (args..)`.
  <1>2 [req] `@xor<w>` is the permutation action `(mut s:wire[w]) (in t:wire[w]) () ()` with body `qxor t s`; each slice view is the operand of exactly one region call (ISA §3.3).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-3.3
DLT-8.6-P6
  <1>1 [req] Each copy-out pair computes slice j into fresh wires, XORs it into the target and uncomputes it by the identical self-adjoint call, whose inputs no call in between modifies, so `t_j` and `f_j` are clean when freed (ISA §5.2 involution; the ISA §3.3 cleanliness check of `qreg.free`); slice 0 and the fault code are delivered by the last call.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-5.2, ISA-3.3
  <1>2 [req] The effect is exactly NS's F_B with a wide payload: slice_j ⊕= payload_j(F_B(x)) for every j and fault ⊕= code(F_B(x)).
  <1>3 [req] Cost: 2k − 1 oracle calls (efficiency item G-15, §25.3).   deps: DLT-1.3-P1.S3 (efficiency item), DLT-25.3
  <1>4 [req] Under a `ctrl` every region call is `ctl`-prefixed (§18.2).   deps: DLT-18.2
````

### §8.7 In-place updates   [DLT-8.7]

````text
DLT-8.7-P1
  <1>1 [req] `inplace(g, ginv, budget, desc)` binds the in-place register x as `INOUT` (exactly one parameter of each candidate pair is `INOUT`, of type `iN` with N = n(x); the result type is `iN`). g and ginv are oracles in the sense of §8.2 with a **payload equal to the new value of x**: their reference objects (§16.2) bind x as an `in` bundle and have an n(x)-bit target.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), DLT-8.2, DLT-16.2
  <1>2 [req] The descriptor's `tgt` MUST be null and its `fault` is ignored (`T-IFACE` otherwise): an in-place update has no fault outcome (NS §4.2), and the `qinplace` expansion allocates its own clean scratch (ISA §4.5).   deps: ISA-4.5
DLT-8.7-P2
  <1>1 [req] **No other bindings**: the call lowers to ISA `qinplace @g @ginv tk x` (ISA §4.5) with @g, @ginv the reference oracles of the pair.   deps: ISA-4.5
DLT-8.7-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Classical `ARG`s with finite value sets**: one `qinplace` per tuple of argument values, each with the pair specialised to those constants (the arguments become `lift.const` in the reference bodies), dispatched by §14.2.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-14.2
DLT-8.7-P4
  <1>1 [def] **Library bijections**: when the pair is a `bennett-arith` library bijection (add or subtract a value, xor, rotate, negate, byte swap, modular addition, modular multiplication by a unit, each with its stated conditions and its g∘ginv = id certificate, NS §3.1), the call lowers to the library's permutation action with `IN`, `MEM_IN` and `ARG` bindings as its `in`, `mem` and `args` operands, which ISA 0.8 expresses directly (ISA §4.7 `action_call`).   deps: ISA-4.7
  <1>2 [req] This is also the TDL-5(b) mechanism (§10).   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5), DLT-10
DLT-8.7-P5
  <1>1 [req] **User pairs with `IN`, `MEM_IN` or arbitrary `ARG`s**: allocate clean n(x)-wire t and 4-wire f at the CC boundary.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>2 [unc] Issue oracle_call @g tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..), exchange x and t by a swap action, then oracle_call @ginv tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..), and qreg.free t and f.
  <1>3 [req] Preserved parameter bindings are identical in both calls.
  <1>4 [req] Under control, predicate every region call including the swap; allocation/free remain unconditional.
  <1>5 [req] This is a CC-level expansion like §8.6, not an assertion that a static action passes ISA §6's coarse provenance windows.   deps: DLT-8.6, ISA-6
  <1>6 [req] Universal inverse and fault-free budget obligations below guarantee clean scratch on every basis state, hence on superpositions.
  <1>7 [req] Parameterised qinplace is efficiency only (§25.3); BLK-3 is retired.   deps: DLT-25.3
DLT-8.7-P6
  <1>1 [req] **Verification.** For every compiled pair and every argument tuple, when N ≤ the configured width (driver option `-bennett-inplace-exhaustive-bits`, default 24, §24), the plugin MUST run the reference interpreter on both reference bodies with an unbounded budget and check that for every x ∈ [0, 2^N), F^g(x) = (y, 0) and F^ginv(y) = (x, 0); it records the **minimal budget** B_min, the componentwise maximum over all those runs of (rows, heap bytes, stack cells, depth).   deps: DLT-24
  <1>2 [req] Above that width it MUST record a `sampled` evidence claim (ISA §12.1) of the same check and B_sample over the sample as an observed lower bound only, and the dynamic net applies: a wrong inverse leaves the scratch register of the `qinplace` expansion non-clean, which `T-CLEAN` catches (NS §3.1).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), ISA-12.1
  <1>3 [req] F_B is monotone in the budget (a run that completes within B completes identically within every B′ ≥ B), so the pair is valid for exactly B ≥ B_min only when the check is exhaustive over the full input/parameter domain.
  <1>4 [req] Sampling cannot establish B_min or universal invertibility.
  <1>5 [unc] In all sampled cases validity assumes mutually inverse total functions and a sound bound B_bound covering every input and parameter value.
  <1>6 [req] A library or producer-supplied bound is permitted with an explicit correctness obligation recorded in the certificate; no mechanised proof format is required.
  <1>7 [req] B_sample never substitutes for B_bound, and T-CLEAN is not a universal budget guarantee.
  <1>8 [req] Use B_bound in the following budget checks for sampled pairs.
  <1>9 [req] A pair that fails the check, or whose budget is static and below B_min in some component, is `BD-E-INPLACE-NOT-INVERSE` (NS §3.1: "an in-place update with no inverse fails loud at compile time"; "a fallible or budget-limited computation cannot be in-place"); a run-time budget (§9.5) below B_min traps at the marker (`T-BUDGET`). *(non-normative)* On a finite set, ginv∘g = id makes g injective, hence a bijection, so g∘ginv = id and ginv is fault-free on every input, as ISA `qinplace` requires.   deps: DLT-22.1-P18.S1 (BD-E-INPLACE-NOT-INVERSE), DLT-9.5
  <1>10 [req] 0.1's `BD-E-INPLACE-UNVERIFIED` is deleted.
````

````text
  <1>1 [ex] Round-1 wrapper boundary vectors.

  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 5 lines) OUT[20]=11 repeated 20 times; targets initially 00 repeated 20 times

  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 4 lines) sample misses rare identity long path needing 1000 steps; B_sample=10 is lower b…
DLT-8.8-P4

  <1>2 [ex] The descriptor and budget record are built by merged constant stores, as `default<O3>` emits them; the budget record asks for 5 steps, no heap, 7 stack cells and depth 1 (§9.1; §16.6 computes these numbers):   deps: DLT-9.1-P1.S1 (budget record), DLT-9.1, DLT-16.6
````

### §9 Budgets   [DLT-9]

````text
DLT-9-P1
  <1>1 [req] NS §3.1: a region-level classical budget of "dynamic steps, heap bytes and recursion depth, passed as ordinary integer operands of the region-entry marker or supplied out of band by the host or driver (a project-wide default, a per-function table) … No per-loop source annotation is ever required".
  <1>2 [req] Budgets are the ISA's four components (ISA §5.4).   deps: ISA-5.4
````

### §9.1 The budget record   [DLT-9.1]

````text
DLT-9.1-P1
  <1>1 [def] Every region-entry marker takes a `ptr` to a **budget record** (outline E1, E12), the LLVM struct `{ i64, i64, i64, i64, ptr }` under the datalayout (C `bennett_budget`, §3.3):   deps: DLT-3.3
DLT-9.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] `steps` | B: reference-interpreter rows (§9.4)   deps: DLT-9.4
DLT-9.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] `heap` | `heap_limit`: heap bytes (ISA §5.4)   deps: ISA-5.4
DLT-9.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] `stack` | `stack_limit`: lifted stack cells (ISA §3.7)   deps: ISA-3.7
DLT-9.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] `depth` | `depth_limit`: call depth
DLT-9.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] `fault` | action regions only: the caller's fault register (4 wires) that receives the combined fault of the region's coherent slices (§10, TDL-8), or null   deps: DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), DLT-8.2-P7.S3 (fault register), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-10
DLT-9.1-P7
  <1>1 [req] A field equal to `BENNETT_BUDGET_TABLE` (all ones) takes its value from the budget table (§9.2).   deps: DLT-9.2-P2.S1 (budget table), DLT-9.2
  <1>2 [req] A null record means all four budget fields come from the table and `fault` is null.
  <1>3 [req] For `oracle` and `inplace` the `fault` field is ignored: the descriptor names the fault register (§8.2).   deps: DLT-8.2-P7.S3 (fault register), DLT-8.2
````

### §9.2 The budget table   [DLT-9.2]

````text
DLT-9.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] The budget-table JSON schema version remains 0.2; it is separate from full dialect 0.2.10.
DLT-9.2-P2
  <1>1 [def] The **budget table** is a driver input (`--bennett-budgets=<file>`), one JSON document in RFC 8785 canonical form:
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 6 lines) { "version": "0.2",
DLT-9.2-P3
  <1>1 [unc] with D a decimal string.
  <1>2 [def] Selectors are `identity` (a provisional shape interface hash, **selection_identity**, defined below), `region_key` (§7.3), or a source-symbol glob (`*` and `?`).   deps: DLT-7.3
  <1>3 [req] Resolution is staged and MUST NOT seek a fixed point:
DLT-9.2-P4
  <1>1 [req] Resolve baseline values per region, shape and component, ignoring identity entries: the first matching region-key entry naming that component, else the first matching symbol entry naming it, else the default.
  <1>2 [req] Missing defaults come from `--bennett-budget-default=<s,h,st,d,ss>`, then 1048576 steps, 1048576 heap bytes, 65536 stack cells, depth 256 and `sstack` 65536 (§24).   deps: DLT-24
  <1>3 [req] `sstack` is shadow-stack bytes (§17.3), part of the heap budget.   deps: DLT-17.3
DLT-9.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] Freeze the link's layout capacities (§16.3) using componentwise maxima of all effective defaults, every raw table entry (including unmatched identity entries), and non-sentinel statically supplied budget fields (the all-ones sentinel is a lookup request, not a capacity).   deps: DLT-16.3
  <1>2 [unc] Neither lookup nor final-object generation shrinks or recomputes these capacities.
DLT-9.2-P6
  <1>1 [unc] Construct each shape's provisional reference object under that frozen layout, with baseline `sstack` and other baseline wrapper inputs.   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object)
  <1>2 [req] Its interface hash is `selection_identity`.   deps: DLT-9.2-P3.S2 (selection_identity)
DLT-9.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] For each component overlay the first identity entry naming that component whose selector equals `selection_identity`, else keep its baseline value.   deps: DLT-9.2-P3.S2 (selection_identity)
  <1>2 [def] Apply this overlay **once only**.
  <1>3 [req] Generate the final object using the resolved values; its hash is the semantic identity of §7.3.   deps: DLT-7.3
  <1>4 [unc] Never rematch against this final hash.
DLT-9.2-P8
  <1>1 [req] Explicit marker-record fields and table sentinels retain §9.1/§9.5 precedence: only table-sourced components use this lookup; non-sentinel explicit fields stay explicit.   deps: DLT-9.1, DLT-9.5
  <1>2 [req] Provisionally and finally, the same explicit fields are applied after table lookup.
  <1>3 [req] The resolved table and build record (§19.9) record per shape both `selection_identity` and final `identity`, the resolved components, frozen capacities and the resolved-table SHA-256.   deps: DLT-19.9-P16.S1 (build record), DLT-9.2-P3.S2 (selection_identity), DLT-19.9
  <1>4 [req] Launch tokens bind the **final** object identity and resolved budget tuple, never the provisional hash.
  <1>5 [unc] A steps-only overlay leaves the object hash unchanged but changes its token.
  <1>6 [req] Multiple shapes resolve separately.
  <1>7 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"defaults":{"depth":"64","heap":"0","sstack":"0","stack":"1024","steps":"100000…
DLT-9.2-P9
  <1>1 [non] *(non-normative)* This is the budget table of the reduced rung-4 link of §16.7: the entry names the region function `q` by symbol; the defaults govern every other region of that link.   deps: DLT-9.2-P2.S1 (budget table), DLT-16.7
  <1>2 [non] Its largest heap (0) and stack (1024) fix that link's `heap_limit_max` and `S_cells` (§16.3); the entry's 400 steps, 512 cells and depth 8 cover the 112 steps, 126 cells and depth 4 that the runs of §16.7 need.   deps: DLT-16.3, DLT-16.7
DLT-9.2-P10
  <1>1 [req] **Staged identity witness (`budget-stage`).** At O0 the 24-byte alloca survives on LLVM 18 and 23.
  <1>2 [req] N24 emits one 24-byte frame; with its 8-byte saved-sp header, the linked runtime below needs 32 bytes.   deps: DLT-12.2-P26.S1 (N24)
  <1>3 [req] With all layout inputs fixed, baseline sstack=16 gives H16; an entry keyed by H16 supplies 32 once, producing H32.

  <1>6 [req] The source pair is N24's canonical output; `budget-stage-runtime` appends those fixed helper definitions before mapping.   deps: DLT-12.2-P26.S1 (N24)
  <1>7 [req] The checked state pins both hashes, resolved fields, final wrapper and final-object token outcomes.
  <1>8 [req] Inputs are x=7.
  <1>9 [ex] (code block, 8 lines) define i8 @f(i8 %x) {
  <1>10 [ex] (code block, 14 lines) define i8 @f(i8 %x) {
  <1>11 [ex] (code block, 73 lines) define i8 @f(i8 %x) {
````
