````text
  <1>13 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"baseline_run":{"code":2,"payload":0,"steps":43},"capacities":{"depth":16,"heap…
````

### §9.3 What each budget governs   [DLT-9.3]

````text
DLT-9.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] `oracle`, `inplace` | the token of every oracle invocation (ISA §5.4, §10 `bennett_budget`) | F_B = (0, `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` / `_HEAP` / `_DEPTH`) on that input (ISA §5.2)   deps: DLT-8.4-P2.S1 (oracle invocation), ISA-5.4, ISA-10, ISA-5.2
DLT-9.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] `ctrl`, `adj`, `compute` | generation G has its full record; **every coherent slice** separately gets the full token record (TDL-9, outline E2) | generation or slice code, combined into the action fault output (TDL-8)   deps: DLT-10-P37.S1 (TDL-9), DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8)
DLT-9.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] `reversible`, and the whole-program R mode | an R-tier step, heap and depth counter (§17.6) | the R run ends with a budget fault record (§17.6)   deps: DLT-17.6
DLT-9.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] device CC code outside regions | none: classical control is "genuinely dynamic" (NS §4.2) | –   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
````

### §9.4 The budget unit   [DLT-9.4]

````text
DLT-9.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] Generation G uses the materialised-instruction unit and pre-instruction checks of TDL-9; each coherent slice separately uses the reference-row unit below.   deps: DLT-10-P37.S1 (TDL-9), DLT-2-P13.S4 (Generation)
  <1>2 [req] R uses §17.6.   deps: DLT-17.6
DLT-9.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] One step is one reference-interpreter row of the reference object (ISA §14.1): the wrapper of §16.2 (including the entry-state replay of §8.5 and the shadow-stack setup of §17.3) and every lproc row, so the fault set is a property of the program, the budget, the LLVM version and pipeline, the normaliser and mapping versions and the runtimes version, never of the plugin's lowering (NS §4.2).   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), ISA-14.1, DLT-16.2, DLT-8.5, DLT-17.3
  <1>2 [req] Heap bytes are counted by `lift.alloc` (`heap_used + max(n, 1)`), stack cells by `lift.call` (R_f cells per call, ISA §3.7, monotonic), depth by `lift.call`/`lift.ret`.   deps: ISA-3.7
  <1>3 [req] The R-tier unit is §17.6.   deps: DLT-17.6
````

### §9.5 Run-time budgets   [DLT-9.5]

````text
DLT-9.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] A budget field of a record is resolved, in this order:
DLT-9.5-P2
  <1>1 [unc] **Static**: a constant, `BENNETT_BUDGET_TABLE`, or a finite value set (§5.3).   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-5.3
  <1>2 [req] The driver's launch stub mints one token per (reference object, budget tuple) with `bennett_budget` (ISA §10) at launch and passes them to the CC entry as `TOKEN` arguments; the CC code selects one by the compare tree of §14.2.   deps: DLT-19.2-P2.S1 (launch stub), DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), ISA-10, DLT-14.2
DLT-9.5-P3
  <1>1 [def] **Launch-supplied**: a field whose abstract value is a bitwise copy of an integer parameter of the device entry (its atoms come only from PT11 for that parameter).   deps: DLT-5.2-P12.S1 (PT11), DLT-5.1-P12.S1 (abstract value)
  <1>2 [req] The launch stub mints the token from the host's argument value before `bennett_run`, for every reference object the record may reach (a finite set), and passes it as a `TOKEN` argument.   deps: DLT-19.2-P2.S1 (launch stub), DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object)
  <1>3 [req] A value that `bennett_budget` rejects (ISA §5.4 validation) makes the stub fail the launch with that status.   deps: ISA-5.4
DLT-9.5-P4
  <1>1 [def] **Device-computed**: any other field (arithmetic on inputs, values derived from measurement results).
  <1>2 [req] ISA 0.8 has no instruction that mints a token from a device value: the verdict is `blocked:BD-B-ISA1` (BLK-2, §25.1), naming the field.   deps: DLT-25.1-P3.S1 (BLK-2), DLT-25.1
DLT-9.5-P5
  <1>1 [req] Exhaustion is always a fault outcome on the inputs that exhaust it (NS §3.1, §4.2), never a compile error and never a truncated result.
  <1>2 [req] Rounding a run-time budget to a pre-minted value is not permitted: it would change F_B (outline B5).
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 38 lines) define internal i8 @inc8(i8 %x) {
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 64 lines) define internal i8 @inc8(i8 %x) {
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) BD-B-ISA1 @run call @__bennett_oracle(@inc8): budget field steps = 5 + a 64-bit …   deps: DLT-25.1-P3.S1 (BLK-2)
DLT-9.5-P6
  <1>1 [non] *(non-normative)* In `budget-device-computed` the oracle's budget depends on measurement outcomes: NS §4.2 "run-time budgets come from the host or from measurement results".
  <1>2 [non] `%acc2` is a loop phi over 64 measured bits, so its value set is ⊤ (it has 2^64 possible values, beyond K = 4096 atoms); it is neither static nor a bitwise copy of a launch argument, and no launch-time set of tokens covers it.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set)
  <1>3 [non] Each wire of `%c` measured 1 is reset by an `x`, so `%c` is clean at its `qfree` (ISA §3.3).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-3.3
````

### §10 Taint-driven lifting under control (tdl 0.2.4)   [DLT-10]

````text
DLT-10-P1
  <1>1 [unc] NS §0: "a way to condition a region on a qubit.
  <1>2 [req] Everything else is inferred"; NS §3.1 taint row: a context-sensitive interprocedural analysis "over data dependence, control dependence and memory effects, from marker results and region entries … with polyvariant specialisation".
  <1>3 [req] This section replaces 0.1's generation-only semantics, in which classical stores of a `ctrl` body happened on both branches of the control (audit 1.4-d, R1).
  <1>4 [req] Rule ids TDL-1 to TDL-12 are normative anchors.   deps: DLT-10-P2.S1 (TDL-1), DLT-10-P47.S1 (TDL-12)
  <1>5 [req] ISA 0.8 expresses every construct below: actions call oracles (ISA §5.3, §4.7) and CC issues controlled region calls (ISA §4.7 `ctl w <call>`).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), ISA-5.3, ISA-4.7
DLT-10-P2
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-1 Interface vs body.** The marker fixes the interface: `ctrl`, `adj` and `compute` give an action; `oracle` and `inplace` give an oracle.
  <1>2 [req] The body of an action region is partitioned by content (TDL-3) and is never rejected for containing classical code.   deps: DLT-10-P14.S1 (TDL-3), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region)
DLT-10-P3
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-2 Coherence states.**   deps: DLT-2-P13.S1 (Coherence state)
DLT-10-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Object state.** Every instance of an abstract object X has a dynamic state K(X) ∈ {Cl, M, Co}, kept by the CC tier (a runtimes table for globals, the coherent allocator's bookkeeping for heap objects; §11, §17): Cl (the CC **shadow** holds X's bytes; X's arena cells are |0⟩), M (materialised: the arena cells hold the same bytes as a basis state; both are valid), Co (the arena is the truth and may be superposed; the shadow is invalid).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-10-P20.S2 (coherent allocator), DLT-11, DLT-17
  <1>2 [req] Every instance starts Cl.
  <1>3 [req] Transitions: Cl → M by **materialisation** (an uncontrolled XOR of the shadow into the arena cells, TDL-4); M → Cl by the same XOR (dematerialisation, before a CC store to X); M or Cl → Co when an event is applied (§18.2) that writes X's cells with a non-empty control set or with a Co value; Co → Cl by `__bennett_observe` of all of X's bytes (TDL-11); partial observe leaves K=Co and the observed cells in their measured basis state.   deps: DLT-10-P19.S1 (TDL-4), DLT-10-P43.S1 (TDL-11), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-18.2-P1.S1 (control set), DLT-18.2
  <1>4 [req] Coverage is tracked since each byte's last write; writes invalidate coverage except a permitted TDL-11 CC store to already-covered bytes.   deps: DLT-10-P43.S1 (TDL-11), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>5 [req] That store writes a known classical value to both arena and shadow, so the bytes remain covered with that store as their covering write.
  <1>6 [req] Release never changes Co to Cl by fiat: first restore the cells to the basis value equal to the shadow with the invocation's own events, then dematerialise to Cl and release.
  <1>7 [req] Otherwise release traps T-TAINT-HOST (code 4), inside or outside generation, before reuse.
DLT-10-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Value state.** During a generation (§18.4) every executed SSA value instance is Cl or Co: Co iff it is a deferred measurement result (§7.5a), a load of a byte whose **view** (below) is Co, the result of an instruction with a Co operand, or a `phi` at the join of a Co branch.   deps: DLT-7.5-P2.S1 (deferred measurement), DLT-18.4, DLT-7.5-P2
DLT-10-P6
  <1>1 [req] **View.** A generation sees outliving memory through a **view**: a byte of object X has view Co if K(X) = Co, or if the generation's recorder (or a recorder of an enclosing generation of the same invocation) holds an event that writes it with a non-empty control set or with a Co value; otherwise its view is Cl.   deps: DLT-18.2-P1.S1 (control set)
  <1>2 [req] A child generation inherits the invoking generation's current speculative view: apply visible enclosing recorders' classical updates in execution order, then the child's updates, to the invocation-entry shadow.
  <1>3 [req] The enclosing invocation control is added only at event composition, not to this inherited view.
  <1>4 [req] This applies to nested ctrl, adj and compute; sibling arm recorders stay isolated until join.
  <1>5 [req] For `compute`, retain gen(f)'s recorder after it ends: it is visible to gen(use) and adjoint(E_f) construction as part of the same invocation.
  <1>6 [req] Its classical events update use's Cl view; its predicated or Co-valued writes give use a Co view. *(non-normative)* Inside `ctrl(q, f)` the body's own stores are recorded without q (q is added when the generation ends, TDL-6), so f sees its own writes classically, as the q = 1 branch must; an inner `ctrl(q2, g)` records g's writes with q2, so f's later reads of them are Co.   deps: DLT-10-P34.S1 (TDL-6)
DLT-10-P7
  <1>1 [def] **Static may-Co analysis MC** (decides partitions, arena placement and the verdict rules below; flow-sensitive, interprocedural over the CC call graph with one summary per (function, coherence signature), merge by union, least fixpoint):   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-4.1-P12.S1 (call graph)
DLT-10-P8
  <1>1 [def] MC1: the state at a program point is the set of abstract objects that may have K = Co there;   deps: DLT-5.1-P1.S1 (abstract objects)
DLT-10-P9
  <1>1 [def] MC2: after an action region invocation (`ctrl`, `adj`, `compute`, or a `reversible` call reached from a generation, §7.4), add every outliving object (TDL-5) that a store in the closure of a candidate may write, if the invocation is `ctrl` or the store may be under an inner control, may store a may-Co value, or may be control-dependent on a may-Co branch;   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), DLT-7.4
DLT-10-P10
  <1>1 [def] MC3: `__bennett_observe(p, len)` removes an object X when AV(p) = {(X, 0)} and len is a constant ≥ size(X); otherwise no change;
DLT-10-P11
  <1>1 [def] MC4: an instruction is may-Co if an operand may be Co, it is a load whose AV(p) contains a may-Co object, or it is control-dependent on a may-Co branch (stores and calls included).
  <1>2 [req] A value is may-Co if its instruction is; an object written by a may-Co store is may-Co after the store;
DLT-10-P12
  <1>1 [def] MC5: calls bind the coherence signature of the callee (which parameters may be Co, and the may-Co set at entry); every distinct signature is a variant (§4.3).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-4.3
DLT-10-P13
  <1>1 [def] **Interprocedural and context-sensitive** by coherence signature (MC5; NS §3.1).   deps: DLT-10-P12.S1 (MC5)
DLT-10-P14
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-3 Partition.** For each variant of an action region and of its CC callees:   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
DLT-10-P15
  <1>1 [req] **G (generation)**: the instructions MC does not mark may-Co.
  <1>2 [req] G runs in CC, once per invocation (§18.4).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-18.4
DLT-10-P16
  <1>1 [def] **Slices**: every maximal single-entry single-exit region of may-Co instructions other than stores to outliving objects and marker calls is outlined (LLVM `CodeExtractor`) into a **slice function** S_k, with: classical live-ins → `ARG` parameters; Co live-ins → `IN` parameters; may-Co objects it loads → the arena, bound as a `MEM_IN` region `coh`; Co live-outs → outputs (§8.6).   deps: DLT-10-P18.S1 (marker calls), DLT-8.6
  <1>2 [req] An irreducible may-Co region is outlined as a whole function.
  <1>3 [req] S_k is normalised (§12) and lifted (§16) like any oracle region; its entry state is the generation's view (§8.5: the arena for may-Co objects, the shadow for the rest).   deps: DLT-12, DLT-16, DLT-8.5
  <1>4 [def] Each slice also keeps its original instructions as a **classical twin** in G.
DLT-10-P17
  <1>1 [def] At run time the generation runs the classical twin when every live-in of the slice is Cl and every object it may load has view Cl; otherwise it records a **slice invocation**: before it, every live instance of a may-Co object the slice may load whose state is Cl is materialised (Cl → M); the invocation computes the Co live-outs into fresh registers (`compute` events, §18.2).   deps: DLT-10-P16.S4 (classical twin), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-18.2
DLT-10-P18
  <1>1 [def] **Stores to outliving objects** and **marker calls** are never inside slices: they become events (TDL-5, TDL-10).   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5), DLT-10-P38.S1 (TDL-10)
  <1>2 [req] A store or marker call that is control-dependent on a Co branch is recorded under the branch's condition wire (TDL-10).   deps: DLT-10-P38.S1 (TDL-10), DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>3 [req] A Co branch is if-converted: the generation constructs both arms from separate copies of the pre-branch view.
  <1>4 [req] Within each arm its own classical updates are interpreted conditional on that arm being taken; the other arm's recorder is invisible.
  <1>5 [req] Only instructions not control-dependent on the Co branch run classically (none in a plain diamond); all others run in slices or as predicated events.
  <1>6 [req] Views merge only at the join, whose `phi`s become slice-computed selects.
  <1>7 [req] May-Co stores to non-outliving workspace and subsequent dependent reads stay together inside the slice's private memory through the join.
DLT-10-P19
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-4 Coherent arena.**
DLT-10-P20
  <1>1 [req] The objects in any may-Co set of MC are placed in the **arena**: a static address range [A_coh, A_coh + 2^A) of the layout, disjoint from CC globals and heap (ISA §7.1, §7.2; G-13 would lift the restriction).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2
  <1>2 [def] Globals in the set are relocated into the arena by the layout (§19.5) in both builds, so their addresses stay native-equal; heap objects of may-Co allocation sites are allocated by the runtimes' **coherent allocator** in the arena range in both builds.   deps: DLT-19.5
DLT-10-P21
  <1>1 [req] The arena's contents are a CC-held register of 8·2^A wires, bound to every slice and arena action as `region[A,8]` (ISA §3.3 binding); cell i is the byte at address A_coh + i.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-3.3
DLT-10-P22
  <1>1 [req] The **shadow** of the arena is the CC global `__bennett_rt_shadow` of 2^A bytes at A_coh + Δ_sh (a layout constant); CC loads and stores whose abstract address may lie in the arena are rewritten to address + Δ_sh when the object's state is Cl or M, after dematerialising an M object for a store; when the state is Co, TDL-11 applies.   deps: DLT-10-P43.S1 (TDL-11), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
DLT-10-P23
  <1>1 [req] **Writing at a run-time address.** A cell write of a classical mask m at a classical run-time address a (an `xmask` event, a materialisation) lowers to an action that calls the runtime oracle `@xorargs` (§16.5) to XOR the classical `args` (a, m) into fresh wires a_w, m_w, then `qstore coh a_w m_w` (ISA §4.6: XOR into the addressed cell; O(2^A) gates unless the target has QRAM, NS §4.3), then `@xorargs` again to uncompute a_w and m_w.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-16.5, ISA-4.6
  <1>2 [req] Objects at static offsets MAY use a static slice (`qreg.slice` with a literal `lo`) and X gates instead.   deps: DLT-25.2-P5.S1 (`qreg.slice`)
DLT-10-P24
  <1>1 [req] The physical `coh` interface range is storage, not a logical allocation.
  <1>2 [unc] Maintain (base, size, live) for every arena global and coherent heap object.
  <1>3 [req] Before every program memory access of n bytes at p, require the entire nonwrapping [p,p+n) to lie in one live logical allocation.
  <1>4 [req] Otherwise produce fault 7 before access, including event-producing stores, whose mutation is gated on success.
  <1>5 [req] The pinned ISA interface entry never satisfies this additional check.
  <1>6 [req] The default is an ordinary materialised runtime helper `__bennett_rt_coh_check(p,n,table)` over the metadata: visit entries in allocation order, OR their live-and-contained predicates, fault 7 when false, then access.
  <1>7 [req] All helper rows are charged.
  <1>8 [req] A statically proven contained access may omit the helper.
  <1>9 [unc] Coherent alloc/free update the same table before subsequent access; snapshot/slice entry transfers metadata along with the corresponding arena view, using preserved `in` metadata for a slice.
  <1>10 [req] Superposed addresses use this same lifted helper, without classical inspection.
DLT-10-P25
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-5 Stores to outliving objects (the store rule).** An object **outlives** an invocation if it is live after it: a global, an object reachable from `env`, or a heap object not freed in the invocation.   deps: DLT-10-P18.S1 (Stores to outliving objects)
  <1>2 [req] A store `store v, ptr p` executed during a generation to an outliving object X is realised by cases on the view of the bytes it writes:
DLT-10-P26
  <1>1 [req] **(a) View Cl** (the old bytes c are known to CC).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
DLT-10-P27
  <1>1 [unc] v and p Cl: the event `xmask X+o, c ⊕ v` (o = p − X's base).
DLT-10-P28
  <1>1 [req] v or p Co: a slice invocation O computes the address and the mask c ⊕ v into fresh registers (c is a classical argument of O); the event `xstore coh a_w m_w` XORs the mask into the addressed cells (`qstore`); a second identical invocation of O uncomputes the registers.   deps: DLT-10-P17.S1 (slice invocation), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>2 [req] O never loads X's written bytes from the arena (their view is Cl, so O receives them as arguments), and no instruction between the two invocations writes anything O reads, so the second invocation cleans them exactly (ISA §5.2 involution).   deps: ISA-5.2
DLT-10-P29
  <1>1 [unc] Exact; no history.
DLT-10-P30
  <1>1 [req] **(b) View Co, and the update is a library bijection** in the old value: the materialised IR is `x = load X+o; y = op(x, k…); store y, X+o` with no intervening store to X, where op is one of: add or sub of k (wrapping at the access width), xor with k, rotate by a constant, negate, `bswap`, add of k modulo a constant m (with x < m), multiply by a constant k modulo m with gcd(k, m) = 1 (with x < m); or a declared `inplace` pair (§8.7). k may be Cl or Co (an `IN` binding of a slice output); k MUST NOT depend on X's written bytes.   deps: DLT-8.7
  <1>2 [req] The event is the `bennett-arith` permutation action for op on the arena cells X+o with k as `in` or `args` (ISA §4.7), preceded by the slice invocation that computes a Co k and followed by its uncomputation.   deps: DLT-10-P17.S1 (slice invocation), ISA-4.7
  <1>3 [req] The complete predicated update MUST be a total bijection on the declared mutable interface, with bounded inverse and clean non-fault workspace.
  <1>4 [req] A bijective op alone is insufficient when its condition depends on the changed bytes.
DLT-10-P31
  <1>1 [unc] **(c) View Co, inside the `f` part of `compute(f, use, budget, env)`**: history.
  <1>2 [req] A slice invocation computes the new bytes into a fresh register t; the event `xchg coh a t` exchanges t with the cells (`qexchm`); t now holds the displaced bytes and is kept until adjoint(E_f) replays the events in reverse (`xchg` again, then the slice invocation again), which cleans it (NS §3.1 compute-with-uncompute; Bennett).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-10-P17.S1 (slice invocation)
  <1>3 [req] `use` MUST NOT write bytes that f wrote by case (c) (`BD-E-COHERENT-OVERWRITE` otherwise: the replay would not restore them).   deps: DLT-22.1-P19.S1 (BD-E-COHERENT-OVERWRITE)
DLT-10-P32
  <1>1 [req] **(d) Otherwise**: `BD-E-COHERENT-OVERWRITE`, decided statically: a store to an outliving object that MC says may be Co at the store, and that is neither (b) nor (c).   deps: DLT-22.1-P19.S1 (BD-E-COHERENT-OVERWRITE)
  <1>2 [req] NS §4.2: "writing a register not known to be clean is a compile-time error … an in-place update x ← g(x) requires a total bijection with a bounded inverse … else it fails loud".   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>3 [req] The diagnostic names the store and the object.
DLT-10-P33
  <1>1 [req] Stores to objects that do not outlive the invocation are workspace: G runs them on the generation's view only when Cl; may-Co workspace stores and dependent reads run in slices in their private memory (§8.5); at exit the objects are released.   deps: DLT-8.5
  <1>2 [req] Allocation in a generation is unconditional, like every allocation in the ISA (ISA §3.5); the release of an object that outlives the invocation is TDL-12.   deps: DLT-10-P47.S1 (TDL-12), ISA-3.5
DLT-10-P34
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-6 Control and adjoint.** `ctrl(q, qi, …)` adds wire q.qi to every event's control set (ISA §4.7 `ctl w`).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-18.2-P1.S1 (control set), ISA-4.7
  <1>2 [req] `oracle_call`, `qexch`, `qexchm`, `qstore`, X and `xmask` are self-adjoint (ISA §5.3 adjoint table); `adj(f)` generates f from the current classical state and applies the adjoint of the event list; `compute` reuses the recording E_f for its uncompute, so masks and history match exactly.   deps: ISA-5.3
DLT-10-P35
  <1>1 [req] **TDL-7 Workspace and declared outcomes.** Non-fault workspace, including temporary Co conditions and slice data, MUST be cleaned by the adjoint of its exact computing sequence before the invocation returns; `compute` f may retain it only until adjoint(E_f).
  <1>2 [req] It is never implicitly made an output or measured.
  <1>3 [req] Predicate and parameter computation are part of TDL-5(b)'s total-bijection check.   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
  <1>4 [req] An uncleanable non-bijective coherent overwrite follows TDL-5(d), as ratified E3 requires; an out-of-place oracle or compute/use/uncompute retains that computation.   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
  <1>5 [req] Per-slice codes and Φ are retained fault outcomes with TDL-8 lifetime, not incidental workspace.   deps: DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8)
  <1>6 [req] Continuation flags, per-iteration conditions and preserved trajectory records are control workspace, including in a standalone adjoint; declaring compiler outputs does not exempt them.
  <1>7 [req] Intentional deferred-measurement records (§7.5a) are separate channel outputs retained until their declared observation boundary, never a cleanup substitute.   deps: DLT-7.5-P2
DLT-10-P36
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-8 Faults.** NS §4.2: "a quantum action has no fault outcome of its own: any fallible computation it needs is a lifted oracle it calls, whose fault register it declares among its outputs".   deps: DLT-8.2-P7.S3 (fault register)
  <1>2 [req] Every slice invocation k gets a fresh 4-wire fault register f_k; the uncomputing invocation uses a second fresh register f′_k, which is then cleaned by `qxor f_k f′_k` (both hold the same code) and freed, so f_k holds the slice's code and is retained (TDL-7).   deps: DLT-10-P35.S1 (TDL-7), DLT-10-P17.S1 (slice invocation), DLT-8.2-P7.S3 (fault register), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>3 [def] At the end of the invocation a runtime combine oracle `@firstfault` (§16.5) XORs the first nonzero code of f_1, …, f_K in event order (including generation-budget and virtual-device fault registers, not just slice codes) into the action's **declared fault register** Φ: the budget record's `fault` field (§9.1), or, when that is null, a register the plugin allocates and retains (TDL-7); f_k, compute copies g_k and a plugin-allocated Φ are declared fault outputs owned by CC.   deps: DLT-10-P35.S1 (TDL-7), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-16.5, DLT-9.1
  <1>4 [req] They are exempt from invocation T-LEAK and are neither measured nor freed at invocation return or device-entry return.
  <1>5 [req] No f_k is freed by firstfault. adjoint(E_f) reuses the same f_k wires (f9 in tdl-d); a cleaned f_k may be freed after that reuse.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>6 [req] The CC epilogue means program end: copy successive chunks of at most 128 bits of each remaining declared output into fresh non-view registers and measure those copies (ISA measure consumes the copies).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>7 [req] The original output wires now hold the known basis outcome and remain live for correction and clean free.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>8 [req] Report the invocation's combined Φ statistics (deduplicate correlated code copies, do not sum their rates), apply outcome-conditioned X corrections and free clean.
  <1>9 [req] Caller-supplied Φ remains caller-owned under §6.6.   deps: DLT-6.6
  <1>10 [req] The certificate reports the rate (`fault_rate`, ISA §12.3).   deps: ISA-12.3
  <1>11 [req] On a faulting basis input a slice's payload is the canonical 0 (ISA §5.2).   deps: ISA-5.2
  <1>12 [req] Every event consuming a fallible slice result is additionally predicated on that slice's code being zero; if multiple results are required, all their codes must be zero.
  <1>13 [req] This includes xchg, negative branch predicates and dependent slices: failure selects neither arm.
  <1>14 [req] Success flags are computed reversibly from retained codes, kept until their last dependent use, then uncomputed.
  <1>15 [req] These predicates do not gate the producing slice's compute/uncompute or fault collection.
  <1>16 [req] Thus failed results leave dependent arena writes and gates unchanged.
  <1>17 [req] A nonzero rate is a reported quantity (NS §4.2), not an error.
DLT-10-P37
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-9 Budget.** Generation G receives its own full region budget.   deps: DLT-2-P13.S4 (Generation)
  <1>2 [req] Before each executed materialised LLVM instruction other than phi/debug/mark it checks and consumes one step; heap and depth are checked before mutation.
  <1>3 [req] Exhaustion is invocation failure, not a source leak.
  <1>4 [req] No further source instruction executes.
  <1>5 [req] Compiler finalisation runs without consuming the exhausted generation budget: before applying any recorded event, discard the failed invocation's speculative views and recorders, cancel all invocation-owned provisional allocations (including nested ones), preserve foreign handles, and emit only the corresponding declared fault event under the invocation/path controls.
  <1>6 [req] Failed gen(f) does not run use; failure of either compute part cancels the still-unapplied composite.
  <1>7 [req] Normal T-LEAK checks apply only to successfully completed source generation.
  <1>8 [req] Nested G execution consumes the enclosing remaining generation limits as well as its own limits, so nesting cannot evade a finite bound.
  <1>9 [unc] Classical twins charge their executed instructions.
  <1>10 [req] Compilation never runs G to completion to discover an event list: G is a bounded CC routine executed at invocation.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>11 [req] Every slice invocation's token comes from the region-entry marker's budget record, each field completed from the budget table (§9); each slice gets the full record (outline E2).   deps: DLT-10-P17.S1 (slice invocation), DLT-9.2-P2.S1 (budget table), DLT-9
  <1>12 [req] Exhaustion is that slice's `FAULT_BUDGET_*` and reaches TDL-8.   deps: DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8)
DLT-10-P38
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-10 Co control of quantum operations and stores.**
DLT-10-P39
  <1>1 [req] A marker call or outliving store that is control-dependent on a Co branch, or a marker call with a Co index or op code: a slice invocation computes the condition (and the index or code) into fresh wires; the event is issued under `qpred` of the condition wire, once per member of the index or code dispatch domain under the predicate "value = member" (ISA §3.5, §4.6); then the wires are uncomputed.   deps: DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call), DLT-10-P17.S1 (slice invocation), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-3.4-P20.S3 (dispatch domain), ISA-3.5, ISA-4.6
DLT-10-P40
  <1>1 [req] A Co rotation angle is predicated per value when its value set is finite.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set)
  <1>2 [req] Otherwise it is `BD-E-COHERENT-ANGLE`: NS promises predication, not superposed parameters, and ISA §3.1 "angles are literals or classical arguments fixed at region entry".   deps: DLT-22.1-P20.S1 (BD-E-COHERENT-ANGLE), ISA-3.1
DLT-10-P41
  <1>1 [req] A loop whose trip count is Co is unrolled in the event list up to N = the `steps` field of the budget record: iteration i is predicated on the flag c_i (c_1 = the first continuation condition, c_{i+1} = c_i ∧ condition_i, computed by `ccx` into fresh wires).   deps: DLT-9.1-P1.S1 (budget record), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>2 [req] Flags c_i, per-iteration conditions and preserved trajectory records are control workspace subject to TDL-7: clean them within their dependency-validity windows and before successful action return.   deps: DLT-10-P35.S1 (TDL-7)
  <1>3 [req] For preserved inputs compute bounded conditions out of place, apply the controlled bodies, then reverse their exact computing slices.
  <1>4 [req] For overwritten inputs the complete bounded transition must meet TDL-5(b)'s total- bijection and bounded-inverse obligation: use that inverse on a copy of the output to reconstruct the entry, recompute the forward trajectory on copies, erase the original control records against it, then reverse the reconstruction and its copies.   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
  <1>5 [req] All these slices preserve their inputs; all trajectory workspace is restored.
  <1>6 [req] In compute, history may instead survive through use and be cleaned by the matching adjoint(E_f), TDL-5(c).   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
  <1>7 [req] A non-bijective data-only update follows the existing TDL-5(d), with out-of-place oracle and compute interfaces still available.   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
  <1>8 [req] A Co trip count alone never rejects a loop.
  <1>9 [req] No trip-count record is measured at program end.
  <1>10 [req] A loop is a structured event; its adjoint is the special rule of §18.2, not flat ccx reversal; if c_{N+1} may be 1 the invocation records `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` under c_{N+1} into Φ.   deps: DLT-18.2
  <1>11 [req] G-14 (§25.3) is the efficient form.   deps: DLT-25.3
DLT-10-P42
  <1>1 [req] A `qalloc`/`qborrow` whose n is Co allocates the maximum of n's value set, padded to its bound (NS §4.2: "an allocation whose size depends on a qubit is padded to its bound"); gates on wires at or beyond the run-time n are predicated off.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>2 [req] An unbounded value set is BLK-1 (§25.1).   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1), DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-25.1
DLT-10-P43
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-11 After the region.**
DLT-10-P44
  <1>1 [req] Code that reads an object whose state is Co, inside the closure of a later region entry, is part of that region's slices (TDL-3).   deps: DLT-10-P14.S1 (TDL-3)
DLT-10-P45
  <1>1 [req] Code outside every generation that may load from, store to, free or otherwise release a may-Co object is compiled in two variants (Cl and Co signatures, §4.3), selected by the object's dynamic state.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-4.3
  <1>2 [req] Its Co variant at host or CC level traps (`T-TAINT-HOST`, fault code 4), and statically the plugin warns (`BD-W-TAINT-HOST`, naming `__bennett_observe`): NS §4.2, classical outputs of coherent memory leave only through measurement.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>3 [req] A CC store is legal after observation of every byte it writes; for a partial observation while K=Co, permit only the covered basis bytes, update their authoritative arena cells and shadow together and retain that coverage.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>4 [unc] Unobserved Co bytes still trap.
  <1>5 [req] Release requires full restoration/dematerialisation as TDL-2 states, even inside a generation; it never silently reuses dirty cells.   deps: DLT-10-P3.S1 (TDL-2)
DLT-10-P46
  <1>1 [unc] `__bennett_observe(p, len)` observes successive chunks of at most sixteen bytes: copy each chunk's cells into a fresh non-view register of at most 128 wires and ISA `measure` it.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>2 [req] Sequential basis measurements preserve the joint distribution.
  <1>3 [req] No whole arena register or slice view is measured.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>4 [req] Write the outcome into the shadow.
  <1>5 [req] For partial observation of X leave its observed cells in |m⟩ and K(X)=Co: the arena stays authoritative.
  <1>6 [req] When every byte of X is covered since its last write other than such a permitted CC covering store, XOR the whole measured X out of the arena and set K(X)=Cl.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>7 [req] Cl bytes are read from the shadow; M objects are dematerialised first. len=0 changes nothing.
DLT-10-P47
  <1>1 [def] **TDL-12 Effects in action regions.** Generations inherit the run's virtual device bindings, established by the CC launch stub from `--bennett-dev` (§19.9) using §8.3 model codes and widths; nested generations inherit the same bindings.   deps: DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), DLT-2-P13.S4 (Generation), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-19.2-P2.S1 (launch stub), DLT-19.9, DLT-8.3
  <1>2 [unc] Marker signatures stay unchanged.   deps: DLT-2-P1.S1 (Marker)
  <1>3 [req] The build/launch record includes bindings, initial images, capacities and state registers.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>4 [req] Mutable model state (console buffer/length, clock counter, entropy cursor, block overlay and presence bits) is a declared mutable action interface, allocated in the arena at run entry and shared across invocations; entropy/disk images are preserved `in` registers.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>5 [req] The model's bounded undo log, containing saved overwritten bytes/presence bits needed for inversion, is explicit declared device state, not invocation workspace.   deps: DLT-11.3-P1.S2 (device state)
  <1>6 [req] Initialisation is classical preparation outside control.
  <1>7 [req] A bound operation is a controlled reversible transition of this state with the read values of §11.3.   deps: DLT-11.3
  <1>8 [req] Reads into program memory obey TDL-5; capacity exhaustion is a budget fault before mutation.   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
  <1>9 [req] Slices compute parameters and validation, TDL-8 success predicates gate the permutation transition, and its adjoint restores state and cleans parameter workspace.   deps: DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8)
  <1>10 [req] External effects wait for explicit CC observation of model state; adjoint/compute uncompute virtual transitions exactly.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>11 [req] CONSOLE appends without silently discarding action output; its finite capacity is a declared bound.
  <1>12 [req] Clock, entropy and block use §11.3 values and cursor/overlay rules.   deps: DLT-11.3
  <1>13 [req] Only unbound/unmodelled operations or genuine host calls have `fault 8` and a trapped coverage line.   deps: DLT-4.5-P4.S1 (host call), DLT-2-P15.S1 (trapped)
  <1>14 [req] Release of outliving Co objects requires clean/restored state, not unconditional fault 8.
  <1>15 [req] Oracle device state stays private under §11.3, with declared outputs under §8.6.   deps: DLT-11.3-P1.S2 (device state), DLT-11.3, DLT-8.6
````

### §11.1 Tier table   [DLT-11.1]

````text
DLT-11.1-P1
  <1>1 [unc] NS §4.2's effects table, made concrete.
  <1>2 [req] The four device models are the ISA's (ISA §7.3): `console`, `clock`, `entropy`, `block`.   deps: ISA-7.3
DLT-11.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] H | – (host code calls its own libc; markers are never called from H) | real
DLT-11.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] CC | `hostcall bennett.dev.<model>.<op>` (ISA §10): a genuine host effect, since ISA `effect` is R-only (ISA §4.1); the host's reply carries the bytes a read returns | host functions by `hostcall` (§11.4)   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), ISA-10, ISA-4.1, DLT-11.4
DLT-11.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] R | ISA `effect <model>.<op>` on the effect tape, reversed by whole-run reversal (ISA §7.3, §10) | – (R makes no host calls)   deps: DLT-4.5-P4.S1 (host call), ISA-7.3, ISA-10
DLT-11.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] L | the lifted device model of §11.3 over state bound by the descriptor (§8.3) | –   deps: DLT-11.3, DLT-8.3
DLT-11.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] action regions | inherited virtual bindings: controlled reversible transitions (TDL-12); external effects deferred | unbound/unmodelled: F(8)   deps: DLT-10-P47.S1 (TDL-12), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region)
DLT-11.1-P7
  <1>1 [req] unmodelled | F(8) with a `trapped` coverage line (§4.5) | same   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (trapped), DLT-4.5
````

### §11.2 Effect markers in the R tier   [DLT-11.2]

````text
DLT-11.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] `__bennett_effect_<model>_<op>(…)` lowers to ISA `effect <model>.<op> …` with the operands in ISA §7.3 order: `console.write(ptr, len)`, `clock.read(ptr)`, `entropy.read(ptr, len)`, `block.read(sector, ptr)`, `block.write(sector, ptr)`.   deps: ISA-7.3
  <1>2 [req] A failing ISA validation (operands out of range, entropy tape exhausted) is the ISA's fault record (ISA §7.3, code 10 for entropy).   deps: ISA-7.3
  <1>3 [req] The driver declares the models in `devices.models` (ISA §9.1) and supplies `block_image` from `--bennett-disk=<file>` (§19.2).   deps: ISA-9.1, DLT-19.2
  <1>4 [req] `bennett-runtimes`' musl maps system calls onto the markers:
DLT-11.2-P2
  <1>1 [req] `write(1, …)`, `write(2, …)`, `writev` on fds 1 and 2 | `console.write`
DLT-11.2-P3
  <1>1 [req] `clock_gettime`, `gettimeofday`, `time` | `clock.read`: the 8-byte counter c; `CLOCK_REALTIME` and `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` both give tv_sec = c / 10^9, tv_nsec = c mod 10^9
DLT-11.2-P4
  <1>1 [req] `getrandom`, reads of `/dev/urandom` and `/dev/random` | `entropy.read`
DLT-11.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] block-device reads and writes (the platform's virtio-blk, §20.3) | `block.read`, `block.write`   deps: DLT-20.3
DLT-11.2-P6
  <1>1 [req] files | a runtimes VFS over the block device, or over a data-image file system linked as a global
DLT-11.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] Device models beyond the four arrive with an ISA version (ISA §17 item 3, a mechanics deferral, §24): until then an effect on another device is `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`.   deps: DLT-2-P14.S1 (Device model), ISA-17-P4, DLT-24
````

### §11.3 Coherent tier: lifted device models   [DLT-11.3]

````text
DLT-11.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] In an L variant, an effect marker call becomes a call of the runtimes function `__bennett_dev_<model>_<op>` with the same operands.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call)
  <1>2 [def] These are ordinary C functions of `bennett-runtimes`, lifted like any code, over the **device state**: runtimes globals `__bennett_dev_<model>_*` that the oracle wrapper's prologue (§16.2) initialises from the descriptor's device bindings (§8.3) before the region function runs.   deps: DLT-16.2, DLT-8.3
  <1>3 [unc] Their contract:
DLT-11.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] `entropy` | base = `lift.addr @e0`, size = 2^A bytes, pos = 0 | `read(p, len)`: if len > size − pos, `__bennett_nz_fault(10)` (`FAULT_ENTROPY_EXHAUSTED`); else copy [base + pos, base + pos + len) to p and pos += len
DLT-11.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] `block` | base = `lift.addr @k0`, nsect = 2^A′; an empty overlay | `read(s, p)`: the overlay's 512 bytes for sector s if present, else sector s of the region if s < nsect, else 512 zero bytes (ISA §7.3: absent sectors read as zero); `write(s, p)`: copy 512 bytes into the overlay entry for s (a heap allocation by `malloc`, lifted to `lift.alloc`)   deps: ISA-7.3
DLT-11.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] `clock` | c = `value` | `read(p)`: store c (8 bytes, little-endian) at p, then c += 1
DLT-11.3-P5
  <1>1 [req] `console` | L = n(q)/8 (0 for a null q), a buffer of L bytes in the oracle's heap, n = 0 | `write(p, len)`: copy the first min(len, L − n) bytes to buffer[n…], n += that count; further bytes are discarded with the oracle's other writes
DLT-11.3-P6
  <1>1 [req] A model without a binding is unbound: every operation on it is `__bennett_nz_fault(8)` on the executions that reach it (NS §4.2).
  <1>2 [req] The `entropy` fault code 10 in an oracle: ISA §4.2 admits `fault C` for every C < 2^F; ISA §5.4 labels code 10 "(R only)", which §25.2 records as a wording item.   deps: ISA-4.2, ISA-5.4, DLT-25.2
  <1>3 [req] The bound console buffer's bytes are output slices (§8.6).   deps: DLT-8.6
````

### §11.4 Host calls from CC   [DLT-11.4]

````text
DLT-11.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] A CC variant's call of an H function (§4.5 item 3) becomes `hostcall bennett.call/<h>` where `<h>` is the hex SHA-256 of the callee's symbol name: the argument block holds the scalar arguments (little-endian, in order) and, for each pointer argument whose pointee the analysis proves pointer-free (no slot holds a pointer atom, §5), the pointee's byte range as an (offset, length) pair and the bytes themselves.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-4.5-P4, DLT-5
  <1>2 [req] The host-side stub calls the function on a copy, writes back the bytes of pointer arguments, and replies with the result and the written bytes.
  <1>3 [req] A pointer argument whose pointee may contain pointers read by device code does not become a host call: the callee is **promoted** to a CC variant (§19.4; ISA-11 would let it stay native, an efficiency item).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-1.3-P1.S3 (efficiency item), DLT-19.4
  <1>4 [req] Every service is declared in `devices.services` (ISA §9.1, §10).   deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-10
````

### §11.5 Fault codes reported from CC   [DLT-11.5]

````text
DLT-11.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] ISA 0.8 does not specify which code a CC trap reports.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>2 [req] `__bennett_nz_fault(C)` in CC lowers to `hostcall bennett.fault` with a payload holding C, then `trap`; the service records C in the run's fault statistics.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>3 [req] A host call is forbidden in R and a generic R trap cannot preserve arbitrary C.   deps: DLT-4.5-P4.S1 (host call)
  <1>4 [req] Typed R faults therefore have dependency blocker BLK-5 = ISA-7 (§25.1).   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5), DLT-25.2-P2.S1 (ISA-7), DLT-25.1
DLT-11.5-P2
  <1>1 [req] On ISA 0.8 a module has verdict blocked:BD-B-ISA7 if its device program contains an R-tier variant reached as an R entry: a reversible region candidate invoked outside generation, or the §19.2 whole-program R entry.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-19.2
  <1>2 [req] Every such variant has the §17.6 budget check, regardless of supplied run-time limits.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-17.6
  <1>3 [req] A reversible call treated as a direct call under §7.4 is not an R entry and does not by itself block.   deps: DLT-7.4
  <1>4 [req] Other rejection/blocker precedence remains §1.6.   deps: DLT-1.6
  <1>5 [req] The R blocker trap is generic ISA trap with no hostcall, reached only on executions that would report a typed fault (budget exhaustion, F(C), nz_fault(C)); all other executions are unaffected.   deps: DLT-1.6-P4.S2 (blocker trap)
  <1>6 [req] This fallback does not implement typed reporting.
DLT-11.5-P3
  <1>1 [req] Required operation: R fault C terminates with fault record code C, performs no subsequent source effect and preserves reversible prefix history.
  <1>2 [req] No ISA 0.8 generic-trap path is claimed to implement this contract.
````

````text
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 8 lines) @msg = private unnamed_addr constant [3 x i8] c"hi\0A"
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) BD-B-ISA7 R entry: BLK-5 (ISA-7); mandatory pre-instruction typed fault 1 requir…   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5), DLT-25.2-P2.S1 (ISA-7)

DLT-11.6-P2

  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 10 lines) @msg = private unnamed_addr constant [3 x i8] c"ok\0A"
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 4 lines) run: status=COMPLETED results=[] faults=[]
DLT-11.6-P3
  <1>1 [ex] The ENTROPY-bound oracle of rung 8 is §16.8.   deps: DLT-16.8
````

### §12.1 Scope, order and naming   [DLT-12.1]

````text
DLT-12.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] The normaliser rewrites every device variant (§4.3) and the initialisers of every global a device variant references; H variants are untouched.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-4.3
  <1>2 [req] It applies the rules of §12.2 in table order, each to the whole module before the next, with one exception: N33 is applied immediately before N32 so any floating-point instructions introduced by its expansions are processed by N32; own-format carrier calls and bit-exact reconstruction remain as specified in §13.4.   deps: DLT-12.2-P35.S1 (N33), DLT-12.2-P34.S1 (N32), DLT-12.2, DLT-13.4
  <1>3 [req] After N27 and N32, re-apply N22 to every instruction they created with an integer side wider than 128 bits.   deps: DLT-12.2-P29.S1 (N27), DLT-12.2-P34.S1 (N32), DLT-12.2-P24.S1 (N22)
  <1>4 [req] Only width legalisation repeats; no other rule re-runs.
  <1>5 [req] Width lowering may be fused with an expansion when the resulting canonical limb rows are identical.
  <1>6 [def] The result is the **materialised module**: the reference semantics (§18), the lifting map (§16) and the native comparison build (§17.7) are all defined on it (NS §3.2: "the native comparison binary is built from that materialised bitcode").   deps: DLT-17.7-P1.S1 (native comparison binary), DLT-18, DLT-16, DLT-17.7
DLT-12.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] **Naming.** A rule that introduces a value names it `%nz.<tag>.<r>`, where `<r>` is the name of the instruction being rewritten without its `%` (a number for an unnamed value).
  <1>2 [req] For a rewritten instruction without a result (a `void` call, a `store`, an `invoke` of a `void` function), `<r>` is `<f>.<k>`: f the enclosing function's name and k the 0-based count, in instruction order, of the result-less instructions of f that the same rule rewrites.
  <1>3 [req] A value introduced once per function is `%nz.<tag>.<f>`.
  <1>4 [req] A constant global a rule needs is `@nz.<tag>.<f>.<r>`.
  <1>5 [req] A declaration a rule needs is appended at the end of the module, and a global after the module's globals, in order of first use.
  <1>6 [req] Each rule states its tags.
````

````text
  <1>1 [def] N21 | **vector scalarisation**: every instruction of vector type, every vector intrinsic (`llvm.vector.*`, `llvm.experimental.vector.*`, `llvm.vp.*`, `llvm.experimental.vp.*`, `llvm.masked.*`, `llvm.stepvector`, `llvm.experimental.stepvector`, `llvm.get.active.lane.mask`, `llvm.experimental.get.vector.length`, `llvm.experimental.cttz.elts`, `llvm.vp.cttz.elts`, `llvm.loop.dependence.raw.mask`, `llvm.loop.dependence.war.mask`), vector parameters and results of internal device functions | per-lane scalar instructions in lane order, by the rules of §12.6   deps: DLT-12.6
DLT-12.2-P24
  <1>1 [def] N22 | **wide integers and non-byte memory**: an integer type iN with N > 128 in an instruction; a load or store of iN with N not a multiple of 8 or of an aggregate type | iN with N > 128 becomes L = ⌈N/64⌉ i64 limbs (limb 0 the least significant; the top limb is i(N − 64(L−1))) with the expansions of §12.7; a load or store of iN with N mod 8 ≠ 0 becomes a load or store of i(8⌈N/8⌉) with a `trunc` after the load and a `zext` before the store (fixing LangRef's unspecified extra bits as zero); a load or store of an aggregate becomes one access per element in element order at its offset (`%nz.e<k>.<r>`)   deps: DLT-12.7

  <1>1 [def] N23 | **varargs** | §14.3   deps: DLT-14.3

  <1>1 [def] N25 | `byval`, `inalloca` and `preallocated` arguments; `llvm.call.preallocated.*`; aggregate returns wider than 128 bits | an explicit caller copy into a shadow-stack slot of the caller's frame (`%nz.bv<j>.<r>` for argument j) passed as a plain `ptr`, and the attribute removed from the callee and the call; a return of a first-class aggregate wider than 128 bits is demoted to a hidden first parameter `ptr %nz.ret` in which the callee stores the value (`sret` semantics)   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
DLT-12.2-P28
````

### §12.3 Fault replacement F(C)   [DLT-12.3]

````text
DLT-12.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] F(C): the instruction is replaced by `call void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64 C)` followed by `unreachable`; the instructions after it in its block are deleted, and the block is removed from the incoming lists of the phi nodes of its former successors.
  <1>2 [req] `__bennett_nz_fault` is a primitive (§3.1): `bennett-runtimes` defines it for the native comparison build (record C and end the device entry with fault C); the map gives it the row `fault C` (§16.4); CC and R lower it by §11.5.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-3.1, DLT-16.4, DLT-11.5
````

### §12.5 Scalable vectors   [DLT-12.5]

````text
DLT-12.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] A scalable vector type `<vscale x L x T>` has L·V lanes, where V is the constant `vscale` of the link: the function attribute `vscale_range(V, V)` when every device function that uses scalable vectors carries it with equal bounds, else the driver option `--bennett-vscale=<V>` (default: the minimum of the `vscale_range` bounds, §24).   deps: DLT-24
  <1>2 [req] The native comparison build is compiled for the same V (`-msve-vector-bits=128·V` or `-mrvv-vector-bits`).
  <1>3 [req] N10 replaces `llvm.vscale` by V and N21 scalarises the L·V lanes. *(non-normative)* x86-64 has no scalable vectors; the rule exists so that AArch64 SVE and RISC-V V code is liftable.   deps: DLT-12.2-P12.S1 (N10), DLT-12.2-P23.S1 (N21)
````

### §12.6 Vector scalarisation (N21)   [DLT-12.6]

````text
DLT-12.6-P1
  <1>1 [req] For a value `%v` of type `<L x T>`, lane k is the scalar `%nz.l<k>.v` (k = 0 … L − 1).
  <1>2 [unc] Rules, in lane order k = 0, 1, …:
DLT-12.6-P2
  <1>1 [req] elementwise instructions (binary, bitwise, `icmp`, `fcmp`, casts, `select` with a vector or scalar condition, unary `fneg`, `freeze`, calls of elementwise intrinsics): one scalar instruction per lane, `%nz.l<k>.r = <op> T %nz.l<k>.a, %nz.l<k>.b`;
DLT-12.6-P3
  <1>1 [req] `bitcast` is elementwise only for equal lane geometries.
  <1>2 [unc] Otherwise pack the source bit string in datalayout order and resegment it into destination lanes or a scalar.
  <1>3 [unc] On supported little-endian targets lane 0 occupies the low bits.
  <1>4 [unc] Zero-extend source carriers to total width, shift by cumulative widths and OR in source-lane order; extract by logical right shift and truncation in destination-lane order.
  <1>5 [unc] Omit identity casts and zero shifts; N22 legalises total widths above 128.   deps: DLT-12.2-P24.S1 (N22)
  <1>6 [req] Drop no source memory access.
DLT-12.6-P4
  <1>1 [req] Every vector load/store follows the vector's datalayout bit string: lane k begins at bit k·bitwidth(T), with no scalar allocation padding between lanes.
  <1>2 [req] `getelementptr T, ptr p, i64 k` followed by load/store T is permitted only when T's allocation stride equals that lane spacing.
  <1>3 [req] Otherwise byte-sized lanes use `getelementptr i8, ptr p, i64 (k·bitwidth(T)/8)`; sub-byte lanes use packed-byte extraction/insertion.
  <1>4 [unc] Replacement accesses stay within the original vector storage range.
  <1>5 [req] At byte offset o, scalar alignment is min(abi-align(T), gcd(original alignment,o)); gcd(a,0)=a.
  <1>6 [req] Masked accesses touch only bytes containing active lanes and preserve inactive bits; padding follows N22.   deps: DLT-12.2-P24.S1 (N22)
  <1>7 [req] This applies also to byte-multiple integer lanes such as i24, not only packed i1.
  <1>8 [req] See [LangRef vector layout](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#vector-type) and [load/store semantics](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#load-instruction);
DLT-12.6-P5
  <1>1 [req] `insertelement`, `extractelement` with constant in-range indices and `shufflevector` with valid indices: lane renaming; N15 has already removed constant out-of-range extraction/insertion.   deps: DLT-12.2-P17.S1 (N15)
  <1>2 [unc] With a non-constant index (after N15): a `select` chain over the L lanes in lane order, using the same widened unsigned index and exact lane constants as N15.   deps: DLT-12.2-P17.S1 (N15)
  <1>3 [req] Constants must never wrap to iK.
  <1>4 [req] Insertion may fuse the out-of-range unchanged-vector select into per-lane equality selects: all equalities are false out of range.
  <1>5 [req] LLVM admits an index of any integer width, independent of L ([extractelement](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#extractelement-instruction), [insertelement](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#insertelement-instruction)); LangRef out-of-range poison is totalised by N15, not by truncating L;   deps: DLT-12.2-P17.S1 (N15)
DLT-12.6-P6
  <1>1 [def] `llvm.vector.reduce.<op>(v)` with op ∈ {add, mul, and, or, xor, smax, smin, umax, umin, fadd, fmul, fmax, fmin, fmaximum, fminimum}: a **left fold** in lane order: `%nz.f1.r = <op> lane0, lane1`, `%nz.f<k>.r = <op> %nz.f<k−1>.r, lane k`, the last named `%r`; the ordered `fadd`/`fmul` forms take the start value as the first operand.
  <1>2 [req] Exact for floating point once N2 has removed `reassoc`;   deps: DLT-12.2-P4.S1 (N2), DLT-12.2-P34.S1 (floating point)
DLT-12.6-P7
  <1>1 [req] Selector/reduction exceptions take precedence over the next bullet.
  <1>2 [req] For `vp.select` and `vp.merge`, the condition selects true/false operands within evl; false is not inactivity.
  <1>3 [req] Outside evl, merge preserves on_false, select's poison becomes N7 zero.   deps: DLT-12.2-P9.S1 (N7)
  <1>4 [req] VP reductions fold the start with active lanes only in increasing lane order; inactive lanes contribute no zero (unless zero is the reduction identity).
DLT-12.6-P8
  <1>1 [req] `llvm.vp.*` (vector-predicated) and `llvm.masked.*`: lane k is active iff k < evl and mask[k]; an active lane executes the scalar operation, an inactive lane yields the passthrough operand (masked loads) or 0 (N7: LangRef's poison), and a masked or predicated store, scatter or compress-store writes only active lanes, each under a conditional branch (blocks `nz.ml<k>.<r>`); gathers and scatters use the lane's pointer;   deps: DLT-12.2-P9.S1 (N7)
DLT-12.6-P9
  <1>1 [req] `llvm.stepvector` lane k is k; `llvm.get.active.lane.mask(base, n)` lane k is base + k < n (unsigned); `llvm.experimental.get.vector.length(cnt, vf, scalable)` is umin(cnt, vf·(V if scalable else 1)); `cttz.elts` is the index of the first nonzero lane (L if none, or the poison-free 0 of N7 when the flag says zero is poison); `loop.dependence.raw/war.mask(a, b, size)` lane k follows LangRef's definition;   deps: DLT-12.2-P9.S1 (N7)
DLT-12.6-P10
  <1>1 [unc] `vector.extract`, `vector.insert`, `vector.reverse`, `vector.splice*`, `vector.interleave<n>`, `vector.deinterleave<n>`, `experimental.vector.compress`, `experimental.vector.histogram.*`, `experimental.vector.match`, `experimental.vector.extract.last.active`, `vector.partial.reduce.*`: lane permutations and folds by their LangRef definitions;
DLT-12.6-P11
````

### §12.7 Wide integers (N22)   [DLT-12.7]

````text
DLT-12.7-P1
  <1>1 [req] Let w_k = min(64, N − 64k).
  <1>2 [req] Limbs a_k, b_k (k = 0 … L − 1) have type i<w_k>.
  <1>3 [req] Every limb operation, constant and incoming carry uses w_k, with an identity conversion when widths agree (in particular an i1 carry into an i1 top limb needs no zext):
DLT-12.7-P2
  <1>1 [unc] `add`: `%nz.m0.r = add a_0, b_0`, `%nz.c0.r = icmp ult %nz.m0.r, a_0`; for k ≥ 1: `%nz.t<k>.r = add a_k, b_k`, `%nz.z<k>.r = zext i1 %nz.c<k−1>.r to i<w_k>`, `%nz.m<k>.r = add %nz.t<k>.r, %nz.z<k>.r` and, for k < L − 1, `%nz.u<k>.r = icmp ult %nz.t<k>.r, a_k`, `%nz.v<k>.r = icmp ult %nz.m<k>.r, %nz.t<k>.r`, `%nz.c<k>.r = or i1 %nz.u<k>.r, %nz.v<k>.r`;
DLT-12.7-P3
  <1>1 [req] `sub`: let beta_-1=0.
  <1>2 [req] At limb k compute t_k=a_k−b_k modulo 2^w_k, z_k=zero-extended beta_(k−1) (identity if w_k=1), and m_k=t_k−z_k modulo 2^w_k.
  <1>3 [req] When another limb follows, beta_k=(icmp ult a_k,b_k) OR (icmp ult t_k,z_k).
  <1>4 [unc] Discard the final outgoing borrow.
  <1>5 [req] Canonical names use `%nz.m0.r = sub a_0,b_0`, `%nz.c0.r = icmp ult a_0,b_0`; thereafter `%nz.t<k>.r`, `%nz.z<k>.r`, `%nz.m<k>.r`, `%nz.u<k>.r`, `%nz.v<k>.r`, `%nz.c<k>.r` in that order.
  <1>6 [req] In particular v_k tests t_k<z_k, never m_k<t_k.
  <1>7 [unc] This derives wrapping subtraction from [LangRef sub](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#sub-instruction) and [usub.with.overflow](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#llvm-usub-with-overflow-intrinsics);
DLT-12.7-P4
  <1>1 [req] `and`, `or`, `xor`: per limb; `icmp`: lexicographic from the top limb (signed on the top limb only for signed predicates); `select`, `phi`: per limb; `zext`, `sext`, `trunc`: limb copies; `sext` extracts the sign at bit w_top − 1, sign-extends the old partial top limb when widening it, and fills each new limb with that sign at its own width;
DLT-12.7-P5
  <1>1 [req] `mul`, `udiv`, `sdiv`, `urem`, `srem`, `shl`, `lshr`, `ashr`: calls of the runtimes entry points `__bennett_rt_i<N>_<op>(ptr r, ptr a, ptr b)` on shadow-stack slots holding the limbs (division by zero calls `__bennett_nz_fault(5)`, signed MIN / −1 `__bennett_nz_fault(6)`, shifts by ≥ N give 0 as N14);   deps: DLT-12.2-P16.S1 (N14), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
DLT-12.7-P6
  <1>1 [req] loads and stores: per limb at offsets 8k, little-endian targets (`%nz.p<k>.r` / `%nz.q<k>.r` for the addresses);
DLT-12.7-P7
  <1>1 [req] a wide parameter or result of an internal device function becomes L limb parameters or an aggregate of limbs.
DLT-12.7-P8
  <1>1 [req] **Pointer-index width witness.** With I=32 and pw=64, index 2^32 becomes zero.
  <1>2 [req] Adding one to low 0xffffffff wraps to zero without carrying into the high pointer bits.
  <1>3 [req] These materialised pairs are unchanged by normalisation; the state witness executes their §16.4 mapped objects, and native executes LLVM.   deps: DLT-16.4
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 21 lines) target datalayout = "e-p:64:64:64:32"
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 21 lines) target datalayout = "e-p:64:64:64:32"
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 4 lines) f(4294967296)=4294967296; code=0
  <1>7 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) {"f":[[4294967296,4294967296,0],[-1,8589934591,0]],"g":[[8589934591,1,4294967296…
````

### §12.8 Integer intrinsics (N27)   [DLT-12.8]

````text
DLT-12.8-P1
  <1>1 [req] At W>128, the arithmetic expansions below (smax/smin/umax/umin, abs, scmp/ucmp, .with.overflow and .sat families) are emitted at width W and legalised by the repeated N22 (§12.1): carries/borrows, lexicographic compares, and per-limb select.   deps: DLT-12.2-P24.S1 (N22), DLT-12.1
  <1>2 [req] Aggregate expansion results carry the limb tuple plus the flag; insertvalue/extractvalue transport these fields without a residual wide aggregate.
  <1>3 [req] Unsigned add overflow is the outgoing carry of the top limb; unsigned sub overflow is its outgoing borrow (retain it for the flag even though ordinary wrapping add/sub discards it).
  <1>4 [req] Signed overflow tests signs at the true top bit.
  <1>5 [req] Runtime rows bswap, bitreverse, ctpop, ctlz, cttz, fshl, fshr, sshl.sat, ushl.sat, smul/umul.fix[.sat], sdiv/udiv.fix[.sat], pdep, pext and clmul use `void @__bennett_rt_i<W>_<name>(ptr dst, ptr a[, ptr b, ptr c][, scalar flags])` instead of a wide call result/parameter.
  <1>6 [req] Each wide argument is a limb slot; scalar flags retain their declared types, in source order.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>7 [req] Counts ctpop/ctlz/cttz write a full W-bit result slot.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>8 [req] Slots use §13.1's push/store/call/load/pop order (destination first, then wide source operands in order); runtime fault rows are unchanged.   deps: DLT-13.1
  <1>9 [req] These are the same limb-slot conventions as wide mul/div in §12.7.   deps: DLT-12.7
DLT-12.8-P2
  <1>1 [req] `smax`, `smin`, `umax`, `umin` (a, b) | `%nz.c.r = icmp sgt/slt/ugt/ult a, b`; `%r = select i1 %nz.c.r, a, b`
DLT-12.8-P3
  <1>1 [req] `abs(a, false)` | `%nz.c.r = icmp slt a, 0`; `%nz.n.r = sub 0, a`; `%r = select i1 %nz.c.r, %nz.n.r, a`

DLT-12.8-P7

DLT-12.8-P8

DLT-12.8-P9
````

````text
  <1>1 [req] **Wide integer sides (M>128).** The preceding i128 conversion rows apply only to M≤128.
  <1>2 [req] For fptosi/fptoui, their .sat forms and lround/llround/lrint/llrint, N32 emits `void @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr d, C operand, i32 M)`.   deps: DLT-12.2-P34.S1 (N32)
  <1>3 [req] Here d is a shadow-stack slot of L=ceil(M/64) limbs at byte offsets 8k (§12.7).   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-12.7
  <1>4 [req] The entry writes the exact M-bit saturated result, limb 0 least significant, using truncation toward zero / the rounding rule above, with NaN mapped to 0; N22 loads each limb at width min(64,M−64k).   deps: DLT-12.2-P24.S1 (N22)
  <1>5 [req] For sitofp/uitofp from iM, store all limbs in slot s and call `C @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr s, i32 M)`, correctly rounded from the exact signed/unsigned M-bit integer.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>6 [unc] No i128 carrier intervenes.
  <1>7 [req] For canonical temporary slots, emit ss_push(8L,8), then limb GEP/store rows in increasing k; for destinations, push then call then limb GEP/load rows in increasing k; pop slots in reverse push order after their last use.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>8 [unc] Bitcast FP operands before destination push and FP results after pop.
  <1>9 [req] The usual shadow-stack checks, budget charges and linked-runtime materialisation apply.
  <1>10 [req] The helper's suffix op is fptosi/fptoui for the corresponding .sat form.
DLT-13.1-P25
  <1>1 [req] **Semantics of the entry points** (the contract with `bennett-arith`): IEEE 754-2019 for binary16 (`half`), binary32, binary64 and binary128; `bfloat` is the IEEE-style format with 8 exponent and 7 fraction bits; `x86_fp80` is the 80-bit x87 extended format (64-bit significand with explicit integer bit; encodings with exponent ≠ 0 and integer bit 0, the unnormals, and pseudo-denormals and pseudo-infinities are treated as NaN inputs, as modern x87 hardware does); `ppc_fp128` is double-double with the operations of LLVM's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` semantics of the major in use.
  <1>2 [req] For all formats: round to nearest ties to even, subnormals supported, no observable flags or exceptions (except §13.5); **every NaN result is the format's canonical NaN** (sign 0, quiet bit set, other payload bits 0: `0x7E00` f16, `0x7FC0` bf16, `0x7FC00000` f32, `0x7FF8000000000000` f64, `0x7FFFC000000000000000` f80, `0x7FFF8000…0` f128, and for ppcf128 the f64 canonical NaN in the high double and +0 in the low); `rem` is C `fmod` (exact, the sign of the dividend); `cmp` is LLVM's `fcmp` for the predicate; `fptosi`/`fptoui` and the `.sat` forms truncate toward zero, map NaN to 0 and saturate values outside the range of iM to its bounds, returned sign-extended (`fptosi`) or zero-extended (`fptoui`) in i128 when M≤128, and in the full limb slot when M>128; `sitofp`/`uitofp` round to nearest even; `minnum`/`maxnum` are IEEE 754-2008 minNum/maxNum (one NaN operand: the other operand; two: the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimum`/`maximum` are IEEE 754-2019 (either NaN: the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimumnum`/`maximumnum` are IEEE 754-2019 minimumNumber/ maximumNumber; `sqrt`, `fma` and every rounding function are correctly rounded (`round` ties away from zero, `rint` and `nearbyint` to nearest even); `fpext` is exact; `fptrunc` rounds to nearest even; `canonicalize` maps every NaN to the canonical NaN and is the identity otherwise; `is.fpclass` tests LangRef's ten classes on the bit pattern; `ldexp`/`frexp` are exact up to rounding of a subnormal `ldexp` result; `lround`/`llround` round half away from zero and `lrint`/ `llrint` to nearest even, saturating like `fptosi`. *(non-normative)* `fneg`, `fabs` and `copysign` stay bit-exact, so a NaN's sign bit set by `fneg` survives, as LangRef requires; an implementation of `half` and `bfloat` + − × ÷ √ in binary32 followed by one rounding is correctly rounded, since 24 ≥ 2p + 2 for p = 11 and p = 8 (Figueroa's double-rounding bound), but the contract is correct rounding, so `fma` needs its own implementation.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-13.5
````

### §13.4 Transcendentals and libm intrinsics (N33)   [DLT-13.4]

````text
DLT-13.4-P1
  <1>1 [def] `llvm.sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `asin`, `acos`, `atan`, `atan2`, `sinh`, `cosh`, `tanh`, `exp`, `exp2`, `exp10`, `log`, `log2`, `log10`, `pow`, `powi`, `sincos`, `sincospi` and `modf` become calls of the **runtimes libm**: `@<name>` for `double`, `@<name>f` for `float`, and `@<name>l` for the target's native `long double` format D (x86-64: `x86_fp80`; AArch64 and RISC-V64: `fp128`).
  <1>2 [req] For every other source format B, including half, bfloat and non-native x86_fp80/fp128/ppc_fp128, use a direct own-format carrier call `C_B @__bennett_sf_<name>_<suffix>(C_B, …)`; C_B and suffix are exactly §13.1's table.   deps: DLT-13.1
  <1>3 [req] No conversion through D is emitted.
  <1>4 [req] All seven source formats remain covered on every supported platform; a format different from D is neither rejected nor trapped.   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (trapped)
  <1>5 [req] This route preserves the source format's precision and cannot request a narrowing fpext or an equal-size cross-format cast.
DLT-13.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] For these carrier calls, bitcast each floating operand k to its **own** carrier in operand order as `%nz.<k>.r`, call as `%nz.i.r`, and bitcast the scalar floating result back as `%r` (§13.1).   deps: DLT-13.1
  <1>2 [req] Retain non-floating operands and their types in source order.
  <1>3 [req] These linked bennett-arith/runtimes functions implement the named operation numerically in B, using the same deterministic, versioned implementation in both builds; they are not bit reinterpretations between FP formats and do not use the host C long-double ABI.
  <1>4 [req] NaN results use §13.1's canonical encoding, with signed zeros preserved where the operation requires.   deps: DLT-13.1
  <1>5 [req] As for the native-format libm below, no correct-rounding claim is made.
DLT-13.4-P3
  <1>1 [req] `powi(x,n)` retains repeated-squaring semantics (compiler-rt's `__powidf2`), with the integer exponent unconverted; its carrier spelling is `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<suffix>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` at the intrinsic's exponent width K (the floating-ABI spelling remains `__bennett_rt_powi_B`).
  <1>2 [req] `sincos(x)` is the pair (sin x, cos x), `sincospi(x)` the pair (sinpi x, cospi x), and `modf(x)` the pair (fractional, integral).
  <1>3 [req] Their carrier entry points return `{ C_B, C_B }`.
  <1>4 [req] For powi and these pair operations use this carrier route on every format, avoiding the C pointer-output ABI.
  <1>5 [unc] Reconstruct pair fields in order j=0,1: `%nz.outbits<j>.r = extractvalue` of `%nz.i.r`, then bitcast to B as `%nz.outfp<j>.r`, then insert into `{ B, B }` (first into zeroinitializer as `%nz.out0.r`, finally as `%r`).
  <1>6 [req] Thus the special operations retain their semantics and field order under the same format policy.
DLT-13.4-P4
  <1>1 [req] The runtimes libm is musl-derived and compiled through this pipeline, so it is soft-float code itself and the same function runs in both builds; calls of `sin`, `exp`, … in source resolve to it by §4.5.   deps: DLT-13.4-P1.S1 (runtimes libm), DLT-4.5
  <1>2 [req] The certificate records the runtimes/arith versions.
  <1>3 [req] No correct-rounding claim is required; bit-exact determinism is (NS §3.2).
  <1>4 [req] The carrier routines are linked bitcode definitions, normalised by this same pipeline, not new ISA primitives or unresolved host calls.   deps: DLT-4.5-P4.S1 (host call)
````

### §13.5 Constrained intrinsics, `strictfp` and the floating-point environment   [DLT-13.5]

````text
DLT-13.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] `llvm.experimental.constrained.<op>(…, metadata <rounding>, metadata <except>)` becomes the soft-float call of §13.1 with the suffix `_rm` and an extra `i32 R` operand: R = 0 for `round.tonearest`, 1 `round.downward`, 2 `round.upward`, 3 `round.towardzero`, 4 `round.tonearestaway`, and for `round.dynamic` the value loaded from the runtimes global `__bennett_rt_fenv` (field `round`); when `<except>` is not `fpexcept.ignore`, the entry point ORs the IEEE flags it raises (invalid 1, divbyzero 2, overflow 4, underflow 8, inexact 16) into `__bennett_rt_fenv` (field `flags`).   deps: DLT-13.1
  <1>2 [req] `llvm.get.rounding`, `set.rounding`, `get.fpenv`, `set.fpenv`, `reset.fpenv`, `get.fpmode`, `set.fpmode`, `reset.fpmode` read and write that state; musl's `<fenv.h>` functions in `bennett-runtimes` do the same.
  <1>3 [req] Unconstrained operations use round to nearest and raise no flags, as LLVM assumes of the default environment.
  <1>4 [req] A `strictfp` call-site attribute on a marker is benign (§3.6).   deps: DLT-3.6
````

````text
DLT-14.6-P1
  <1>1 [def] **Switch primitives**: the runtimes' `swapcontext`, `makecontext`, `setcontext`, Julia's task switch (`jl_swap_fiber`, `ctx_switch`), and the platform shims `swtch` and `__switch_to` (§20.5); all are implemented by `__bennett_rt_fiber_switch(from, to)` over records that hold a fiber's shadow stack (the `makecontext` stack argument) and its saved frame chain.   deps: DLT-20.5
DLT-14.6-P2
  <1>1 [req] Every function on a call-graph path to a switch primitive is **instrumented**, Asyncify-style: its SSA values that are live across a call that may switch are spilled to its shadow frame before the call and reloaded after it, and it gets a resume index (the call site it was suspended at).
DLT-14.6-P3
  <1>1 [req] A switch sets the EH state to `active` = 3 and propagates (§14.4): each instrumented frame on the way out saves its resume index and returns early, up to the fiber's base; the runtime records the chain, then **rewinds** the target fiber: it calls the target's base with the rewind state, and each instrumented frame jumps to its saved resume index, reloads its values and calls the next frame, until the target's switch call returns.   deps: DLT-14.4-P2.S1 (EH state), DLT-14.4
DLT-14.6-P4
  <1>1 [req] This works in CC, R and L on ISA 0.8: it is ordinary code over memory.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>2 [req] G-16 (ISA fibers, §25.3) is the efficient alternative.   deps: DLT-25.3
DLT-14.6-P5
  <1>1 [req] A `swapcontext` ping-pong in the R tier: the main fiber logs 0, 1, 2 and the second fiber 100, 101, 102, alternately; the status encodes the order (digits 0 0 1 1 2 2 give 1122, and 1122 mod 256 = 98):
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 29 lines) #include <ucontext.h>
DLT-14.6-P6
  <1>1 [req] The same source as a whole-program CC entry has no R entry and is an accepted N29/N30 witness; native success is checked against the same cc-run result.   deps: DLT-12.2-P31.S1 (N29), DLT-12.2-P32.S1 (N30), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 29 lines) #include <ucontext.h>
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) BD-B-ISA7 R entry: BLK-5 (ISA-7); mandatory pre-instruction typed fault 1 requir…   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5), DLT-25.2-P2.S1 (ISA-7)
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 6 lines) driver: --bennett-tier=reversible --bennett-entry=main
````

````text
DLT-14.8-P1
  <1>1 [req] `switch` lowers by the compare tree of §16.4 in L, and by the same tree of `icmp`/`br` in CC and R (ISA has no switch instruction).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-16.4
````

### §14.9 `blockaddress` and `indirectbr`   [DLT-14.9]

````text
DLT-14.9-P1
  <1>1 [req] `blockaddress(@f, %bb)` has the device value B(f) + i, where i is the index of %bb among f's k address-taken blocks in layout order and B(f) is the base of a k-byte object `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>` that the driver adds to the data section of the comparison link (§19.5) and whose address the link map records.   deps: DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (comparison link), DLT-19.5
  <1>2 [req] These values are nonzero, pairwise distinct and distinct from every A(F) and every other object's bytes, so comparisons and dispatch on them are exact.
  <1>3 [req] `indirectbr ptr v, [labels]` dispatches with the compare tree over the labels' values to static branches (`qjump` in L, `br` in CC/R); no match is F(4). *(non-normative)* LangRef permits a `blockaddress` only in `indirectbr` and in comparisons with null; an execution in which its bits influence a result otherwise (printing, hashing) is layout-dependent and validated against the reference interpreter (NS §3.2; §17.1).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-17.1
````

### §15.1 Table format   [DLT-15.1]

````text

DLT-15.1-P2

DLT-15.1-P3

DLT-15.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] A row has a **class** and a **replacement** in IR.

  <1>3 [req] The rows of this section are normative; they are also published as data files `spec/asm/<arch>.toml` (`asm 0.2`, one table per architecture with the fields `asm`, `constraints`, `class`, `replacement`), generated from this section; the section prevails on a difference.
  <1>4 [req] Rows are added with a version change (§23).   deps: DLT-23
````
