### §16 The lifting map (map 0.2.9)   [DLT-16]

````text
DLT-16-P1
  <1>1 [def] The lifting map turns a materialised region function, a binding shape (§8.4) and the link layout (§19.5) into the **reference object** of that shape: the ISA object whose oracle `@ref` defines F_B and the budget unit by the reference interpreter (ISA §4.2 "Reference body and binding", §14.1).   deps: DLT-8.4, DLT-19.5, ISA-4.2, DLT-14.1
  <1>2 [req] It is a function of its inputs alone, so two plugins on the same LLVM version and pipeline produce byte-identical reference objects.   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object)
  <1>3 [req] Its version (`map 0.2.9`) is the certificate's `dialect_mapping_version` (ISA §12.3).   deps: ISA-12.3
  <1>4 [req] The plugin's device objects (§17.4) are plugin-defined; they MUST be extensionally equal to the reference objects (ISA §5.7, T6; §18.7).   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), DLT-17.4, ISA-5.7, DLT-18.7
````

### §16.1 Liftable means materialised, always   [DLT-16.1]

````text
DLT-16.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] Every materialised function is liftable (outline B1): §12 legalises every construct into integer SSA values of at most 128 bits, memory, calls and branches at the map boundary.   deps: DLT-12
  <1>2 [req] Materialised LLVM may still contain bit-exact FP forms and aggregates; §16.4 maps these through integer carriers and field locations.   deps: DLT-16.4
  <1>3 [req] Every such accepted form has a row.
  <1>4 [req] 0.1's `BD-E-LIFT-SUBSET` is deleted.
````

### §16.2 The reference object   [DLT-16.2]

````text
DLT-16.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] For a shape with region function f, the reference object is the ISA text (ISA §16) with:   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), ISA-16
DLT-16.2-P2
  <1>1 [unc] the header `bennett-object isa=0.8 object=0.8 launch=0.8 sched=0.8 cert=0.8 coverage=0.8 target=0.8 ptr_width=<pw>`;
DLT-16.2-P3
  <1>1 [ex] `section symbols`: one JCS line, keys `"0"`, `"1"`, … in the order `@ref`, `@L1` … `@Ln`, then the data symbols `G…` in `section data` order, each with its kind, name, signature (ISA §9.1; `args` types spelled as JCS strings of the ISA §16.2 type, e.g. `"i64"`) and interface hash (ISA §9.2);   deps: DLT-21.1-P2.S1 (L1), ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2, ISA-9.2
DLT-16.2-P4
  <1>1 [req] `section functions`: `@ref`, then the lprocs `@L1` … `@Ln`: one per function of the L closure of f (direct calls and indirect-call candidates), numbered in depth-first preorder from `@ref` along the `lift.call` rows in text order;   deps: DLT-21.1-P2.S1 (L1)
DLT-16.2-P5
  <1>1 [unc] `section data`: one line `G<n> <hex>` per global of the layout below, in `layout.globals` key order, with the global's initialiser bytes after §12 (function addresses encoded as A(F) in P(a)-bit little-endian at each pointer field, §4.4; pointers to globals as their bases plus offsets);   deps: DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (function address), DLT-12, DLT-4.4
DLT-16.2-P6
  <1>1 [unc] `section layout`: one JCS line (§16.3).   deps: DLT-16.3
DLT-16.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] **The wrapper `@ref`.** Its header is `oracle @ref (<ins>) (tgt:wire[T]) (fault:wire[4]) (<mems>) (<args>)`:   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
DLT-16.2-P8
  <1>1 [unc] `<ins>`: `x<k>:wire[n] in` for the k-th `IN` binding in parameter order (n = n(q));   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
DLT-16.2-P9
  <1>1 [req] T: the width of the variant's slice (§8.6), or 1 when the shape has no output;   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-8.6
DLT-16.2-P10
  <1>1 [req] `<mems>`: first `coh:region[A,8] in` when a slice or oracle reads an arena object, then `m<k>:region[A,8] in` for the k-th `MEM_IN` binding in parameter order, then `e0` for an ENTROPY binding, `k0` for a BLOCK binding, `s0` for an entry-state snapshot (§8.5);   deps: DLT-8.5
DLT-16.2-P11
  <1>1 [unc] `<args>`: `a<k>:i<W>` for the k-th `ARG` binding in parameter order (W = P(a) for a `ptr addrspace(a)`).
DLT-16.2-P12
  <1>1 [req] Its body is one block `^entry():` with, in order:
DLT-16.2-P13
  <1>1 [req] **Prologue** (each part only when needed): the entry-state replay `t = lift.call @L(__bennett_rt_entry_replay) (s)` with `s = lift.addr @s0`; the device-state stores of §11.3 (for ENTROPY: `d0 = lift.addr @e0`, `d1 = lift.addr @G(base)`, `lift.boundscheck d1 8`, `lift.store.i64 d1 d0`, `d2 = lift.const.i64 <2^A>`, `d3 = lift.addr @G(size)`, `lift.boundscheck d3 8`, `lift.store.i64 d3 d2`; the position starts at its data image 0; the other models likewise in model order); the shadow-stack setup `t = lift.call @L(__bennett_rt_ss_init) (c)` with c = `lift.const.i64 <sstack>` (baseline for the provisional object, resolved for the final object under §9.2; no rematching) when the closure contains a call of `__bennett_rt_ss_push` (§17.3).   deps: DLT-11.3, DLT-9.2, DLT-17.3
DLT-16.2-P14
  <1>1 [unc] **Arguments**, for each parameter i in order: `IN` with n = W: the pseudo-location `x<k>` itself; `IN` with n < W: `w<i> = lift.zext.iW x<k>` (`lift.sext` for SEXT); `ARG`: `a<k>` itself; `MEM_IN`: `w<i> = lift.addr @m<k>`; `OUT`: `w<i> = lift.alloc <size>`.
DLT-16.2-P15
  <1>1 [req] **Call**: `r = lift.call @L1 (<arguments>)` (`r:reg[R]`, R the return width, 1 for `void`).   deps: DLT-21.1-P2.S1 (L1)
DLT-16.2-P16
  <1>1 [def] **Unwind check** (only when f may unwind, §14.4): `e = lift.addr @G(__bennett_rt_eh)`, `lift.boundscheck e 4`, `l = lift.load.i32 e`, `z = lift.const.i32 0`, `u = lift.icmp.ne l z`, `lift.faultif u 4`.   deps: DLT-14.4
  <1>2 [req] A function **may unwind** iff it is not `nounwind` and calls (directly or through a candidate) a function that may unwind, an external function without `nounwind`, or one of the raise functions of §14.4 (least fixpoint).   deps: DLT-14.4
DLT-16.2-P17
  <1>1 [def] **Common delivery**, identical in every W_j: compute and validate *every* payload slice in output/slice order (§8.6), including all address offsets, bounds checks, loads, casts, dropped-bit checks and console slices.   deps: DLT-8.6
  <1>2 [req] Store each projection in `p<j>:reg[w_j]`.
  <1>3 [req] Only after this identical sequence does `lift.retval p<j>` select this variant's result.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>4 [req] For k=0 there are no projections or dropped-return-bit checks: discard r and emit `p0 = lift.const.i1 0` followed by `lift.retval p0`, with `p0:reg[1]`.
  <1>5 [req] These two terminal rows are part of the charged reference body, after the call and any unwind check.
  <1>6 [unc] A fault still delivers code through the four-wire fault interface and zero payload to the hidden bit.
  <1>7 [req] For k=1 retain the original direct result without a projection move.
  <1>8 [req] Every variant has the same location list and row charges, including unused projections.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>9 [req] The per-slice computation is: for the `tgt` output with n(tgt) < R: `c = lift.const.iR <n(tgt)>`, `t = lift.lshr r c`, `z = lift.const.iR 0`, `u = lift.icmp.ne t z`, `lift.faultif u 4` (the dropped-bits check of §8.2, in every variant); then the slice value: from `r` by `lift.lshr` of its low bit position (when nonzero) and `lift.trunc`/`lift.zext` to T (when the widths differ); from an `OUT` buffer `w<i>` by `p = lift.add w<i> <offset>` (when nonzero), `lift.boundscheck p <T/8>`, `v = lift.load.i<T> p`; then `p<j> = lift.mov <value>`; after all slices, `lift.retval p<selected>`.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-8.2
DLT-16.2-P18
  <1>1 [req] Wrapper locations are `w<i>` (arguments), `r` (the call's result), `d<k>` (prologue), `p<j>` (delivery projections, including synthetic `p0` for k=0), and `c<j>`, `t<j>` for its constants and temporaries in emission order.
DLT-16.2-P19
  <1>1 [req] **lprocs.** Each lifted function g becomes `lproc @L<n> (a0:reg[W0], …) (r:reg[R]) () () ()` with the parameters in order (`ptr addrspace(a)` → `reg[P(a)]`; an aggregate parameter flattened into one parameter per field in field order), the result `r` of the return width (1 for `void`, whose `ret void` is `lift.ret r`), a `regs` line, and the blocks of §16.4.   deps: DLT-16.4
DLT-16.2-P20
  <1>1 [req] The entry-state dependency decision of §8.5 precedes every private allocation in the wrapper prologue (§16.2), including device buffers and ss_init.   deps: DLT-8.5, DLT-16.2
  <1>2 [req] Replay allocates the caller's sizes in order, frees dead entries, and preserves bump/liveness even when no caller byte is read.
  <1>3 [req] No fresh allocator reset is permitted for an allocation-only closure.
````

### §16.3 Names and layout   [DLT-16.3]

````text
DLT-16.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] the i-th parameter of g | `a<i>` (fields of a flattened aggregate `a<i>_<f>`)
DLT-16.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] the k-th instruction of g that produces a value (0-based, in layout order, phis included) | location `v<k>` (fields of an aggregate value `v<k>_<f>`)
DLT-16.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] the j-th constant materialisation of g, in text order of the output | location `c<j>`
DLT-16.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] the j-th mapping temporary of g, in text order | location `t<j>`
DLT-16.3-P5
  <1>1 [req] the i-th basic block of g (0-based, layout order) | block `^b<i>`
DLT-16.3-P6
  <1>1 [req] a block created by a compare tree in block i (§16.4), in creation order | `^b<i>.s<j>` (j ≥ 1)   deps: DLT-16.4
DLT-16.3-P7
  <1>1 [req] for the q-th indirect call of block i (q ≥ 1): the leaf of candidate k (sorted order), the default, the continuation | `^b<i>.q<q>c<k>`, `^b<i>.q<q>d`, `^b<i>.q<q>r`
DLT-16.3-P8
  <1>1 [req] the edge block from block i to block s | `^b<i>.e<s>`   deps: DLT-16.4-P38.S3 (edge block)
DLT-16.3-P9
  <1>1 [req] the n-th global of the layout (1-based, in link order) | data symbol and global `G<n>`
DLT-16.3-P10
  <1>1 [req] The `regs` line lists every location of the function that is neither a parameter nor the result, in order of first appearance as a destination in the text, as `name:reg[W]`, separated by `, `; it is omitted when empty.
  <1>2 [unc] W: a value's width (`i1` → 1, `ptr addrspace(a)` → P(a)); a constant's width; a temporary's width (1 for a compare, else its operation's width).
DLT-16.3-P11
  <1>1 [req] **Layout.** The reference object's `layout` is the device link layout (§19.5) restricted to the non-arena globals that the L closure names in `lift.addr` and, transitively, non-arena globals their initialisers point to (in link order, numbered G1, G2, …), with the link's `ptr_width`, `null_size`, `heap_base`, `heap_limit_max`, `H`, `A_tab` and `S_cells`, and `interface_regions` = { "ref": the shape's regions }.   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), DLT-19.5
  <1>2 [req] An arena-relocated global has no G<n> symbol or data image: its address is @coh plus its arena offset.
  <1>3 [req] `coh` is placed at A_coh (§19.5), disjoint from globals and heap.   deps: DLT-19.5
  <1>4 [req] Bases of all other regions follow `<mems>` order: each base is the smallest multiple of the region's size that is ≥ the end of the previous range (the heap's end `heap_base + heap_limit_max` for the first when there is no arena; otherwise the arena's end).
  <1>5 [req] H is the smallest value with every range inside [0, 2^H); A_tab is the smallest value with 2^A_tab ≥ P + `heap_limit_max`, P the link's number of globals plus the largest number of interface regions of any oracle, so that allocation-table overflow never precedes heap exhaustion (both fault with code 2, ISA §4.2, and a reference object with fewer pre-entries than the device object faults at the same allocation); S_cells is the smallest value with 2^S_cells ≥ the frozen `stack` capacity of §9.2.   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), ISA-4.2, DLT-9.2
  <1>6 [req] These link capacities are frozen before provisional identity construction and include unmatched raw entries and static non-sentinel record fields; identity overlays never recompute them.
````

### §16.4 Rows   [DLT-16.4]

````text
DLT-16.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] Pointer constants, flattened pointer fields, parameters, results and pointer memory values use P(a), including their DataLayout storage size.
  <1>2 [unc] Materialise null at P(a).
  <1>3 [req] Before an ISA memory address operand, bridge the source pointer representation to pw with the target's address conversion (zero-extension/truncation for the integral flat-address spaces); this bridge never changes the stored pointer width or the source ptrtoint/inttoptr semantics.
  <1>4 [req] Apply the same rules to constant expressions and the native comparison build.
  <1>5 [req] Representations above 128 bits use §12.7 limbs; mixed-width layouts are not rejected.   deps: DLT-12.7
DLT-16.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] Every row accessing program memory first performs TDL-4's logical validation if the address can be in coh, before the physical ISA boundscheck.   deps: DLT-10-P19.S1 (TDL-4)
  <1>2 [req] Loads and stores, including slice/event producers, use the same helper and fault gating.
  <1>3 [req] Proved containment in a static arena global (as in arena-o1's one-byte @cnt load) discharges this check without extra rows; a physical coh entry alone is never such a proof.
  <1>4 [req] For an address that can lie in shadow-stack backing storage, also perform §17.3's logical-frame check before every program load, store or memory-function access, including non-coh addresses.   deps: DLT-17.3
  <1>5 [req] The materialised __bennett_rt_ss_check(p,n,table) helper's comparisons, calls and fault rows count normally.
  <1>6 [unc] Gate mutations and dependent events on its success.
  <1>7 [req] Physical boundscheck/load/store rows remain required; they do not substitute for the logical check.
  <1>8 [req] Explicit runtime-internal bookkeeping uses the internal path of §17.3, not the program-access helper.   deps: DLT-17.3
DLT-16.4-P3
  <1>1 [req] Rows of an instruction are emitted in instruction order within its block.
  <1>2 [def] Before the rows of an instruction, each of its **constant operands** that is a value operand (in operand order; not an immediate flag; for `getelementptr`, not a constant index) is materialised: an integer constant by `c<j> = lift.const.iW <unsigned decimal>`; `null` by `lift.const.i<P(a)> 0`; a non-arena global `@G` by `c<j> = lift.addr @G<n>`; an arena global by `c<j> = lift.addr @coh`, and if its arena offset δ is nonzero, `c<j+1> = lift.const.i<pw> δ`, `t<j> = lift.add c<j> c<j+1>` (the operand reads this temporary); in an lproc the base is its hidden arena-base parameter rather than lift.addr @coh.
  <1>3 [req] The wrapper materialises @coh once before the region call, appends this pw-bit argument after source parameters, and every transitive lproc call threads it unchanged.
  <1>4 [req] Only functions in the arena-reading closure receive it; materialising the base in a lproc uses lift.mov of that parameter.
  <1>5 [unc] Materialise a function `@F` by `c<j> = lift.const.i<pw> <A(F)>`; a constant expression by the rows of its instructions, innermost first, into temporaries.
  <1>6 [req] The operand then reads `c<j>`.
  <1>7 [unc] Aggregate constants materialise one scalar carrier constant per flattened field in field order before packing.
  <1>8 [req] A non-constant operand reads the location of its value (`a<i>` for a parameter).
  <1>9 [req] `A`, `B`, `C` below are the operands so read; `v` is the instruction's location.
DLT-16.4-P4
  <1>1 [req] `add sub mul and or xor shl lshr ashr` | `v = lift.<op> A B`
DLT-16.4-P5
  <1>1 [req] `udiv sdiv urem srem` | `v = lift.<op> A B` (division faults, ISA §4.2)   deps: ISA-4.2
DLT-16.4-P6
  <1>1 [req] `icmp eq/ne/ult/ule/slt/sle a, b` (integers or pointers) | `v = lift.icmp.<pred> A B`
DLT-16.4-P7
  <1>1 [req] `icmp ugt/uge/sgt/sge a, b` | `v = lift.icmp.{ult,ule,slt,sle} B A` (operands swapped)
DLT-16.4-P8
  <1>1 [req] `select c, a, b` | `v = lift.select C A B`
DLT-16.4-P9
  <1>1 [req] `zext/sext/trunc a to iM` | `v = lift.<op>.iM A`
DLT-16.4-P10
  <1>1 [req] `ptrtoint` to iM, `inttoptr` from iM | Let P=P(a) for the pointer operand/result.
  <1>2 [req] M = P: `v = lift.mov A`; ptrtoint M < P or inttoptr M > P: `lift.trunc` to the destination width; otherwise `lift.zext` to the destination width (LangRef zero-extends)
DLT-16.4-P11
  <1>1 [req] `bitcast` of equal-width integer/FP carriers or pointers | `v = lift.mov A`; FP constants materialise their carrier bit pattern
DLT-16.4-P12
  <1>1 [req] `addrspacecast` | materialise the incoming target's specified conversion between address-space representations, including any non-width conversion; `lift.mov` only for bit-preserving equal-width conversions.
  <1>2 [req] For integral zero-extend/truncate conversions emit `lift.zext`/`lift.trunc` to P(destination)
DLT-16.4-P13
  <1>1 [req] `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | Let P=P(a), I=I(a) for p's address space.
  <1>2 [req] Arithmetic on the low I bits is modulo 2^I; only representation bits above I are preserved, also for constant expressions.
  <1>3 [req] Canonical rows: acc is the pointer operand (when I<P, first `t = lift.trunc.i<I> P_operand` and acc is t).
  <1>4 [req] For each non-constant sequential index in index order: sign-extend/truncate to I (omit the cast when already I wide); if its element allocation size s≠1 emit `c = lift.const.i<I> (s mod 2^I)` then `t = lift.mul index c`, else use the index directly; emit `acc′ = lift.add acc t`.
  <1>5 [req] Constant sequential indices, interpreted signed at their source width then scaled by s, and constant structure field offsets accumulate into Δ mod 2^I, with no per-index rows.
  <1>6 [req] After the dynamic sequence, if Δ≠0 emit `c = lift.const.i<I> Δ` then `acc′ = lift.add acc c`.
  <1>7 [req] For I=P the last acc′ row writes v; with no dynamic index and Δ=0 the only row is `v = lift.mov P_operand`.
  <1>8 [req] For I<P always emit recombination, even when acc equals trunc(P_operand), in this order: `lo = lift.zext.i<P> acc`; `c = lift.const.i<P> <ones above I>`; `hi = lift.and P_operand c`; `v = lift.or hi lo`.
  <1>9 [req] Every emitted row is charged normally
DLT-16.4-P14
  <1>1 [req] `load T, ptr p` (T = iN with N a multiple of 8, or `ptr`; N ≤ 128 after §12; FP uses its carrier) | `lift.boundscheck P <N/8>`; `v = lift.load.iN P`   deps: DLT-12
DLT-16.4-P15
  <1>1 [req] `store v, ptr p` | `lift.boundscheck P <N/8>`; `lift.store.iN P V`
DLT-16.4-P16
  <1>1 [req] `insertvalue`, `extractvalue` | `lift.mov` of the depth-first flattened field locations (one per scalar field); unchanged fields remain their input locations
DLT-16.4-P17
  <1>1 [req] FP `load`, `store`, `select`, `phi` | the corresponding integer-carrier row, bit-exact
DLT-16.4-P18
  <1>1 [req] `fneg`, `llvm.fabs`, `llvm.copysign` | for IEEE/x87 formats, xor sign mask / and complement sign mask / clear magnitude sign then or source sign, at carrier width (sign bit 15, 31, 63, 79 or 127).
  <1>2 [req] For ppc_fp128, fneg flips both component signs; fabs conditionally flips both using the high double's sign; copysign conditionally flips both according to differing high signs, preserving APFloat PPCDoubleDouble semantics
DLT-16.4-P19
  <1>1 [req] aggregate `ret` of packed width ≤128 | zext each field to packed width (identity if equal), shift by cumulative prior field widths (omit zero shift), or in field order; `lift.ret` packed value
DLT-16.4-P20
  <1>1 [req] aggregate call result | `lift.call` into a packed-width temporary, then for each field lshr by cumulative offset (omit zero shift), trunc to field width (identity if equal)
DLT-16.4-P21
  <1>1 [req] `phi` | none at its position (edge moves)
DLT-16.4-P22
  <1>1 [req] direct `call @g(args)` of a defined function | `v = lift.call @L(g) (A_1 … A_m)` (flattened arguments); a `void` callee writes a temporary `t<j>:reg[1]`
DLT-16.4-P23
  <1>1 [req] `call @__bennett_nz_alloc(n)`, `call @__bennett_nz_free(p)` | `v = lift.alloc N`; `lift.free P`
DLT-16.4-P24
  <1>1 [req] `call void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64 C)`; `unreachable` | `fault C` (one row for the pair)
DLT-16.4-P25
  <1>1 [req] indirect call | the compare tree below over the candidates' A(F_k) (§14.2) on the callee value, leaf k `v = lift.call @L(F_k) (…)` then `qjump` to the continuation, default `fault 4`; the rows after the call continue in the continuation block   deps: DLT-14.2
DLT-16.4-P26
  <1>1 [req] `ret a` | `lift.ret A` (`void`: `lift.ret r`)
DLT-16.4-P27
  <1>1 [req] `unreachable` | `fault 4`
DLT-16.4-P28
  <1>1 [req] `br label %S` | edge moves (B → S); `qjump ^S`
DLT-16.4-P29
  <1>1 [req] `br i1 c, label %T, label %F`, T ≠ F | `qbranch C ^T′ ^F′`
DLT-16.4-P30
  <1>1 [req] `br i1 c, label %S, label %S` | as `br label %S`
DLT-16.4-P31
  <1>1 [req] `switch iW c, label %D [k_j, label %L_j]` | the compare tree below on C, cases sorted unsigned, default D′
DLT-16.4-P32
  <1>1 [req] `indirectbr` | the compare tree over the block values of §14.9, leaves `qjump`   deps: DLT-14.9
DLT-16.4-P33
  <1>1 [def] **Compare tree** (outline E6) over sorted distinct keys k_lo … k_hi of width W, condition C, targets T_j and default D, emitted into the current block X by node(lo, hi, X):
DLT-16.4-P34
  <1>1 [req] if hi − lo + 1 ≤ 3: for j = lo … hi, in block X: `c = lift.const.iW k_j`; `t = lift.icmp.eq C c`; `qbranch t ^T_j ^Y`, where Y is a new block `^b<i>.s<n>` for j < hi and D for j = hi; X := Y;
DLT-16.4-P35
  <1>1 [unc] otherwise, with mid = ⌊(lo + hi)/2⌋: in X: `c = lift.const.iW k_mid`; `t = lift.icmp.ult C c`; new blocks L then R; `qbranch t ^L ^R`; node(lo, mid − 1, L); node(mid, hi, R);
DLT-16.4-P36
  <1>1 [unc] with no key: `qjump ^D`.
DLT-16.4-P37
  <1>1 [req] **Round-5 pointer and canonical-row witnesses.** For pointer-as270, truncation at P(270)=32 followed by zero-extension gives 0 and 1.

  <1>3 [req] The constant index 2^32 accumulates Δ=0, so the constant-expression GEP emits exactly trunc/zext/const/and/or, with no add or multiply.
  <1>4 [req] Counts include constant-expression operands, casts, return and wrapper.
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 9 lines) target datalayout = "e-p:64:64-p270:32:32-p271:32:32-p272:64:64"
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) null_size=4096 heap_base=4096 heap_limit_max=0 stack max 32
````

### §16.5 Runtime oracles, identity and F_B   [DLT-16.5]

````text
DLT-16.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] The CC tier's entry-state transfer (§8.5) and TDL (§10) use three families of runtime oracles, generated per width and part of the device object:   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-8.5-P2.S14 (entry-state transfer), DLT-8.5, DLT-10
DLT-16.5-P2
  <1>1 [unc] `@xor128`: `oracle @xor128 () (t:wire[128]) (f:wire[4]) () (lo:i64, hi:i64)` with body `a = lift.zext.i128 lo`, `b = lift.zext.i128 hi`, `s = lift.const.i128 64`, `h = lift.shl b s`, `v = lift.or h a`, `lift.retval v` (6 steps; memoryless, ISA §7.2);   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-7.2
DLT-16.5-P3
  <1>1 [unc] `@xorargs<A>`: payload = a mod 2^A | m · 2^A for the classical arguments (a: i64, m: i8), a (A + 8)-wire target (TDL-4);   deps: DLT-10-P19.S1 (TDL-4)
DLT-16.5-P4
````

### §17.1 Memory model   [DLT-17.1]

````text
DLT-17.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] There is one classical memory, shared by H, CC, R and the entry state of every L invocation (§8.5; NS §3.2):   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-8.5
DLT-17.1-P2
  <1>1 [def] **Live allocations**: a shadow-stack frame from its push to its pop (§17.3); a global, always; a heap allocation of the runtimes allocator from its allocation to its `free` (`free` of a null pointer does nothing).   deps: DLT-17.3
  <1>2 [req] The null region is never allocated.
DLT-17.1-P3
  <1>1 [def] **Zero fill**: shadow-stack frames and heap allocations are zero-filled in both builds; globals hold their initialisers.
  <1>2 [req] Region inputs are not zero-filled: they are the bytes the host supplies (NS §3.2).
DLT-17.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] **Bounds**: a load, store or memory-function range is valid iff all its bytes lie in one live allocation (an address model: the object a pointer was derived from is irrelevant; NS §3.2 "faulting memory access is defined").
DLT-17.1-P5
  <1>1 [req] **Addresses**: the comparison link (§19.5) places globals and functions; the runtimes allocator's fixed base places the heap; the shadow stack is heap memory, so native stack addresses never reach device results.   deps: DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (comparison link), DLT-19.5
  <1>2 [req] The residue is host-stack addresses passed in by host code: executions in which they influence a result are *layout-dependent* (NS §3.2; §24 D12), validated against the reference interpreter.   deps: DLT-24-P2.S1 (D12), DLT-24
DLT-17.1-P6
  <1>1 [def] **Single thread**: atomics and volatile are sequentialised (N9; NS §3.2; §24 D24).   deps: DLT-12.2-P11.S1 (N9), DLT-24-P8.S1 (D24), DLT-24
````

### §17.3 Shadow stack (N24)   [DLT-17.3]

````text
DLT-17.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] NS §3.2: "Region allocas are rewritten by the normaliser onto a shim-managed shadow stack in both builds where possible"; in device variants it is always possible.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>2 [unc] The runtimes provide, with these contracts:
DLT-17.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] `void __bennett_rt_ss_init(i64 size)` | is idempotent: if stack state is already set (by entry replay in an oracle), allocates nothing and keeps base, size and stack pointer; otherwise allocates one heap block of `size` bytes (`sstack`, §9.2), by `__bennett_nz_alloc`; called by the oracle wrapper prologue (§16.2), the R entry stub and the CC entry stub when their closure uses the shadow stack   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-9.2, DLT-16.2
DLT-17.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] `ptr __bennett_rt_ss_push(i64 size, i64 align)` | pushes a frame of `size` bytes aligned to `align` and returns its base; the frame is zero-filled; overflow of the block is `__bennett_nz_fault(2)` (`FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`)
DLT-17.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] `void __bennett_rt_ss_pop(ptr fp)` | pops the frame fp and every frame pushed after it
DLT-17.3-P5
  <1>1 [req] `ptr __bennett_rt_ss_alloca(i64 n, i64 align)` | a zero-filled dynamic allocation in the current frame, released by its pop
DLT-17.3-P6
  <1>1 [req] `ptr __bennett_rt_ss_save()`, `void __bennett_rt_ss_restore(ptr)` | `llvm.stacksave`/`llvm.stackrestore`
DLT-17.3-P7
  <1>1 [req] Shadow-stack backing storage is physical storage, not a program allocation.
  <1>2 [req] Maintain logical frame base, extent and liveness, including dynamic allocations, extensions and saved restore points.
  <1>3 [req] A program access requires its entire nonwrapping byte range inside one live logical frame/allocation under §17.1; otherwise fault 7 before any access or mutation.   deps: DLT-17.1
  <1>4 [req] Containment in the backing ISA allocation is insufficient.
  <1>5 [req] Push registers a live frame; pop invalidates it and every younger frame; restore invalidates released ranges, truncating a surviving dynamic extent at the saved limit.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>6 [req] Separate dynamic allocations in a frame keep their individual extents; an access may not span distinct live frames/allocations or unused capacity.
  <1>7 [req] The address model permits a reused address once a new live logical allocation contains the complete range.
DLT-17.3-P8
  <1>1 [req] The default validation is the ordinary materialised runtime helper __bennett_rt_ss_check(p,n,table): reject wrapping ranges, visit logical entries in creation order, OR the live-and-contained predicates, and fault 7 if false.
  <1>2 [req] Its rows and metadata updates are charged in §16's reference object, and both builds implement the same checks and stack layout.   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), DLT-16
  <1>3 [req] Proven containment may eliminate the helper.
  <1>4 [req] Runtime bookkeeping, zero-fill and entry replay use an explicit internal path restricted to backing storage and metadata, never exposed to source program accesses; it is not a blanket exemption for ordinary calls into runtime-named functions.
  <1>5 [unc] Replay the logical metadata as well as backing bytes under §8.5.   deps: DLT-8.5
DLT-17.3-P9
  <1>1 [req] N24 rewrites each function with allocas: `%nz.fp.<f> = call ptr @__bennett_rt_ss_push(i64 S, i64 A)` at entry, where the static allocas of the entry block are laid out in instruction order, each at the next offset aligned to its alignment, S is the total rounded up to A, the largest alignment.   deps: DLT-12.2-P26.S1 (N24)
  <1>2 [req] The pushed frame base is aligned to A; its end (the next frame start before any additional alignment) is base + S.
  <1>3 [req] Each static alloca becomes `getelementptr i8, ptr %nz.fp.<f>, i64 <offset>` with the alloca's name; every other alloca becomes `__bennett_rt_ss_alloca`; `call void @__bennett_rt_ss_pop(ptr %nz.fp.<f>)` precedes every `ret` (including the early returns of §14.4).   deps: DLT-14.4
  <1>4 [req] `llvm.frameaddress(0)`, `llvm.sponentry`, `llvm.addressofreturnaddress`, `llvm.swift.async.context.addr` and `llvm.eh.dwarf.cfa` give `%nz.fp.<f>`; `llvm.frameaddress(n > 0)` and `llvm.returnaddress` give null (LangRef: "zero if it cannot be identified"); `llvm.stackaddress` gives the current shadow-stack pointer; `llvm.localescape` records the escaped allocas' offsets in a per-function table that `llvm.localrecover` reads with the parent's frame address; `llvm.structured.alloca` is an alloca.
````

### §17.4 CC and R lowering   [DLT-17.4]

````text
DLT-17.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] All pointer conversions, carriers, memory widths and address bridges use P(a) and the incoming target conversion exactly as in §16.4, in CC/R and the native build alike.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-16.4
  <1>2 [req] Every GEP, including constant expressions, uses §16.4's datalayout pointer-index width and preserves higher pointer bits.   deps: DLT-16.4
  <1>3 [unc] CC/R express its truncations, scaled modular offsets and high/low recombination with ordinary ISA integer operations.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>4 [req] This follows [LangRef GEP semantics](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#getelementptr-instruction) and [datalayout pointer indices](https://llvm.org/docs/LangRef.html#data-layout), not full-width pointer addition when I<P(a); differing widths do not reject a layout.
DLT-17.4-P2
  <1>1 [req] The lowering of device CC and R variants to ISA CC and R (ISA §4.1) is plugin-defined and constrained by §18.7, with these dialect rules: LLVM integer and memory instructions map to the ISA instructions of the same name (phis to block parameters, ISA §3.4); the primitives to `alloc`, `free` and §11.5; multi-result calls carry aggregates; marker calls lower to the region calls and `qreg.*` instructions of §18.3 with the handle register environment of §6.5; dispatch uses the compare trees of §14.2; host calls are §11.4; effect markers §11.1.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-10-P18.S1 (marker calls), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-4.5-P4.S1 (host call), ISA-4.1, DLT-18.7, ISA-3.4, DLT-11.5, DLT-18.3, DLT-6.5, DLT-14.2, DLT-11.4, DLT-11.1
  <1>2 [req] In R, every executed instruction is one frame of the R history (ISA §7.4).   deps: ISA-7.4
````

### §17.5 Fault outcomes   [DLT-17.5]

````text
DLT-17.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] `udiv`, `urem`, `sdiv`, `srem` with divisor 0 | 5 `FAULT_UB_DIV0`
DLT-17.5-P2
  <1>1 [req] `sdiv`, `srem` of the minimum value by −1 | 6 `FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW`
DLT-17.5-P3
  <1>1 [req] an access outside every live allocation; `free` of a non-null pointer that is not the base of a live heap allocation | 7 `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT`
DLT-17.5-P4
  <1>1 [req] `unreachable` reached; N11; an indirect call or `indirectbr` matching no candidate (§14.2, §14.9); unwinding out of a region (§14.4); a descriptor shape mismatch (`T-IFACE`); `T-TAINT-HOST`; every other run-time check of §18.3 | 4 `FAULT_UB_TRAP`   deps: DLT-12.2-P13.S1 (N11), DLT-14.2, DLT-14.9, DLT-14.4, DLT-18.3
DLT-17.5-P5
  <1>1 [req] `__bennett_nz_fault(C)` | C
DLT-17.5-P6
  <1>1 [req] steps budget exhausted | 1 `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS`
DLT-17.5-P7
  <1>1 [req] heap budget or shadow stack exhausted | 2 `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`
DLT-17.5-P8
  <1>1 [req] depth or lifted stack budget exhausted | 3 `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH`
DLT-17.5-P9
  <1>1 [req] a trapped call, an unmodelled or unbound device | 8 `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (trapped)
DLT-17.5-P10
  <1>1 [req] entropy exhausted (ISA §7.3; §11.3) | 10 `FAULT_ENTROPY_EXHAUSTED`   deps: ISA-7.3, DLT-11.3
DLT-17.5-P11
  <1>1 [req] a blocker point (§25.1) | 4, with the blocker report   deps: DLT-6.5-P10.S1 (blocker point), DLT-25.1
DLT-17.5-P12
  <1>1 [req] In an oracle region a fault is the F_B outcome (ISA §5.2).   deps: ISA-5.2
  <1>2 [req] In an action region it is TDL-8's.   deps: DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region)
  <1>3 [req] In other device code the run ends `FAILED` with that fault record (ISA §10); CC reports the code by §11.5; for R code the required typed record depends on BLK-5/ISA-7 (§25.1); ISA-8 (§25.2) separately governs status delivery and reverse eligibility.   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5), DLT-25.2-P2.S1 (ISA-7), DLT-25.2-P3.S1 (ISA-8), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), ISA-10, DLT-11.5, DLT-25.1, DLT-25.2
DLT-17.5-P13
  <1>1 [req] The R-entry classification of §11.5 applies to all §17.6 counters and §17.5 typed fault points; successful-path execution examples below retain their results.   deps: DLT-11.5, DLT-17.6, DLT-17.5
````

### §17.6 The R tier   [DLT-17.6]

````text
DLT-17.6-P1
  <1>1 [def] The R lowering inserts a **step counter**.
  <1>2 [req] Before each counted materialised LLVM instruction, check that one step remains, then consume it before execution; otherwise raise code 1 before executing the instruction.
  <1>3 [req] Count every instruction except phi, debug intrinsics and N34 marks, including straight-line suffixes and returns.   deps: DLT-12.2-P36.S1 (N34)
  <1>4 [req] Batching is permitted only if it has the identical first-fault boundary and effect-tape prefix as these per-instruction checks.
  <1>5 [req] Heap/depth checks likewise precede mutation.
DLT-17.6-P2
  <1>1 [req] Exhaustion is `__bennett_nz_fault(1)`, `(2)` or `(3)`, with the typed-fault contract of §17.5.   deps: DLT-17.5
  <1>2 [req] On ISA 0.8 R sites needing this code preservation are blocked:BD-B-ISA7 (§25.1), not claimed implemented by a generic trap.   deps: DLT-25.1
  <1>3 [unc] Affected fault points stop before later effects.
DLT-17.6-P3
  <1>1 [req] R faults end the run (ISA §4.1); §25.2 records ISA-8 (fault status to the CC caller, reverse-eligibility of faulted runs).   deps: DLT-25.2-P3.S1 (ISA-8), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), ISA-4.1, DLT-25.2
DLT-17.6-P4
  <1>1 [req] The zero-step example has the same named ISA dependency verdict as the explicit typed-fault example below: `blocked:BD-B-ISA7` (BLK-5).   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5)
  <1>2 [req] This is a verdict on ISA 0.8, not a claim that host execution fuel is the dialect's R step counter.   deps: DLT-17.6-P1.S1 (step counter)
  <1>3 [req] The materialised boundary after the failing pre-instruction check is:
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 10 lines) define internal void @budgeted(ptr %e) {
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) BD-B-ISA7 @budgeted pre-instruction step check: BLK-5 (ISA-7); ISA 0.8 cannot re…   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5), DLT-25.2-P2.S1 (ISA-7)
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 6 lines) source R body: console.write("x"); return; steps=0   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5)
DLT-17.6-P5
  <1>1 [req] The exact dependency is specified in the read-only ISA 0.9 draft at commit `61550b4`, section 0.2 row K5 and section 4.1 row `fault`: CC/R `fault C` takes a literal 0 ≤ C < 2^64, records the exact nonzero C (zero means code 4), ends FAILED without a successor, and preserves prefix history for reversal.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>2 [req] The implicit cause table remains part of BLK-5.   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5)
  <1>3 [req] The expected zero-step result above requires the dialect's per-instruction counter lowered to `fault 1`; ISA 0.9 does not turn the interpreter's host-fuel option into that counter.   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (lowered)
  <1>4 [req] Both this example and `r-fault-code` are runtime/materialised IR, not producer inputs.   deps: DLT-3.1-P7.S1 (producer input)
DLT-17.6-P6
  <1>1 [req] The next module is runtime/materialised IR (reserved primitives are not producer inputs).   deps: DLT-3.1-P7.S1 (producer input)
  <1>2 [ex] (code block, 10 lines) define internal void @fail(ptr %e) {
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) BD-B-ISA7 @fail call @__bennett_nz_fault(2): ISA 0.8 generic R trap cannot prese…   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5), DLT-25.2-P2.S1 (ISA-7)
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 5 lines) required code=2; no subsequent source effect; prefix history intact
````

### §17.7 The native comparison build   [DLT-17.7]

````text
DLT-17.7-P1
  <1>1 [def] Differential testing (NS §5.1) compares the artefact with the **native comparison binary**: the materialised device variants, code-generated by the same LLVM major and linked with `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` (never host libc or libm) under the comparison link of §19.5.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (comparison link), DLT-19.5
  <1>2 [req] It is never an `-O0` build: optimisation legally changes marker operands (spike F4), and the materialised optimised module is the one program both builds implement.
  <1>3 [req] The driver MUST make division by zero and INT_MIN/−1 trap in the native build: on targets whose divide instruction does not trap it inserts, after materialisation and outside the materialised module, a check that calls `__bennett_nz_fault` with code 5 or 6.   deps: DLT-12.1-P1.S6 (materialised module)
  <1>4 [unc] Results compare by the outcome table of §18.7.   deps: DLT-18.7
````

### §18.1 State   [DLT-18.1]

````text
DLT-18.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] The reference semantics runs H functions natively and every device entry, region invocation and whole-program entry on the materialised module over a state Σ = (C, Q, H, G, K, T):   deps: DLT-12.1-P1.S6 (materialised module)
DLT-18.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] **C**, the classical state: LLVM's semantics of the materialised module (LangRef), with every remaining choice fixed by §12–§14 and the fault outcomes of §17.5; it includes the shadows of the arena (TDL-4).   deps: DLT-10-P19.S1 (TDL-4), DLT-12.1-P1.S6 (materialised module), DLT-12, DLT-13, DLT-14, DLT-17.5
  <1>2 [req] In particular GEP uses the pointer-index width, as fixed in §16.4 and §17.4, also in constant expressions; pointer representations and conversions use P(a), not an implicit pw-bit move;   deps: DLT-16.4, DLT-17.4
DLT-18.1-P3
  <1>1 [req] **Q**, the quantum state of the ISA formal model (ISA §14.2): a unit vector over the live wires in allocation order, including the arena's wires (TDL-4);   deps: DLT-10-P19.S1 (TDL-4), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-14.2
DLT-18.1-P4
  <1>1 [req] **H**, the handle table: for each handle, its wires, whether it is live, and the generation it was allocated in, if any;   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
DLT-18.1-P5
  <1>1 [unc] **G**, a stack of **recorders**, one per active generation (§18.4);   deps: DLT-18.4
DLT-18.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] **K**, the coherence states of the object instances (TDL-2);   deps: DLT-10-P3.S1 (TDL-2)
DLT-18.1-P7
  <1>1 [req] **T**, the device-model states and the effect tape (§11).   deps: DLT-11
DLT-18.1-P8
  <1>1 [req] Measurement and `observe` make the semantics a tree: every outcome sequence has its Born probability (ISA §14.2) and its own continuation.   deps: ISA-14.2
````

### §18.2 Events   [DLT-18.2]

````text
DLT-18.2-P1
  <1>1 [def] A marker call or a TDL store produces **events**: ISA-level operations, each with a **control set** (a set of wires, initially empty).   deps: DLT-3.5-P1.S1 (marker call), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>2 [req] The forms are:
DLT-18.2-P2
  <1>1 [unc] `qalloc H n`, `qfree H`; the gates `x h s sdg t tdg cx cz swap ccx` (ISA §4.4) and `rx θ`, `ry θ`, `rz θ` on wires; `qload D M A`, `qstore M A V`, `qprep H bytes`;   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-4.4
DLT-18.2-P3
  <1>1 [unc] `oracle R B (ins..) tgt fault (mem..) (args..)` and `wide R …` (§8.6), `inplace G G′ B X`;   deps: DLT-8.6
DLT-18.2-P4
  <1>1 [unc] the TDL events of §10: `slice O (args..) (ins..) (mem..) -> regs`, `xmask X+o m`, `xstore X+a m`, `perm P X+a (params..)`, `xchg X+a h`, `fault C` (TDL-12), `firstfault (f..) Φ`;   deps: DLT-10-P47.S1 (TDL-12), DLT-10
DLT-18.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] `qcopy S -> D`: allocate D clean then XOR S into it; `quncopy S D`: XOR S into D and release clean D; `qxor S T`: T XOR= S; `copy F -> G` means qalloc G then qxor F G (TDL-8);   deps: DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8)
DLT-18.2-P6
  <1>1 [unc] `observe X+o len`, `measure` (outside generations), `effect dev.op` (R).
DLT-18.2-P7
  <1>1 [req] When no recorder is active, an event is **applied** to Σ at once; otherwise it is appended to the innermost recorder.
  <1>2 [req] Applying an event with control set S applies it only on the basis states where every wire of S is 1 (ISA `ctl`, ISA §4.7, §5.3); `qalloc` and `qfree` are unconditional (ISA §3.5).   deps: DLT-18.2-P1.S1 (control set), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-4.7, ISA-5.3, ISA-3.5
  <1>3 [req] Arena writes with nonempty control set, Co address or Co value first materialise Cl objects and make Cl or M objects Co.   deps: DLT-2-P13.S3 (Arena), DLT-18.2-P1.S1 (control set)
  <1>4 [req] Otherwise apply events per form: `xmask` XORs its mask into shadow only for Cl (arena stays zero), both copies for M, arena only for Co; `xstore` does the same XOR at its selected address; `perm` applies its bijection to shadow only for Cl, both copies for M, arena only for Co.
  <1>5 [req] `xchg` with a Co register first materialises and makes X Co; with a classical basis operand it exchanges with shadow for Cl, both copies consistently for M (returning one old value), arena only for Co.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>6 [req] Unconditional Cl-valued xmask/xstore/perm leave K unchanged.
  <1>7 [req] Event application does not also reapply its speculative generation-view update.
  <1>8 [req] Event-application writes invalidate observation coverage; permitted TDL-11 CC stores retain it as covering writes.   deps: DLT-10-P43.S1 (TDL-11), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
  <1>9 [req] The plugin realises applied events as CC-level region calls in event order (ISA §4.7), whose temporaries are released by `qreg.free` with the dynamic cleanliness check of ISA §3.3; it MAY compile an event list into one ISA action when that action satisfies ISA §6.   deps: ISA-4.7, ISA-3.3, ISA-6
DLT-18.2-P8
  <1>1 [req] **Adjoint** of an event list (ISA §5.3 adjoint table): reverse the list and replace each event: `x h cx cz swap ccx qload qstore qprep oracle wide slice xmask xstore xchg` are self-adjoint; `s` ↔ `sdg`; `t` ↔ `tdg`; `rx θ` ↦ `rx −θ` (and `ry`, `rz`); `qalloc` ↔ `qfree` of the same wires; `inplace G G′ B X` ↦ `inplace G′ G B X`; `perm P` ↦ the library inverse of P.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-5.3
  <1>2 [req] Control sets are unchanged.
  <1>3 [req] Ordinary workspace qcopy and quncopy are mutual adjoints.
  <1>4 [req] A deferred-measurement qcopy is a channel record and is never adjointed to quncopy.
  <1>5 [req] In standalone adj(f), keep non-record events in reverse-list order.
  <1>6 [req] Insert each fresh retained record immediately before its earliest reversed consumer, after every reversed event preceding that consumer.
  <1>7 [req] A record without consumers stays at its reversed source position.
  <1>8 [req] Records in the same insertion gap follow original source order, with record producers before dependent records.
  <1>9 [req] Thus record placement is deterministic, including when noncommuting events change its source.
  <1>10 [req] For local sources, hoist required unconditional inverse allocations before their copies and retain the sources through their last use; this lifetime extension does not commute a copy across an arbitrary quantum operation.
  <1>11 [req] Optimisations MUST preserve this complete instrument and the §7.5(a) measurement boundary.   deps: DLT-7.5-P2
  <1>12 [req] In compute(f,use), E_f's records survive E_u and adjoint(E_f), which reuses these wires without copying or uncopying them, and measurement is at the first §7.5(a) boundary after compute returns.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-7.5-P2
DLT-18.2-P9
  <1>1 [req] A Co-loop adjoint is the inverse of the complete clean-workspace transition, not forward evaluation of the guard on the adjoint-entry mutable data.
  <1>2 [req] For conditions on preserved inputs, compute flags forward, apply body adjoints for i=N..1, then uncompute the flags with their exact computing slices.
  <1>3 [req] For overwritten inputs in a standalone adj, use the bounded inverse required by TDL-5(b) to reconstruct the unique forward entry on copies, compute its trajectory, apply the inverse iterations, and clean that reconstruction against the restored entry.   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
  <1>4 [req] No assumed earlier invocation supplies this entry.
  <1>5 [req] When no unique inverse exists on the declared interface, the existing TDL-5(d) applies; changing adj into an entry-guard instrument is not an alternative inverse.   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
  <1>6 [req] Out-of-place evaluation and compute retain the computation under TDL-5(c). compute's adjoint(E_f) reuses the actual forward flags/history and cleans them as each iteration state is restored, without a fresh trajectory.   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
  <1>7 [req] Only genuine measurement records and actual fault outputs have their separate retained lifetimes.
DLT-18.2-P10
  <1>1 [req] qxor and firstfault are self-adjoint XORs.
  <1>2 [req] `fault C` is an xmask of C on a fresh declared fault register, also self-adjoint.   deps: DLT-10-P36.S3 (declared fault register)
  <1>3 [req] Adjoint of `copy F -> G` is qxor F G then qfree G, not another allocation.
````

### §18.3 Marker rules   [DLT-18.3]

````text
DLT-18.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] Wire `h.i` is wire i of handle h; n(h) its width.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>2 [req] A wire is **named** by an event when it is one of the event's operand wires.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
  <1>3 [req] The conditions are checked when the marker is called, against H, including generation-specific identity and liveness (not physical-slot liveness alone), and H is updated then; Q changes only when an event is applied.
  <1>4 [req] A failed condition is a run-time trap: fault code 4 (§17.5) with the named diagnostic (§22.2).   deps: DLT-17.5, DLT-22.2
DLT-18.3-P2
  <1>1 [req] `qalloc(cls, n)` | n ≥ 1 (`T-SIZE`); cls in the class table (`T-CLASS`) | a new live handle h of n wires; event `qalloc h n`, whose application appends the n wires to Q in state 0; h belongs to the innermost active generation, if any   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
DLT-18.3-P3
  <1>1 [req] `qborrow(cls, n)` | as `qalloc` | inside a generation compiled as an ISA action, ISA `qborrow`/`qreturn` with borrow-frame verification (ISA §5.6); otherwise as `qalloc` (a correct user of borrowed wires also works on clean wires; ISA-9, §25.3).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), ISA-5.6, DLT-25.3
  <1>2 [req] 0.1's `T-BORROW` is deleted
DLT-18.3-P4
  <1>1 [req] `qfree(h)` | h live (`T-DEAD`); if a recorder is active, h belongs to it (`T-FOREIGN`); when applied, Q is the product of the all-zero state on h's wires and a state of the other wires (`T-CLEAN`) | event `qfree h`   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
DLT-18.3-P5
  <1>1 [req] `gate(op, h, i)` | h live (`T-DEAD`); i < n(h) (`T-INDEX`); op in the table (`T-OP`) | the realisation of §3.4 on `h.i`   deps: DLT-3.4
DLT-18.3-P6
  <1>1 [req] `gate2(op, a, i, b, j)` | as above for both; `a.i` ≠ `b.j` (`T-ALIAS`) | `cx a.i b.j`, `cz a.i b.j` or `swap a.i b.j`
DLT-18.3-P7
  <1>1 [req] `gate3(op, a, i, b, j, c, k)` | as above; the three wires distinct (`T-ALIAS`) | `ccx a.i b.j c.k`   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire)
DLT-18.3-P8
  <1>1 [req] `rot(op, h, i, θ)` | as `gate`; θ finite (`T-THETA`) | `rx θ`, `ry θ` or `rz θ` on `h.i`
DLT-18.3-P9
  <1>1 [req] `measure(h, i)` | as `gate`; no recorder active unless deferred (§7.5a) (`T-MEASURE`) | projective measurement of `h.i` (ISA §14.2); outcome m with its Born probability; the wire stays live in state m (ISA: copy h.i onto a fresh one-wire non-view register and measure that copy; h is never whole-register measured, ISA-6 not a gap); returns m   deps: DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-7.5-P2, ISA-14.2
DLT-18.3-P10
  <1>1 [req] `qload(dst, mem, addr)` | the three handles live and distinct (`T-DEAD`, `T-ALIAS`); n(mem) = 2^n(addr) · n(dst) (`T-SIZE`) | event `qload`: for every cell c, on the basis states where addr holds c, `dst.b ⊕= mem.(c·W + b)` for b < W = n(dst) (ISA §4.6 cell order)   deps: ISA-4.6
DLT-18.3-P11

DLT-18.3-P12

DLT-18.3-P13
````

### §18.4 Generation of action regions   [DLT-18.4]

````text
DLT-18.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] `gen(R, env)` pushes a recorder, runs `R(env)`'s CC variant under §18.1 with the TDL partition of §10 (its G instructions run once on the generation's view; its slices, stores to outliving objects and marker calls produce events per §10 and §18.3, nested `ctrl`, `adj` and `compute` producing their events into this recorder), pops the recorder and returns its event list E.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-10-P18.S1 (marker calls), DLT-18.1, DLT-10, DLT-18.3
  <1>2 [req] On generation exhaustion, finalise by cancellation exactly as TDL-9 specifies, outside the exhausted budget; no unmatched provisional allocation is applied, no foreign register changes and a failed compute does not apply either part.   deps: DLT-10-P37.S1 (TDL-9), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>3 [req] **Leak rule** (successful source generation only, excluding the declared fault/measurement outputs): (`T-LEAK`): for `ctrl` and `adj`, every register allocated during the generation is freed during it; for `compute`, every register allocated during gen(use) is freed during it, while a register allocated during gen(f) and not freed during it is freed by adjoint(E_f), in which its `qalloc` event becomes a `qfree` event, so gen(use) MUST NOT free it (`T-FOREIGN`).   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>4 [req] Outside generations the check is at program end (§6.6): a register that is still live then, neither freed by device code or the host (`bennett_qreg_free`) nor consumed by a `measure`, is `T-LEAK`. *(non-normative)* The classical part of an action region (G) runs once per invocation; its effect on outliving memory is the events of TDL-5, so under `ctrl` it happens exactly on the branch where the control is 1 (the audit's counterexample to 0.1).   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-7.1-P7.S3 (action region), DLT-6.6
  <1>5 [req] NS §4.2 requires "allocations inside released clean or borrowed and restored": `T-LEAK` and `T-CLEAN` are those checks.
````

### §18.5 Oracle semantics   [DLT-18.5]

````text
DLT-18.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] An `oracle` event applies, on every basis state of its operands, the permutation |x⟩|t⟩|e⟩ ↦ |x⟩|t ⊕ payload(F_B(x, S))⟩|e ⊕ code(F_B(x, S))⟩ where F_B is the reference interpreter's result on the reference object of the dispatched shape (§16) with the token's budget, and S is the entry state of §8.5 (the caller's classical memory at the call).   deps: DLT-16-P1.S1 (reference object), DLT-16, DLT-8.5
  <1>2 [req] It is an involution (ISA §5.2).   deps: ISA-5.2
````

### §18.6 Reversible semantics   [DLT-18.6]

````text
DLT-18.6-P1
  <1>1 [req] `reversible(f, budget, env)` and the whole-program R mode (§19.2) run f on the R tier (ISA §3.1): its classical effects on memory are those of §18.1, its effects are ISA device models on the tape (§11.2), its region calls are R-tier region calls (§7.5b), and the run history (ISA §7.4) is retained, so the whole run is reversible by `bennett_reverse` (ISA §10).   deps: DLT-19.2, ISA-3.1, DLT-18.1, DLT-11.2, DLT-7.5-P3, ISA-7.4, ISA-10
  <1>2 [req] A fault ends the run (§17.6).   deps: DLT-17.6
````

### §18.7 Conformance   [DLT-18.7]

````text
DLT-18.7-P1
  <1>1 [req] A plugin and an executor (ISA §1.2) together conform iff, for every module the rules accept or block, every host input and every sequence of measurement and `observe` outcomes, outside the executions that reach a blocker point: the probability of that sequence, every classical value the host observes (return values of device entries, memory they write, console output), every fault code and every trap equal those of the reference semantics; and the quantum state at every measurement and at every device-entry return equals the reference state, exactly when the program uses permutation gates only, and otherwise within the error bound its certificate states (ISA §12.3).   deps: DLT-6.5-P10.S1 (blocker point), ISA-1.2, ISA-12.3
  <1>2 [req] A program that reaches `T-CLEAN` in the reference semantics is erroneous; an executor that cannot test cleanliness (hardware) need not reproduce that trap (ISA §17 item 14).   deps: ISA-17-P15
DLT-18.7-P2
  <1>1 [def] **Outcome table** (NS §5.1: "one outcome table governs §3.2, §5.1 and §8"). For every input of a declared domain the comparison with the native comparison build (§17.7) has one of four outcomes:   deps: DLT-3.2, DLT-5.1, DLT-8, DLT-17.7
DLT-18.7-P3
  <1>1 [req] native-comparable success | the reference completes without fault and no layout-dependent value influences the result | the artefact's results equal native
DLT-18.7-P4
  <1>1 [req] reference fault | the reference faults (code ≠ 0) | the artefact reports the same code; native supplies no cause (it may trap, silently read adjacent memory or hang)
DLT-18.7-P5
  <1>1 [req] layout-dependent | the reference interpreter's address provenance shows a host-stack address, or a `blockaddress` value (§14.9), influencing the result | validated against the reference interpreter instead of native, counted separately   deps: DLT-14.9
DLT-18.7-P6
  <1>1 [req] classifier-flagged undefined | an instrumented build (UBSan, ASan, MSan) flags the input (NS §3.2) | excluded from comparison, counted separately
````

````text
DLT-18.8-P13
  <1>1 [non] *(non-normative)* The states are those of 0.1 §8.8.
  <1>2 [non] Control: after h, one controlled flip gives (|00⟩ + |11⟩)/√2 and the second cancels it.
  <1>3 [non] Adjoint: h|0⟩ = (|0⟩ + |1⟩)/√2, s multiplies the |1⟩ amplitude by i and t by e^{iπ/4}, so before the adjoint it is i·e^{iπ/4}/√2 = e^{i3π/4}/√2 = (−1 + i)/2.
  <1>4 [non] Compute: h, cx give (|00⟩ + |11⟩)/√2; s s = Z on the ancilla gives (|00⟩ − |11⟩)/√2; the uncomputing cx gives (|0⟩ − |1⟩)/√2 ⊗ |0⟩, and h maps it to |10⟩; x returns a to |0⟩ before release.
DLT-18.8-P14
  <1>1 [ex] **TDL.** The following programs exercise every case of TDL-5, TDL-10 and TDL-11 on the globals `@cnt` and `@other`, which are in the may-Co set (TDL-2) and hence in the arena.   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5), DLT-10-P38.S1 (TDL-10), DLT-10-P43.S1 (TDL-11), DLT-10-P3.S1 (TDL-2)

  <1>4 [ex] `text state=` blocks list the handles in allocation order and then the arena bytes of `@cnt` (and `@other`) in hex, as `|q…; cnt other>`.
  <1>5 [ex] Temporary and retained registers (TDL-7, TDL-8) hold 0 in these fault-free runs and are omitted.   deps: DLT-10-P35.S1 (TDL-7), DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)

  <1>2 [non] Under 0.1's semantics the store would have happened on both branches and `@tdl_a` would return 5 with certainty.

  <1>1 [non] *(non-normative)* Before event 10 the copy-out pair is complete: `@other` = cnt + 1 exactly where H1 is 1: 0 + 1 = 1 and 5 + 1 = 6.
  <1>2 [non] The permutation "add 3" under H1 then gives cnt = 5·H0 + 3·H1: 0, 3, 5, 8.
````

### §19.1 Pipeline position and bitcode sources   [DLT-19.1]

````text
DLT-19.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] Front ends emit bitcode with their stock pre-link pipelines; no Bennett code runs before the final link (Enzyme PR #3211: "forgetting it is a loud failure"), except in the explicit closed-unit mode (§19.7).   deps: DLT-19.7
  <1>2 [req] The driver links all bitcode inputs (§19.8), links `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` bitcode of the link's major M, runs the post-link pipeline (`lto<O3>` by default, or any envelope pipeline of §21.1 the user selects), then runs the plugin on the whole program (NS §3.3).   deps: DLT-19.8, DLT-21.1
  <1>3 [unc] With `opt`:
  <1>4 [ex] (code block, 3 lines) opt -load-pass-plugin=libbennett-plugin-<M>.so -passes='lto<O3>,bennett' -bennet…
DLT-19.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] The plugin runs on one merged module (full LTO; §24 D23).   deps: DLT-24-P7.S1 (D23), DLT-24
  <1>2 [req] It registers one module pass, `bennett`, and MAY register a full-LTO last extension point for use from a linker plugin; it MUST do nothing in a pre-link phase.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register)
  <1>3 [def] **Bitcode sources** (NS §3.3): Rust `std` from the bitcode embedded in rlibs (`.llvmbc`) where present and readable by §19.8, else `-Zbuild-std`; the Julia runtime by the runtimes' shim bitcode; every remaining call without bitcode is a `trapped` coverage line (§4.5).   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (trapped), DLT-19.8, DLT-4.5
````

### §19.2 Mode `--bennett-tier=reversible --bennett-entry=<sym>`   [DLT-19.2]

````text
DLT-19.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] The whole program is the device program in tier R (NS §4.2: "entered through `__bennett_reversible` or a driver mode"; rungs 3, 5, 6):
DLT-19.2-P2
  <1>1 [def] a CC **launch stub** fetches the argv and envp bytes with `hostcall bennett.argv`, initialises the shadow stack (§17.3), calls the R entry `<sym>` with (argc, argv, envp), catches the exit pseudo-exception (§14.7) and returns the status;   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-17.3, DLT-14.7
DLT-19.2-P3
  <1>1 [req] effects are the ISA device models (§11.2): console, clock, entropy (the launch's entropy tape), block with `block_image` from `--bennett-disk=<file>`;   deps: DLT-11.2
DLT-19.2-P4
  <1>1 [req] un-run is `bennett_reverse` (ISA §10): whole-run reversal restores the entry state; tape replay is deterministic from the tape and the entropy;   deps: ISA-10
DLT-19.2-P5
  <1>1 [req] a `measure` reachable in the program is `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE` (§7.5b).   deps: DLT-22.1-P16.S1 (BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE), DLT-7.5-P3
````

### §19.3 Mode `--bennett-oracle=<sym>` ("oracle from program")   [DLT-19.3]

````text
DLT-19.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] A convenience: the driver synthesises the translation unit a quantum programmer would write (§16.7): a CC function that builds a descriptor binding `<sym>`'s parameters by `--bennett-bind=<param>:<kind>:<width>[:<value>]` (kind `in`, `arg`, `mem_in`, `out`), device bindings by `--bennett-dev=<model>:<width>[:<value>]`, and a payload: the return value, the exit status caught by the wrapper (§14.7), or the first L bytes of console output (`--bennett-payload=return|exit|console:<L>`), and applies the oracle once to registers it allocates, as a device entry the host calls with those registers.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-4.5-P4.S1 (host call), DLT-16.7, DLT-14.7
  <1>2 [req] The normative route is the separate translation unit with marker calls naming the unmodified function (rungs 4, 7, 8).   deps: DLT-10-P18.S1 (marker calls)
````

### §19.4 Placement: host vs device CC   [DLT-19.4]

````text
DLT-19.4-P1
  <1>1 [def] H functions stay native unless they share **pointer-rich** memory with device code: a pointer argument of a device-entry call, or a global accessed by both, whose pointee may contain pointers that device code reads (§5).   deps: DLT-5
  <1>2 [req] Such H functions are **promoted** to CC variants, transitively up to their roots (in the limit `main`, and then H is the system-call proxy only: `hostcall bennett.syscall`).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
  <1>3 [req] Results are equal by the materialised semantics; ISA-11 would let them stay native (an efficiency and placement item, §25.3; outline E11).   deps: DLT-25.3
````

### §19.5 Layout: the comparison link   [DLT-19.5]

````text
DLT-19.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] The driver links the materialised module natively (§17.7) with a linker script that fixes the addresses: non-PIE; identical-code folding and constant merging off; the null region [0, `null_size`) with `null_size` = 4096; the data sections (`.rodata`, `.data`, `.bss`, in that order) from `data_base` = 4096; the text from `text_base` = 0x400000, or, when the device ranges below (data, heap, arena, interface regions, computed first) end above 0x400000, from the first multiple of 0x200000 at or after their end, so that the native comparison binary maps its code disjointly from them; the defaults are options (`--bennett-layout-data-base`, `--bennett-layout-text-base`, §24).   deps: DLT-12.1-P1.S6 (materialised module), DLT-17.7-P1.S1 (native comparison binary), DLT-17.7, DLT-24
  <1>2 [req] The link map gives A(F) for every function and the base of every global.
  <1>3 [unc] The device `layout` (ISA §9.1) replicates it:   deps: ISA-9.1
DLT-19.5-P2
  <1>1 [unc] `globals`: every global of the link at its link address with its image;
DLT-19.5-P3
  <1>1 [req] `heap_base`: the first multiple of 4096 at or after the end of the data; `heap_limit_max`: the largest `heap` of the budget table (which covers the shadow stack, §9.2);   deps: DLT-9.2-P2.S1 (budget table), DLT-9.2
DLT-19.5-P4
  <1>1 [req] the arena (TDL-4) at the first multiple of its size 2^A at or after the heap's end, with 2^A the smallest power of two covering the may-Co globals and the coherent allocator's heap (its size from `--bennett-arena-heap`, default the largest `heap` of the table); the arena's shadow `__bennett_rt_shadow` is a global of the data sections;   deps: DLT-10-P19.S1 (TDL-4), DLT-10-P20.S2 (coherent allocator)
DLT-19.5-P5
  <1>1 [unc] interface regions per oracle above the arena (§16.3); H, A_tab and S_cells by §16.3;   deps: DLT-16.3
DLT-19.5-P6
  <1>1 [req] the production host image is linked with the same addresses for every function and global whose address may cross the boundary (§4.4).   deps: DLT-4.4
DLT-19.5-P7
  <1>1 [non] *(non-normative)* With data low and text high, H covers the data, heap and regions; function addresses are values only and never occupy device memory.
````

### §19.6 Build-time snapshots   [DLT-19.6]

````text
DLT-19.6-P1
  <1>1 [req] `--bennett-snapshot=<dump>` rewrites the initialisers of the mutable globals listed in the dump (and the platform RAM global, §20.1) to the dump's bytes before the plugin runs; the data images then carry the snapshot and R of §8.5 is empty for them (rung 7: "kernel state snapshot fixed classically after boot").   deps: DLT-20.1, DLT-8.5
  <1>2 [req] The dump is recorded by hash in the build record.   deps: DLT-19.9-P16.S1 (build record)
````

### §19.7 Closed-unit mode   [DLT-19.7]

````text
DLT-19.7-P1
  <1>1 [def] `-bennett-closed-unit` (the REPL fast path, NS §3.3: "a per-TU invocation remains available as a fast path for programs closed within one unit, with the same semantics"): the plugin runs on one module plus the runtimes and arith bitcode, after a **closedness check**: every device function's callees are defined in that set or are trapped exactly as in whole-program mode; no device function's address escapes the module; no function pointer enters through a device-entry parameter.   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (trapped)
  <1>2 [req] If the check passes, processing is identical to whole-program mode.
  <1>3 [req] If it fails, the driver falls back to whole-program mode (it does not reject).
  <1>4 [req] Objects are content-addressed (ISA §9.2) and linked at launch (ISA §9.3), so the REPL is incremental.   deps: ISA-9.2, ISA-9.3
````

### §19.8 Mixed LLVM majors   [DLT-19.8]

````text
DLT-19.8-P1
  <1>1 [req] The driver reads every bitcode input with the newest major M of the envelope (bitcode is readable by newer majors), links with M and runs plugin M; runtimes and arith ship bitcode for each M.
  <1>2 [req] Every input MUST have one target datalayout and triple (NS §3.2) (`BD-E-VERSION` otherwise: a tool-version mismatch, not a program property).   deps: DLT-22.1-P2.S1 (BD-E-VERSION)
  <1>3 [req] An input newer than M is `BD-E-VERSION`.   deps: DLT-22.1-P2.S1 (BD-E-VERSION)
  <1>4 [req] The tested (front end, major) pairs are published in the envelope; a corpus case links clang 18 bitcode with Julia's and rustc's majors (§21.2; bd `.16`).   deps: DLT-21.2
````

### §20 Platform profile `riscv64-bennett-virt` (platform 0.2.1; rungs 6–7)   [DLT-20]

````text
DLT-20-P1
  <1>1 [req] NS §5: "The kernel compiles, boots on the VM in the reversible tier with its devices modelled, writes a serial console to the tape, and un-boots to the reset vector."
  <1>2 [req] This section fixes the mechanisms (outline E4: riscv64 first); its row lists come from the platform spike of §20.7, a mechanics deferral (§24): the capability is not in question (audit O-06).   deps: DLT-20.7, DLT-24
````

### §20.1 Model   [DLT-20.1]

````text
DLT-20.1-P1
  <1>1 [unc] A virtual board, selected by `--bennett-platform=riscv64-bennett-virt`:
DLT-20.1-P2
  <1>1 [req] RAM: one runtimes global `__bennett_rt_platform_ram` of the configured size (`--bennett-platform-ram`, default 128 MiB), which the profile's linker script places at the board's physical base 0x80000000 after the other data (the heap and the text follow it by §19.5), so kernel physical addresses are ordinary device addresses;   deps: DLT-19.5
DLT-20.1-P3
  <1>1 [unc] the CSR file (privilege mode, `satp`, `stvec`, `mtvec`, `sstatus`, `mstatus`, `sie`, `sip`, `scause`, `sepc`, `stval`, `sscratch`, `mscratch`, `time`, …) as the runtimes global `__bennett_rt_csr`;
DLT-20.1-P4
  <1>1 [unc] devices: a UART 16550 (console), a CLINT and timer (clock), a PLIC, and a virtio-blk MMIO device (block), each a runtimes C model over the effect markers.
````

### §20.2 asm rows   [DLT-20.2]

````text
DLT-20.2-P1
  <1>1 [req] The privileged forms are `shimmed` rows of §15: `csrr`, `csrw`, `csrs`, `csrc` (and the immediate forms) → `__bennett_rt_csr_read(i32 csr)`, `_write(csr, v)`, `_set(csr, v)`, `_clear(csr, v)`; `sfence.vma` → `__bennett_rt_sfence_vma(va, asid)`; `wfi` → `__bennett_rt_wfi()` (advances the clock model to the next timer event); `ecall` in kernel mode → `__bennett_rt_sbi_call(…)` (the SBI of the board), in user mode → the trap path of §20.4; `mret`, `sret` → `__bennett_rt_trap_return (mode)`; `fence.i` and `fence` → deleted (lowered).   deps: DLT-2-P15.S1 (shimmed), DLT-15, DLT-20.4
  <1>2 [req] The exact asm strings and constraint strings are those the §20.7 spike inventories.   deps: DLT-20.7
````

### §20.3 MMIO routing (N35)   [DLT-20.3]

````text
DLT-20.3-P1
  <1>1 [req] Under the platform profile, N9 first routes every volatile load and store, and every MMIO asm form, to `__bennett_rt_mmio_{load,store}_i<N>(addr[, v])`; these dispatch by address range to the device models' handlers (C code in runtimes that calls the effect markers), and otherwise access memory.   deps: DLT-12.2-P11.S1 (N9)
  <1>2 [req] A non-volatile access to an MMIO window lies outside every live allocation and faults with code 7, as the reference semantics defines (§17.1).   deps: DLT-17.1
````

### §20.4 Interrupts (N35)   [DLT-20.4]

````text
DLT-20.4-P1
  <1>1 [req] Poll points at loop back edges and function entries (N35) call `__bennett_rt_poll()`, which checks the timer model (deterministic, driven by the clock model) and the PLIC's pending bits; on delivery it sets the CSRs (`scause`, `sepc`, `stval`, `sstatus`) and calls the trap vector `stvec` by A(F) dispatch (§14.2).   deps: DLT-12.2-P37.S1 (N35), DLT-14.2
  <1>2 [req] Interrupts are therefore taken at poll points only, a deterministic choice recorded in the certificate; NS §3.2's single-thread assumption covers the uniprocessor kernel (`CONFIG_SMP=n`, xv6 `CPUS=1`).
````

### §20.5 Context switch and traps   [DLT-20.5]

````text
DLT-20.5-P1
  <1>1 [req] The assembly entry points (`swtch`, `trampoline`, `uservec`, `userret`, `kernelvec`, Linux `__switch_to`, `handle_exception`, `ret_from_exception`) are platform shims by symbol (§15.3), written in C over the fibers of §14.6: a kernel context switch is a fiber switch; a trap entry saves the user registers into the trap frame from the platform register file and calls the kernel's handler; a trap return restores them and resumes the user fiber.   deps: DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-15.3, DLT-14.6
````

### §20.6 User processes   [DLT-20.6]

````text
DLT-20.6-P1
  <1>1 [unc] N35 translates **all guest memory accesses** in translation-enabled effective privilege contexts through the modelled satp and Sv39 page tables/permissions, including supervisor kernel accesses and user accesses (and effective privilege selected by MPRV for data).   deps: DLT-12.2-P37.S1 (N35)
  <1>2 [req] Bare/physical accesses use physical addresses.
  <1>3 [req] Runtime model helpers and page-table walks access physical backing storage directly, and are excluded from reinstrumentation.
  <1>4 [req] LLVM guest pointers remain virtual values through GEP, ptrtoint and comparisons; translate only on memory access.
  <1>5 [req] The layout records virtual data/code addresses and their physical backing.
  <1>6 [req] A physical RAM address and every permitted virtual alias name the same backing byte.
  <1>7 [req] Indirect dispatch and trap resumption preserve this mapping: A(F), stvec and sepc retain guest virtual code addresses, resolved through the code-address map after Sv39 validation.
  <1>8 [req] Translation failures deliver the modelled guest page/access trap before memory mutation; they are not accidental accesses to an unallocated high host/device address.
  <1>9 [unc] Runtime helpers avoid a second translation.
  <1>10 [req] These rules apply equally to CC/R and L variants.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
DLT-20.6-P2
  <1>1 [req] User programs are built to bitcode and linked into the device program under the comparison layout (§19.5); their ELF images on the disk image are the same link, so entry and function addresses are A(F).   deps: DLT-4.4-P1.S1 (function address), DLT-19.5
  <1>2 [def] `exec` and `sret` to user mode dispatch the user entry by A(F); user loads and stores go through a **soft-MMU**: N35 instruments all guest functions in translation-enabled privilege contexts with an Sv39 walk over the modelled `satp`, including supervisor kernel accesses, with guest page faults delivered through §20.4.   deps: DLT-12.2-P37.S1 (N35), DLT-20.4
  <1>3 [ex] (code block, 6 lines) RAM physical P=0x80000000 holds 0x12
````

### §20.7 Platform spike (mechanics)   [DLT-20.7]

````text
DLT-20.7-P1

DLT-20.7-P2

DLT-20.7-P3

  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 6 lines) define i8 @program(i8 %x) {
  <1>6 [ex] (code block, 6 lines) driver: --bennett-oracle=program --bennett-bind=0:in:8 --bennett-payload=return

  <1>2 [req] Device code follows head.next and reads 9.

  <1>4 [req] Keeping a native host-only tail store without coherent state would instead leave the image value 1.

DLT-20.7-P5

  <1>2 [req] With heap_limit_max=4194304 the heap ends at 4202496, above 0x400000.
  <1>3 [req] With no arena or interfaces, text begins at the next multiple of 0x200000: 6291456 (0x600000).
  <1>4 [req] The initial pointer slot contains 4096 in little-endian; native and device layouts must agree.   deps: DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot)
  <1>5 [ex] (code block, 9 lines) @bytes = internal global [3 x i8] [i8 7, i8 8, i8 9], align 1

DLT-20.7-P6
````

### §21.1 The corpus   [DLT-21.1]

````text
DLT-21.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] `corpus/legality/` holds the cases (`cases/`: the 56 spike cases plus the `spec-*` cases of this document), four negative controls (`controls/`), the harness (`harness/run.py`), the trace runtime (`runtime/`) and the spike's results.
  <1>2 [req] Spike cases use the provisional ABI v0 and arbitrary operation codes: they are **legality** cases (what LLVM passes do to marker programs), not well-formedness cases; the legality property (§3.6) does not depend on a marker's signature.   deps: DLT-3.6
  <1>3 [req] The harness selects the historical ABI v0 or ABI 0.2 trace runtime from each module's marker signatures; `spec-sem2-*` uses the budget-record ABI of §3.2 and the normative header.   deps: DLT-3.2
  <1>4 [req] The harness checks and their 0.2 reading:
DLT-21.1-P2
  <1>1 [def] L1 | the dynamic marker sequence and the classical result are preserved | unchanged; for cases whose marker operands are computed from floating point or query intrinsics, the baseline is the materialised module (§17.7)   deps: DLT-12.2-P34.S1 (floating point), DLT-12.1-P1.S6 (materialised module), DLT-17.7
DLT-21.1-P3
  <1>1 [def] L2 | no pass adds a forbidden attribute | the three lists of §3.6   deps: DLT-3.6
DLT-21.1-P4
  <1>1 [def] L3 | handles stay contained (guard page, `BADHANDLE`; static review of new casts) | dynamic part unchanged; the static review is decided by §6   deps: DLT-6
DLT-21.1-P5
  <1>1 [def] L4 | region identity | every region operand resolves (§7.2) to candidates content-equal (§7.3) to the baseline's: aliases, forwarding thunks and merged twins pass (harness relaxation, bd `-cjb`)   deps: DLT-7.2-P4.S1 (forwarding thunks), DLT-7.2, DLT-7.3
DLT-21.1-P6
  <1>1 [def] L5 | native code generation keeps L1 and every marker an undefined external | unchanged   deps: DLT-21.1-P2.S1 (L1)
DLT-21.1-P7
  <1>1 [def] L6 | region-operand shapes | every shape resolves (§7.2: the candidate set is bounded by the program)   deps: DLT-7.2
DLT-21.1-P8
  <1>1 [def] **Supported envelope**: LLVM 18, 20 and 23; `default<O0>` … `default<O3>`; `default<Os>`, `default<Oz>` on 18 and 20 (LLVM 23's `opt` rejects them); `lto-pre-link<O3>`, `lto<O3>`, `thinlto-pre-link<O3>`, `thinlto<O3>`, `default<O3>` three times; clang `-O0` … `-O3` with `-flto`; rustc `-C opt-level` 0, 1, 2, 3, s, z, including its MergeFunctions in aliases mode; Julia 1.13's own pipeline; `strip`, `strip-nondebug` and `mergefunc` alone.
  <1>2 [req] **Unsupported** (LLVM bugs outside the contract on every supported major; spike findings §2): the Attributor, module and CGSCC (llvm-project #234748, #236104, and on 18 and 20 the constant-table fold fixed by PR #151359); `normalize` without `preserve-order` (#236128); `structurizecfg` without its prerequisites; the passes the harness classes `non-preserving` and the sanitizer and instrumentation passes; any other single pass not run inside an envelope pipeline (§24 D20).   deps: DLT-24-P5.S1 (D20), DLT-24
DLT-21.1-P9
  <1>1 [req] Work items the corpus owns for 0.2: rerun P4 (clang `-flto` end to end) and add a rustc `-Clinker-plugin-lto` case (bd `-j88`); minimise the three P3-only interactions (bd `-ape`); a `-g` variant of every `.c` case (bd `-u6a`); Windows funclet and non-x86-64 cases (bd `-kdj`).   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant)
````

````text
DLT-23-P2
````
