### §25.1 Blockers   [DLT-25.1]

````text
DLT-25.1-P1
  <1>1 [req] A **blocker** is a capability the dialect fully specifies whose stated case has no realisation on ISA 0.8.
  <1>2 [req] BLK-3 has been retired by §8.7.   deps: DLT-8.7
  <1>3 [req] Unlike the original outline list, BLK-5 can affect typed fault points in R-tier rungs; successful executions remain covered.   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5)
  <1>4 [req] The maintainer ruled (2026-10-29) that ISA 0.9 waits until this dialect is council-accepted.
DLT-25.1-P2
  <1>1 [def] **BLK-1** = ISA-4 | `BD-B-ISA4` | NS §3.1 handle tables; §4.1, §4.2 dynamic allocation ("real allocation"); Bennett.jl vectors of qubits; adjoints of generations whose register set is known only at run time | allocation sites or entry parameter/result roots whose handles persist across entries without a finite instance bound (§6.5); register widths with value set ⊤ (§3.4); a Co width with an unbounded value set (TDL-10) | direct `qreg` values for handles that stay out of memory; static register files with dispatch for bounded sites (§6.5) | (i) a dynamic-width type `qreg[*]`; `q = qreg.new cls n` with n an `i64` value yields `qreg[*]`; `w = qreg.width q`. (ii) Binding a `qreg[*]` to a `wire[n]` or `region[A,W]` slot checks the width at the call: CC trap, R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`, on a mismatch. (iii) `id = qreg.id q` (non-consuming; the §10 launch id) and `q = qreg.from_id id` (yields `qreg[*]`; a trap if the id is not live, is viewed or is consumed).   deps: DLT-10-P38.S1 (TDL-10), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-5.1-P4.S1 (allocation site), DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-2-P11.S2 (Binding), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-4.1, DLT-4.2, DLT-6.5, DLT-3.4, DLT-10
    <2>1 [req] (i) a dynamic-width type `qreg[*]`; `q = qreg.new cls n` with n an `i64` value yields `qreg[*]`; `w = qreg.width q`.
    <2>2 [req] (ii) Binding a `qreg[*]` to a `wire[n]` or `region[A,W]` slot checks the width at the call: CC trap, R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`, on a mismatch.   deps: DLT-2-P11.S2 (Binding), DLT-2-P3.S2 (wire), DLT-2-P10.S1 (slot), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC)
    <2>3 [req] (iii) `id = qreg.id q` (non-consuming; the §10 launch id) and `q = qreg.from_id id` (yields `qreg[*]`; a trap if the id is not live, is viewed or is consumed).   deps: DLT-10
  <1>2 [req] History frames: `qreg.id` is pure; `qreg.from_id` records the handle reference
DLT-25.1-P3
  <1>1 [def] **BLK-2** = ISA-1 | `BD-B-ISA1` | NS §4.2 "run-time budgets come from the host or from measurement results" | budget fields computed on the device (§9.5 case 3) | pre-minted finite sets; stub-minted copies of launch arguments (§9.5) | a CC instruction `tk = budget.mint @region s h st d` (`i64` operands) with the ISA §5.4 validation of `bennett_budget` as a CC trap; the token is scoped to the run; an impure history frame recording the token   deps: DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-9.5, ISA-5.4
DLT-25.1-P4
  <1>1 [def] **BLK-5** = ISA-7 | `BD-B-ISA7` | NS §4.2 named fault outcomes and R reversible prefixes | every R entry (§11.5), including mandatory budget codes 1/2/3 and explicit __bennett_nz_fault(C); direct calls under §7.4 excluded | CC hostcall reports C; generic R trap stops execution but does not satisfy typed reporting | R `fault C`: terminate with fault record code C, no later source effect, retain reversible prefix history; specify the implicit trap-cause-to-code table (div0 5, overflow 6, memory 7, trap 4, allocation failure 2)   deps: DLT-25.2-P2.S1 (ISA-7), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-11.5, DLT-7.4
DLT-25.1-P5
  <1>1 [def] **BLK-4** = new ISA-12 (audit B-38) | `BD-B-ISA12` | NS §4.2 quantum allocation inside reversible-tier code | `qalloc`/`qborrow`/`qfree` in an R closure with no finite instance bound (§7.5b); entry-state transfer from R code (§8.5) | hoisting to the CC caller with a static count (§7.5b) | permit `qreg.new`, `qreg.free`, `qreg.slice` and `qreg.concat` in R; the R history records them; the reversal of `qreg.new` is `qreg.free` with the cleanliness obligation (simulator check; hardware per ISA §17 item 14); the reversal of `qreg.free` is `qreg.new` (clean); `measure` stays CC-only   deps: DLT-8.5-P2.S14 (entry-state transfer), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-25.2-P5.S1 (`qreg.slice`), DLT-7.5-P3, DLT-8.5, ISA-17-P15
DLT-25.1-P6
  <1>1 [req] BLK-5's checked witnesses are `r-fault-code` (explicit code 2) and `r-budget-prefix` (the pre-instruction code 1 boundary).   deps: DLT-25.1-P4.S1 (BLK-5)
  <1>2 [req] Their verdict is `blocked:BD-B-ISA7` when grading against ISA 0.8.
  <1>3 [req] The exact change is now specified in the ISA 0.9 draft, commit `61550b4` (section 0.2 K5 and section 4.1 `fault C`); its presence in that draft does not change this draft's ISA target.
DLT-25.1-P7
  <1>1 [req] On ISA 0.8 a module has verdict blocked:BD-B-ISA7 if its device program contains an R-tier variant reached as an R entry: a reversible region candidate invoked outside generation, or the §19.2 whole-program R entry.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-19.2
  <1>2 [req] Every such variant has the §17.6 budget check, regardless of supplied run-time limits.   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-17.6
  <1>3 [req] A reversible call treated as a direct call under §7.4 is not an R entry and does not by itself block.   deps: DLT-7.4
  <1>4 [req] Other rejection/blocker precedence remains §1.6.   deps: DLT-1.6
  <1>5 [req] The R blocker trap is generic ISA trap with no hostcall, reached only on executions that would report a typed fault (budget exhaustion, F(C), nz_fault(C)); all other executions are unaffected.   deps: DLT-1.6-P4.S2 (blocker trap)
  <1>6 [req] This fallback does not implement typed reporting.
DLT-25.1-P8
  <1>1 [def] **Blocker report** (one line per blocker point, in processing order, in the outputs, §19.9): `<code> <function> <value or call>: <reason>; <ISA 0.8 constraint>; <BLK id> (<ISA item>) <change>`.   deps: DLT-6.5-P10.S1 (blocker point), DLT-19.9
DLT-25.1-P9
  <1>1 [req] **Blocker trap.** A blocked program is compiled (§1.6) with each blocker point lowered to the ISA 0.8 realisation that exists for the bounded cases (the static file of §6.5 with D29's size, the pre-minted budgets of the value set, the specialised pairs) and, on an execution that falls outside them, CC uses `hostcall bennett.fault` with code 4 and the blocker's code as payload, then `trap` (§11.5).   deps: DLT-24-P12.S1 (D29), DLT-2-P18.S1 (Blocker), DLT-6.5-P10.S1 (blocker point), DLT-2-P15.S1 (lowered), DLT-2-P10.S1 (value set), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-1.6, DLT-6.5, DLT-11.5
  <1>2 [req] R uses generic `trap` without hostcall, with the static blocker report naming the dependency; exact typed R reporting requires ISA 0.9 K5.
  <1>3 [req] So only the executions the blocker affects fail, and they fail loud.
````

### §A Changes from the ratified outline   [DLT-A]

````text
DLT-A-P1
  <1>1 [non] The text follows `spec/outline-0.2.md` and its rulings.
  <1>2 [non] Where it departs, the reason is below; every departure keeps or widens the capability the outline gives.
DLT-A-P2
  <1>1 [non] A1 | §8.6: k + 2 calls, cleaning each slice's fault bundle by `qxor` from a copy computed by a separate variant W_c | 2k − 1 calls: a copy-out pair per extra slice, issued as CC-level region calls (§8.6) | the outline's `qxor` cleanup is neither `reverse` nor `recompute` of the bundle's own computing sequence (ISA §6.2, §6.4), so as an action it would be ill-formed; the copy-out pair cleans its temporaries by the oracle's involution (ISA §5.2), checked by `qreg.free` (ISA §3.3)   deps: DLT-2-P7.S2 (variant), DLT-8.6, ISA-6.2, ISA-6.4, ISA-5.2, ISA-3.3
DLT-A-P3
  <1>1 [non] A2 | §8.5 snapshot release | chunked unpreparation using retained classical bytes, then clean free | D02-10: whole qreg measurement exceeds ISA 0.8 width limits   deps: DLT-8.5
DLT-A-P4
  <1>1 [non] A3 | §6.5: every root in a static register file; unbounded instance counts are BLK-1 | a handle that never reaches memory is a direct CC `qreg` value with no bound; BLK-1 only for memory-reaching sites without a reference bound (§6.5) | ISA 0.8's dynamic SSA instances already name unboundedly many registers; the blocker's case shrinks to what ISA 0.8 truly lacks   deps: DLT-25.1-P2.S1 (BLK-1), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-6.5
DLT-A-P5
  <1>1 [non] A4 | §6.5, §7.5(b): "statically bounded number of live instances", "static count" | an exact reference instance bound (self-disjoint sites, constant trip counts) (§6.5) | verdicts must not depend on a plugin's analysis precision   deps: DLT-6.5-P8.S1 (self-disjoint), DLT-6.5, DLT-7.5-P3
DLT-A-P6
  <1>1 [non] A5 | TDL-2: states Cl, Co | Cl, M (materialised, both copies valid), Co (§10) | materialising an object before a slice reads it would otherwise make it Co, and later classical reads would trap (`T-TAINT-HOST`) on classical data   deps: DLT-10-P3.S1 (TDL-2), DLT-10
DLT-A-P7
  <1>1 [non] A6 | TDL-3: slices outlined; stores in slices | slices never contain outliving stores or markers (those are events); each slice keeps a classical twin selected dynamically | the event forms of TDL-5 need the stores as events; the twin avoids slice cost when the data is classical at run time   deps: DLT-10-P14.S1 (TDL-3), DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5), DLT-10-P16.S4 (classical twin)
DLT-A-P8
  <1>1 [non] A7 | TDL-5(a), Co value: XOR v, then `xmask c`, then uncompute | one XOR of the mask c ⊕ v (c a classical argument of the slice) | equal effect, one event fewer   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5)
DLT-A-P9
  <1>1 [non] A8 | TDL-5(a)/§18: "the uncompute is valid (ISA §6.2)" | events are applied as CC-level region calls with dynamic cleanliness checks; a static ISA action only when it satisfies ISA §6 (§18.2) | ISA §6.1 makes an oracle's value depend on every cell of a region it reads, so a static action cannot uncompute a slice after writing any arena cell; at CC level the uncompute is exact because the slice does not read the written cells   deps: DLT-10-P25.S1 (TDL-5), DLT-2-P7.S1 (CC), DLT-18, ISA-6.2, ISA-6, DLT-18.2, ISA-6.1
DLT-A-P10
  <1>1 [non] A9 | TDL-8: combine into Φ; plugin register when null | per-slice fault registers retained and combined by `@firstfault`; in `compute`, copied between E_f and E_use (TDL-8) | the uncomputing call XORs the code out again, and in `compute` adjoint(E_f) would erase f's faults   deps: DLT-10-P36.S1 (TDL-8), DLT-2-P3.S1 (register), DLT-8.2-P7.S3 (fault register)
DLT-A-P11
  <1>1 [non] A10 | TDL-12 effects | inherited per-run virtual bindings and reversible state | D02-07 removes the action-effect narrowing   deps: DLT-10-P47.S1 (TDL-12)
DLT-A-P12
  <1>1 [non] A11 | TDL-7 history | clean control workspace; explicit fault/channel outcomes | D02-05 restores ratified E3; no implicit history channel   deps: DLT-10-P35.S1 (TDL-7)
DLT-A-P13
````
